| 2026-11-23 | minor | Signing hand-off package, headless: What: a new pair of headless tools hands a document and its local signing plan to someone else as one zip. `signing_handoff_export` takes `doc` and `path` (a new file ending in .zip, inside the write root) and writes, atomically and without replacing anything (an existing file at `path` is refused and left alone), one zip with exactly three entries: `document.pdf` (the PDF as it is on disk, byte for byte), `plan.json` (the plan beside it, as the plan file would hold it) and `manifest.json` (a format name, format version 1, the PDF's file name, its SHA-256 and size, the SHA-256 of `plan.json`, and the plan's state and recipients with name, email, role, order and status). The output path is checked first, so a path outside the roots is refused before the document is looked at. Refused with nothing written: a document with no file, with unsaved changes, that opening changed (a repair or a form layout), whose file on disk is no longer byte for byte the file it was opened from (the same check `signing_plan_sign` makes), or with no plan beside it; an output that does not end in .zip, exists, or is outside the write root; a PDF whose name is not a plain name, and a zip name that is only an extension (`.zip`, `dir/.zip`; the name is taken with `Path::file_name`). `signing_handoff_import` takes `path` (the zip, inside the read root) and `folder` (inside the write root, made when missing) and, before writing anything, checks that the archive holds exactly manifest.json, document.pdf and plan.json once each (a zip-slip name such as `../x`, an absolute or drive name, a folder entry, a duplicate or a fourth entry is refused), that no entry expands past its cap (manifest 64 KiB, plan 1 MiB, PDF 512 MiB, at most three entries, counted on the bytes that really come out so a small archive that expands hugely is refused), that the manifest is of this format and version 1 (another version is refused) and that its file name is one plain name ending in .pdf (no folder part, drive, `..`, leading dot, control, hidden or direction-changing character, and no Windows device name such as `CON`, `NUL` or `COM1`, with or without an extension, by the same `is_device_name` the roots use, now `pub(crate)`), all three checked right after the manifest is read and before the plan or the PDF is inflated, then that the PDF starts with %PDF- and its size and SHA-256 are the manifest's, that the plan file's SHA-256 is the manifest's (so a plan whose message, history or names were changed is refused even when it is still a valid plan), that the plan is a valid plan (the plan reader's own checks) and that the manifest's description of the plan equals the plan's. Only then are the PDF and the plan written side by side in `folder` under the names the plan expects (`<name>.pdf` and `<name>.pdf.pdfcraft-sign.json`), each published atomically without replacing a file (`bates::publish_new`, so a file another process creates in the moment before is not replaced either); if either name already exists the call is refused before anything is written, and if the second file cannot be written the first, made by this call, is removed. The result is the plan as `signing_plan_get` reads it (so `next` is the next recipient), the written paths, the hash and size, `hash_checked` true and `signatures_checked` false. Every result says `sent` false, `notified` false and `local_record_only` true and that the package is not signed or encrypted (the hashes (of the PDF and of the plan) catch damage or a change to either, not someone who changes a file and the manifest together). The package holds no secret: a plan has none and a digital ID or password is never read. Both tools are marked destructive (not read-only) and described as such. Zip reading and writing is `craft-zip`, already a workspace dependency of `pdfcraft-export` and on the licence allowlist; `pdfcraft-automation` now lists it too (one new edge in Cargo.lock, no new package). `signing_plan.rs` gained `plan_from_bytes` and `plan_bytes`, taken out of `read_plan_sidecar` and `save` with their behaviour unchanged (the sidecar's refusals still end `it was left alone`; the package's plan ends `nothing was written`, as `plan_from_bytes` now takes that ending as a parameter), and the new code is its child module `signing_plan/handoff.rs`. Parity: `sign.handoff-package` is partial (headless, local plan only); the notes of `sign.sequential-signing` no longer say there are no hand-off packages. Import does not yet validate the signatures in the PDF or check that nothing outside the recipient's fields changed, as `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 4) hopes; it says so in every result. Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 4) wants a plan plus PDF to travel as one file by mail or a shared folder, and the pieces are merged (plan, field labels, `signing_plan_sign`); this is the smallest step that moves a half-signed document and its plan together without trusting the receiver to copy two files and name them right, and it is testable with the validator as the oracle. No network, provider or server code is added. Cost: export reads the PDF from disk once and compares it with the open document's saved bytes, and holds the PDF, its deflated copy and the zip in memory (up to the 512 MiB input cap, as every tool that reads a PDF does); import reads the zip (the same cap), inflates each entry within its cap, hashes the PDF once and writes two files; an export refused early costs only the reads that found it; an import reads the whole zip, then inflates only the manifest and checks its format, version and file name before it inflates the plan and the PDF, so a package of another version or with a bad file name costs the manifest alone. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_handoff.rs`, eleven tests with made-up people at example.org and self-signed P-256 test IDs: `a_package_round_trips_the_pdf_bytes_and_the_plan_and_holds_nothing_else` (the package holds exactly the three entries, the manifest has only its documented keys, the password, the ID file name and the words key and password are in no entry but the PDF, the imported PDF and plan files are byte for byte the originals, the next recipient is Ana, `signatures_checked` and `sent` are false, `signing_plan_get` reads the same plan in both places, and the manifest carries the plan's hash), `a_signed_document_round_trips_still_validates_and_the_next_recipient_can_sign_there` (Ana signs with `signing_plan_sign`, the package is exported and imported, the imported file equals the signed file, `sign_list` reads the same statuses for it and Ana's is not invalid, the plan says Bo is next, Bo signs the imported copy, Ana's status is unchanged and only the imported copy took Bo's turn), `a_pdf_that_does_not_match_the_manifest_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written` (one byte flipped with the length kept, a wrong size in the manifest, and bytes that match their manifest but are not a PDF: each refused, the folder snapshot unchanged, no destination folder), `a_manifest_of_another_version_or_with_a_plan_that_disagrees_is_refused` (version 2 and 0, another format name, an unknown manifest key, a manifest whose plan statuses differ from the plan, and a plan that is not a valid plan whose hash the manifest carries, refused as not a signing plan with the ending `nothing was written` and not `left alone`: each refused and nothing made), `paths_that_climb_out_of_the_folder_are_refused_in_the_archive_and_in_the_manifest` (seven zip-slip, absolute, drive and folder entry names in place of the PDF in an archive of exactly three entries, so that the name list refuses each and the test asserts its wording `is not part of a hand-off package`; an archive that names `manifest.json` twice (a third entry, `manifest.jsoX`, byte-replaced to the same length) refused with `twice`; and fifteen manifest file names such as `../evil.pdf`, `sub/evil.pdf`, `C:evil.pdf`, `CON.pdf`, `nul.PDF`, `com1.pdf`, `con .pdf`, a right-to-left override, a NUL and an empty name: each refused, nothing written inside or outside the folder), `an_archive_that_is_too_big_or_has_too_many_entries_is_refused` (a 4 MiB manifest and a 4 MiB plan that deflate to a few kilobytes, an archive of five entries and a file that is not a zip: each refused with the folder unchanged and with its own reason asserted: `larger than` for the two expansions, `entries, more than 3` for the five entries, `not a ZIP archive` for the junk file, and none of them with the name-list wording), `an_existing_file_in_the_destination_is_never_overwritten_and_nothing_else_is_written` (an existing PDF, and separately an existing plan, in the destination: refused, the existing file and the whole tree unchanged; a second import into one folder refused; a file where the folder should be refused), `an_export_is_refused_without_a_saved_unchanged_document_a_plan_or_a_new_zip_name` (an output not ending in .zip, a bare `.zip`, `sub/.zip`, a path through a file, an existing output left alone, unsaved changes, a document with no file, no plan: each leaves no zip), `a_stale_copy_of_the_document_is_not_exported` (another copy signs, the first copy is refused with the on-disk sentence and nothing is written, the copy that signed exports) `a_plan_changed_inside_the_package_is_refused_even_when_it_is_still_a_valid_plan` (plan.json with a changed message, a changed history or a changed recipient name, each still a valid plan and the manifest unchanged: refused with the plan-hash sentence, the folder unchanged) and `paths_outside_the_roots_are_refused_and_nothing_is_created` (separate read and write roots and a folder outside both: exports to the outside, to the read root and through `..` are refused; imports from a zip in the write root or outside the read root, and into the read root, outside and through `..` are refused; none of the three folders changed; the allowed import works); `file_writing_tools_are_not_read_only` lists both tools; the hostile-input sweep `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` has a fixture for each (a saved document with a plan, and a package made from it), runs each once unchanged and asserts that the export wrote its zip and the import both files, and `no_tool_escapes_the_root` still passes with no allowlist entry and now reaches both tools' path arguments for real (the export checks its output path before the document). Mutations: 25 were run on the new code, each with the new tests and the hostile and file-writing tests: 24 killed and 1 survived. Killed: the hash comparison disabled, the size comparison disabled, the version check disabled, the manifest format check disabled, the plain-name check disabled, the leading-dot rule removed from it, the path separators removed from its character list, the Windows device-name refusal removed from it, the existing-destination refusal disabled, the manifest cap raised to 64 MiB, the plan cap raised to 64 MiB, the PDF header check disabled, the manifest-describes-the-plan check disabled, the plan-hash check disabled, the entry-name allowlist disabled, the entry limit raised from 3 to 300, the duplicate-name refusal disabled, the unsaved-changes refusal of export disabled, the on-disk equality check of export disabled, the existing-output refusal of export disabled, the .zip-name check of export disabled (it is now taken from the file name), the zip-name-is-only-an-extension check disabled, the export output checked against the read root instead of the write root, and the import folder checked against the read root instead of the write root (9, most of the suite); each by one or more tests (the three run again after the manifest was moved ahead of the plan and the PDF were killed again). Survived: the changed-on-open refusal of export disabled: a document that opening changed also fails the disk-equality check, so the refusal is redundant with it and exists for a clearer message; no test makes opening change a file to show its own sentence. Not mutated: the order of the reads in import (that the manifest is checked before the plan and the PDF are inflated: no test can see which read happened first), the words of the refusals and results, the description and parity text, the `Manifest` field names, the removal of the first file when the second cannot be written, the order of the checks inside import after the manifest is read, the 200-byte name limit, the `plan_summary` content beyond what the manifest comparison reads, and the check that the export's source file is inside the read root. Not tested: the removal of the PDF when the plan cannot be written (nothing makes the second write fail after the first succeeds as root); an archive with a duplicate entry name; a zip that expands to more than the 512 MiB PDF cap (the caps are tested through the manifest and plan entries only, which share the code path); a file that appears in the destination between the check and the write (`persist_noclobber` refuses it, but no test races it); an export whose source file is outside the read root; a PDF whose name has a hidden character (the manifest names are tested, the export side uses the same check); empty destination folders this call created may be left behind when a later write fails (not cleaned up, not tested); Windows and macOS (only Linux was run; the file modes of written files are those of a temporary file, owner only, as `bates::publish_new` makes them); that a person who changes a file and the manifest together is detected (it is not: the package is not signed); that the imported signatures validate or that nothing outside the recipient's fields changed (not done by import; the signed round trip checks the validator reads the same statuses); a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli` and an MCP client; no screen, no sending and no server exist to test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings` for `pdfcraft-engine`, `pdfcraft-automation` and `pdfcraft-cli`, one crate at a time (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (94 library tests passed; 799 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed), `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-cli` (all passed: 5 and 103 tests in its two targets) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed: 907 library tests, 2 of them ignored, and 9, 2 and 61 in the other targets, 2 of them ignored); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `pdfcraft-cli` and `pdfcraft-engine` (`pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), and a hand check of the tool through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-23 | minor | Sequential signing, headless: What: a new headless tool, `signing_plan_sign`, performs the next signer's turn of the local signing plan beside the document's file in one step. Arguments: `doc`, `email` (the recipient, compared with the plan's addresses without regard to case), `id` and `password` (a digital ID exactly as `sign_document` takes it: a .p12/.pfx path, `keychain:` or `windows:`), optionally `field`, and `reason`, `location`, `contact`, `appearance`, `labels` and `graphic`, which mean what they mean in `sign_document`; `certify`, `page`, `rect`, `out` and `run_form_scripts` are not offered (the schema refuses them). In order: (1) the plan beside the document's own file is read (a document with no file, with unsaved changes, or with no plan is refused; the signed file replaces the saved one, so unsaved changes are refused instead of being swallowed; and the file on disk must still be byte for byte the file the open document was read from, checked before signing and again just before the write after the plan is read again, within one session (a write by another program in the moment before the rename is not detected), otherwise the call is refused with `the file changed on disk since it was opened; reopen it`, so a stale second copy of the document cannot overwrite an earlier recipient's signature; a document that opening changed (a repair or a form layout) cannot be compared and is refused with a sentence saying to save and reopen it; the plan is also read again right before the write and the turn re-checked) and the recipient must be the plan's next in order and a signer: an address not in the plan, a viewer, someone else's turn, a finished plan and an approver are each refused with a sentence (an approver's turn is recorded with `signing_plan_advance` status `approved`; there is no `approve` mode here, by choice). The plan's own turn check is the one `signing_plan_advance` uses (`Plan::turn_of`, and `Plan::record` for writing the step, both taken out of `signing_plan_advance` with its behaviour unchanged). (2) The signature field is the one unsigned signature field whose `/PdfCraftSigner` label (`form_field_signer`) names the recipient, compared without regard to case; none, or several, is refused with a sentence that says what to do (several are listed, at most 10), unless `field` names the field to sign: a field with preSign or postSign scripts is refused with a sentence pointing at `sign_document` and `signing_plan_advance` (this tool never runs form scripts); a named field must be a signature field, with a name no other field has, that holds no signature and is not labelled for another recipient (an unlabelled one is allowed, because the caller chose it). (3) The signing is `sign_document`'s own code: the body of `sign_document` up to the signed bytes is now `Automation::sign_bytes` (returning the signed file and what the writing needs) and `sign_document` calls it and then writes as before, so nothing is copied; the document is signed as an incremental update, so the bytes of earlier signatures stay in front. (4) Before anything is written the validator reads the signed bytes (`Session::check_signing` in the engine, which opens them without touching the open document and validates them against the open document's trust): every signature the document holds must still be there and not worse (its status, changes it now forbids, changes that can still be checked), which is the check `signature_damage` makes for a signer label, now with its words chosen by the change (`signature_damage_by`; the sentences for a label are byte for byte as they were), and the new signature must be present and not invalid; for a signature that already had forbidden changes before, the new signing also must not add a kind of forbidden change it did not have (a label keeps its older, looser rule). A field locked by an earlier signature's lock (`signature_field_lock`) and a certification that allows no changes are refused here, naming the earlier signature; the refusal is `the document was not signed`, and the document, its file and the plan are untouched, so there is nothing to roll back (the signed bytes are never adopted until the check passes). (5) Then the signed file is written over the document's own file atomically, the document adopts it (like `sign_document`: the file is saved, the document shows the signed file and the undo history is dropped, because a signature cannot be undone; a call refused for not being the recipient's turn keeps the history and adds no step), and only then is the plan read again, the recipient checked to still be next, and the step written (`signed`, atomically, through the plan's own writer). If the plan cannot be used or written after the document was signed, the result is not `complete`: `status` is `partial`, `ok` false, `needs_attention` true, `signed` true, `plan_advanced` false, with the reason and a warning that says which cause it was (a write that failed: the plan still shows the recipient as next; a plan that is gone or was changed or finished by someone else: this call did not change it, look at it with `signing_plan_get`) and that the step can be recorded with `signing_plan_advance` if it is still the recipient's turn, and not to sign again. Over MCP a partial result is also marked `isError` (`tool_content` in `mcp.rs`, as for `command_batch`), and `pdfcraft-cli run` fails on it (`apps/pdfcraft-cli/src/run.rs`, as `batch` does for failed files: a single tool exits non-zero, a `--script` stops at that step, a `--report` marks the step `error`), each with the JSON body kept. If the open document cannot adopt the signed file after it was written, the plan is still updated (the file on disk is signed) and the result is `partial` with `plan_advanced` true and a warning to reopen the file. The tool is marked destructive (it replaces the document's file) and the description says that signing uses a person's credential (use only a digital ID and password the person gave you for this signing, ask before each call) and gives `password` its own description; a refusal after a crash between the save and the plan write points at `signing_plan_advance`. A `complete` result has the new signature as `sign_list` reads it, the plan as `signing_plan_get` reads it, and the signature counts before and now. Every result says `identity_checked` false (the plan does not verify who holds the digital ID: the signer certificate is not compared with the recipient's address, so whoever supplies an ID signs as the recipient; the description says so), `sent` false, `notified` false and `local_record_only` true. `sign_as_recipient` takes the plan writer as a parameter (the real one is passed by the tool) so that a test can make the write fail after the document is signed. Parity: `sign.sequential-signing` is partial (headless, local plan only, no screen, no hand-off packages). Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, items 3 and 7) needs a recipient to sign their turn and the plan to advance without trusting the caller to do both in the right order; with the pieces already merged (plan, field labels, `signature_field_lock`, the PAdES path) this is the smallest step that does it and is testable with the validator as the oracle. Cost: the file on disk is read twice (before signing and before the write) and compared with the saved bytes of the open document; the signed file is read back and its signatures validated once more before it is written (the signature validation an open or an edit does; the save checks of `check_save` are not run on it); the plan is read three times (before signing, before the write and after); an early refusal costs only the reads that found it. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_plan_sign.rs`, sixteen tests with made-up people at example.org and self-signed P-256 test IDs: `two_recipients_sign_in_order_and_the_first_signature_stays_intact` (the stored label has an upper-case letter and the call spells the address in capitals; Ana then Bo: each file keeps the earlier bytes in front, Ana's status after Bo's signature equals her status alone, Bo's is not invalid, a fresh open of the saved file reads two signatures and says Ana's covers changes_allowed, the plan lists both signed and the approver next), `the_plan_does_not_check_who_holds_the_id_and_the_result_says_so` (Ana's turn signed with Bo's ID succeeds, `identity_checked` false, `sent` false, the signer is Bo), `a_turn_out_of_order_or_not_a_signers_is_refused_and_nothing_changes` (Bo first, the approver, the viewer, an unknown address, an approver's turn after both signed, a repeat: each leaves the file, the plan and every status as they were), `a_refused_call_keeps_the_undo_history_and_a_signing_drops_it`, `no_plan_unsaved_changes_extra_arguments_and_a_document_without_a_file_are_refused` (also that the schema refuses `certify`, `out`, `page`, `rect` and `run_form_scripts`), `a_finished_plan_refuses_a_further_turn` (both signed and the approver done: a further turn is refused), `the_field_is_the_one_labelled_for_the_recipient_or_the_one_the_caller_names` (unlabelled refused, a field labelled for another refused, a text field and an unknown name refused, a named own field signed), `a_named_unlabelled_field_is_signed_and_the_labelled_one_is_left_alone`, `two_unsigned_fields_for_one_recipient_need_the_caller_to_choose`, `a_recipient_whose_only_field_already_holds_a_signature_is_refused`, `a_field_locked_by_an_earlier_signature_is_refused_by_the_validator_and_the_plan_stays_put` (Ana's lock names Bo's field: refused naming her signature, file, plan and statuses unchanged, the plan still has Bo next; a field the lock does not name signs), `a_certification_that_allows_no_changes_refuses_the_next_signer` (file, plan and every status unchanged), `a_plan_that_is_not_a_plan_or_an_id_that_does_not_open_refuses_before_any_write` (a missing ID names the file, a wrong password and a bad plan file are refused, each with the file, plan and statuses snapshot unchanged), `a_stale_copy_of_the_document_cannot_overwrite_an_earlier_signature` (two document ids on one file: Ana signs through one, Bo through the stale other: refused with the on-disk sentence, the file is Ana's signed file byte for byte, the plan unchanged; a copy opened afterwards signs), `a_field_with_signing_scripts_is_refused_and_nothing_changes` (an XFA box with signing scripts: refused, file and plan unchanged) and `a_field_named_with_a_dot_is_found_labelled_and_signed` (a field `Party.Cy`: the form's name and the validator's name agree); three unit tests in `crates/automation/src/signing_plan.rs`, `a_plan_that_cannot_be_written_after_signing_says_the_document_was_signed` (a plan writer that fails: status partial, `ok` false and `needs_attention` true, the warning says the plan still shows Ana as next, the plan writer was called once and only when the saved file already held the signature, the document is signed, the plan file is as it was, Ana is still next, and the result names the recipient), `the_plan_is_not_written_when_the_signature_is_refused` (the writer is never called for a turn refused or a field not found) and `the_plan_is_not_written_for_a_signature_the_validator_refuses` (Ana's lock names Bo's field: Bo's turn is refused and the writer was called once, for Ana only); in `crates/automation/src/mcp.rs` `a_partial_signing_is_reported_as_an_error_and_a_complete_one_is_not`; in the engine `a_signing_may_not_add_a_kind_of_forbidden_change_to_a_signature_that_already_had_one`; in the CLI crate `a_result_that_needs_attention_fails_the_run_and_an_ordinary_one_does_not` (`print_output` fails for `needs_attention` true or `ok` false with status `partial`, after printing the body, and not for an ordinary result); `file_writing_tools_are_not_read_only` lists the tool; the hostile-input panic sweep `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` has a fixture for the tool (a saved document with a labelled signature field, a plan and an ID that opens) and asserts that some call signed; `no_tool_escapes_the_root` still passes with no allowlist entry, but it is not claimed to cover this tool's `id` or `graphic` (that sweep gives the tool an unsaved blank document, so it is refused before it reaches them). Mutations: 17 were run on the new code, each with the new tests: 14 killed and 3 survived. Killed: the turn check replaced by a plain lookup (2 tests), several labelled fields resolved by taking the first (1), the validator check after signing removed (2: the lock and the certification), the check moved after the write so a refusal leaves a written file (1: the lock test, which compares the file), the plan written before the document is saved (2 unit tests and 7 integration tests), the partial status reported as `complete` (1 unit test), the approver refusal removed (1), the unsaved-changes refusal removed (1), the label compared without lower-casing (11), the on-disk equality check made always true (1: the stale-copy test), the signing-scripts refusal disabled (1), the partial result not marked `isError` over MCP (1 unit test), the CLI's needs-attention check disabled (1 CLI unit test), and the engine's forbidden-kinds comparison for a new signing disabled (1 engine unit test). Survived: the check that the new signature is not invalid, disabled (no test can make the validator read a freshly made signature as invalid); the second turn check when the step is written after signing, replaced by a plain lookup, and the turn re-check right before the write, removed (nothing can change the plan between the reads of one call, so neither can be reached by a test). Not mutated: the words of the refusals and results, the description and parity text, the checks that a named field is a signature field with a unique name, the `signed_names` term of the unsigned-field test, the 10-name cap of the several-fields sentence, the engine's `Signing` wording of `signature_damage_by`, the changed-on-open refusal, and the sentences by cause of a partial result. Not tested: a plan write that really fails on the disk (the partial status is tested through the plan writer parameter; as root a read-only folder does not stop a write, and a folder in place of the sidecar is refused when the plan is read first, before signing); the open document failing to adopt the signed file after it was written (a partial result with the plan updated, not exercised); a plan that is gone or changed between the checks and the write (the plan-gone and plan-changed warnings are not exercised); an interrupted call between the save and the plan write (only the refusal sentence that points at `signing_plan_advance` is asserted by what a signed field says, not a real crash); a file changed on disk between the second check and the rename (the window is small and not tested); the changed-on-open refusal; a name two fields share, through this tool; `graphic`, `appearance` and `labels` through this tool (they are passed to `sign_document`'s code unchanged); Keychain and Windows store IDs; an encrypted document, a dynamic form, a file name ending in `.PDF`; the roots refusing the sidecar for write through this tool (the same check as the other plan tools, not asserted here); a plan file that goes bad between the two reads of one call; a new signature the validator reads as invalid; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run); no screen and no hand-off package exist to test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings` for `pdfcraft-engine`, `pdfcraft-automation` and `pdfcraft-cli`, one crate at a time (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (94 library tests passed; 788 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed) `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-cli` (all passed: 5 and 103 tests in its two targets) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed: 907 library tests, and 9, 2 and 61 in the other targets); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `pdfcraft-cli` and `pdfcraft-engine` (`pdfcraft-sign` and `pdfcraft-organize` were not changed; `pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), and a hand check of the tool through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | patch | Sanitize signer label: What: removing private data without also removing form fields (the Remove Hidden Information panel, `doc_remove_hidden` with a `categories` list that lacks `form-fields`, and the optimizer's discard of private data, `doc_optimize` `discard`) no longer keeps the form field signer label `/PdfCraftSigner` (a recipient's email address, written by `form_field_signer`): it is removed from every field, widget kid and nested field dictionary the owner sweep reaches. Whole-document Sanitize Document already dropped the label, because removing form fields flattens the fields, drops their widgets and removes `/AcroForm`, and the full rewrite writes only reachable objects; it now also counts the label under private data. `OWNED` in `crates/redact/src/sanitize.rs` gained one entry, `(Hidden::PrivateData, SIGNER_LABEL_KEY)`; the key is repeated in `pdfcraft-redact` as `SIGNER_LABEL_KEY` (re-exported by the engine as `SANITIZED_SIGNER_LABEL_KEY`) because redact and organize are siblings and neither may depend on the other, and a test in the automation crate asserts it equals `FIELD_SIGNER_KEY`. Each dictionary that held a label adds 1 to the existing private-data count (`doc_hidden_info`, `removed` of `doc_remove_hidden`); there is no separate count. A full Sanitize counts a labelled field twice in the summed `removed` total (once under form-fields, once under private-data), because that total is the sum before minus the sum after. The sweep strips the key from any dictionary it reaches outside name maps, not only from dictionaries that are form fields, and no other category removes it (removing comments alone leaves it). The English choice text now says what the category holds: `Hidden::label` is `Private data (other applications' data and PdfCraft signer labels)` and the optimizer's checkbox `Discard private data (other applications' data and PdfCraft signer labels)` (the old English text is a translation key, so other languages show English for them until craft-i18n catches up); the `doc_hidden_info`, `doc_optimize` and `form_field_signer` descriptions, the parity notes and the redact README say it too. A signed document is refused exactly as for every sanitize and for the optimizer (both need a full rewrite), so its label stays; this is tested for `doc_remove_hidden`. Why: PR #181 added the label, but a person who removed private data without removing form fields, or who discarded it in the optimizer, still sent every recipient's address in the file. Cost: one more key in the owner sweep's existing single walk; nothing extra for a file with no label. Tested: `private_data_removes_signer_labels_from_fields_widgets_and_nested_fields` (redact: a plain field that is its own widget, a parent field with a widget kid, a nested field with its own widget kid: five labels counted and removed, fields, names and other keys kept), `a_form_without_signer_labels_is_not_touched_by_private_data` (count 0, refused as nothing to remove, field dictionary unchanged), `sanitize_leaves_no_signer_label_in_the_saved_file`, and in the automation crate `private_data_removed_with_other_categories_but_not_form_fields_leaves_no_label_in_the_file` (`doc_remove_hidden` with `private-data` and `metadata`, saved: no label bytes; fails on the code before this change), `the_optimizers_private_data_discard_leaves_no_label_in_the_copy` (`doc_optimize` with and without `discard` `private-data`; the open document is unchanged), `removing_private_data_takes_the_label_out_counts_it_and_can_be_undone` (count, another category leaves it, saved file has none, fields stay, undo restores both labels, Sanitize document removes them), `a_signed_document_is_not_sanitized_and_keeps_its_label` and `the_sanitizers_copy_of_the_key_is_the_one_the_label_is_written_under`. Mutations: 4 were run on the redact change and checked with the automation and redact tests: the entry removed (killed, by 4 automation tests including both new ones), the entry filed under comments (killed, by the same 4), filed under metadata (killed by 3 automation tests; the `private-data` plus `metadata` test passes by design because metadata is in its list), the key text changed (killed, by 2 redact tests). Not mutated: the descriptions and docs text and the English choice text. Not tested: the signed-document refusal through `doc_optimize` (the same `is_signed` check, read, not run); a label on a dictionary that is not a field (it is removed too, by design, and not asserted); the GUI dialog and the optimizer's checkbox; a file whose label sits deeper than the sweep follows (refused as for any private data); Windows and macOS (only Linux was run).
