Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root), and `canvas::tests::a_moved_picture_is_drawn_from_the_fresh_raster_while_a_tile_is_still_stale` in `pdfcraft-ui-egui` (no GPU adapter in this environment: `No adapter found`). Not run: the user interface integration tests (`pdfcraft-ui-egui` `it`, which need a GPU), tests of other crates, `cargo xtask i18n-report`, and the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | patch | Windows path display: What: error messages that name a path no longer show Windows' extended-length form. A new display helper `shown` in `crates/automation/src/paths.rs` drops a `\\?\` prefix from a drive path (`\\?\C:\x` shows as `C:\x`) and turns `\\?\UNC\server\share\x` into `\\server\share\x`; any other verbatim name and every ordinary path is left as it is. It is used for display only: the path used to open or write a file is never changed. It is applied where the failing tests showed the wart: the `is a folder, not a file` refusal of `write_atomic` (so `doc_save` and the other writers), the same refusal in `image_save`, and the `<folder>: <system error>` message when a folder cannot be made (`Written::within`, so `action_run` and the other tools that make a folder, and `write_atomic`'s own folder). Other messages that print a path with `.display()` are unchanged and may still show the prefix on Windows. Why: four automation tests failed on the maintainer's Windows PC: the three in `save_flatten_failure.rs` and `an_action_run_whose_folder_cannot_be_made_names_it` in `partial_output.rs`. The root is canonicalised (on Windows that gives `\\?\C:\...`) and the tools built their messages from that path, so the message read `\\?\C:\Users\...\taken.pdf: is a folder, not a file` where the tests expected `C:\Users\...\taken.pdf: ...`. For `partial_output.rs` the wording after the path (`Cannot create a file when that file already exists. (os error 183)`) is not what the test checks, only that the message starts with the folder path; the same prefix was the cause there. The product was at fault (a message a person reads should not show `\\?\`), not the tests, so no test was changed, weakened or skipped. Cost: one string prefix check on an error path; nothing on the success path. Tested: `paths::tests::the_extended_length_prefix_is_dropped_for_display_only_where_it_adds_nothing` checks the helper on strings, so it runs on Linux: a drive path, a UNC path, an ordinary Windows path, a Unix path, a non-verbatim UNC path, a verbatim name that is not a drive and the `Path` form. Mutations: 4 were run on the helper: UNC prefix dropped to the bare rest (killed), no drive-colon check (killed), alphanumeric instead of alphabetic drive letter (killed), `shown` not calling the string rule (survived at first: the test only called the string rule; the `Path` assertion was added and the mutation is now killed). Not mutated: the call sites of `shown`. Not tested: the Windows behaviour was NOT run here (this is Linux) and the four failing tests have not been seen to pass; it will be checked on the maintainer's PC. If a temp folder there is given as an 8.3 short name, the root's canonical form would still differ from it and the tests would still fail for that reason; this is not known. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (90 library tests passed; 767 integration tests passed and 1 failed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the four failing tests on Windows (only Linux was run), and the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | minor | Field signer, label: What: two new headless tools, `form_field_signer` and `form_signer_report`, assign a form field to a signing-plan recipient. `form_field_signer` takes `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` gives it) and optionally `signer`: leaving `signer` out only reads; an email address sets the label; an empty string or an explicit null clears it (a null is not the same as leaving the argument out). The label is a private key of the field's own dictionary, `/PdfCraftSigner`, an email address written as a text string (PdfCraft had no private key of its own before; the name is unlikely to collide with another reader's key, and a reader that does not know it ignores it); it is read from the field's own dictionary only, not inherited from a parent or taken from a widget kid. It is a label stored in the file: it does NOT restrict who can fill or sign the field, nothing is sent and nobody is notified, and other readers ignore it; every result says so (`label_only` true and a `note`), and so do both tool descriptions and the parity notes (rule 15). The address is stored in the PDF and travels with every copy of the file; the note says so. Departures from `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 2): the label lives on the field's own dictionary and not on the widget kid, and the assignment lives in the PDF while the plan lives in its sidecar, so there are two places to keep in step by hand (`form_signer_report` lists where they differ); both are said in the tool description. The address is checked by the signing plan's own checker (`check_email` in `signing_plan.rs`, now `pub(crate)` and not copied): one @, no spaces, a dot in the domain, at most 254 bytes and a local part of at most 64 bytes. The plan is looked up beside the document's own file (`<file name>.pdfcraft-sign.json`, from the document's path, which goes through `resolve_for` and the read root; the file itself may be gone, and after a Save As the plan beside the new file is the one looked at). With a plan beside the file, a set must name a recipient of it whose role is signer or approver: an address that is not in the plan and a viewer are refused with a sentence that says so, and the label is written with the plan's own spelling of the address (addresses are compared without regard to case). With no plan, or a document with no path, or a path not ending in .pdf, a well-formed address is accepted and the result says plainly that it was not checked against a plan (`plan.state` is `none` or `not_checked`). A sidecar that is not a valid plan, or one the roots refuse, refuses a set (nothing is written, the file is left alone), and a read, and `form_signer_report`, still answer with `plan.state` `error` and the plan parts empty. A set or clear is one undo step (`Edit::SetFieldSigner`, label "Set field signer", checked by the same permission rule as other field property edits: it needs permission to modify the document; the `settable` flag in a result does not look at permissions, and the description says so), a refused call writes nothing and adds no undo step, and setting the label a field already has or clearing a field with none changes nothing (`changed` false, no undo step). A signature field that holds a signature (`/V` not null, `/FT` found on the field or a parent) is refused for a write and still read. Labelling ANY field, and clearing a label, is a change to the form after the document was signed, so it can break a signature already in the file: a certification that allows no changes (`P` 1) treats it as a change it forbids, and a signature whose `/Lock` names the field, or all fields, locks it. This is decided by the validator and not by a copy of its rules (`pdfcraft-organize` cannot use `pdfcraft-sign`, same layer): in `Session::apply` (`crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`), once the edit is in and the document is validated again as it already is after every edit, `signature_damage` compares every signed signature's verdict before and after; if any one's status fell, it now has changes it forbids, or its changes could no longer be checked, the edit is rolled back (the redo steps are kept as they were, and so is the oldest undo step that the push made room for when the history was full) and refused with a sentence that names the signature field and the cause: a certification that allows no changes (any change to the form, a label on any field included), a lock that covers this field, a status that fell, or changes that could no longer be checked; a signature that allows form filling (an approval signature, or a certification at level 2 or 3) does not stop the label, and a signature that already had changes it forbids before the edit passes, because the label does not make its verdict worse. The check also applies to a label edit inside a batch (`contains_field_signer`, recursive). It does not cover signatures made after the label is written: sign_list after labelling a document that will be signed. A field name that two fields share is refused, for a read too, as `signature_field_lock` does. A label read from a file is echoed with control, zero-width and direction-changing characters taken out (the check of `signing_plan.rs`, `is_hidden`, now `pub(crate)`), `signer_cleaned` says whether any were, and `well_formed` says whether the stored text is a well-formed address as it stands (an address with spaces round it is not called well formed, since the plan comparisons do not trim); an empty string (or white space only) is no label in both tools (`signer` null, `empty_label` true; the report counts the field as unassigned; a clear takes the key out). `form_signer_report` lists every field with its label (`assigned`, each with its kind, `signature_field`, the signer and the plan recipient it matches), the fields with none (`unassigned`, with `unassigned_signature_fields` counting the signature fields among them), labels that are not a string (`unreadable`), labels naming an address that is not a recipient of the plan (`signers_not_in_plan`, with `signers_not_in_plan_count`) and plan recipients with no field (`plan_recipients_without_fields`, with its count, each with whether the recipient has a turn); each of the five lists has at most 500 rows (the counts cover every field; `truncated` says any list was cut), and a name two fields share is marked. The label is in `pdfcraft_organize::field_signer` (read, change check and set, with a read allowance of 1000 reads), the edit in the engine, the tools in `crates/automation/src/field_signer.rs`. No Field Properties combo and no recipient-limited signing view are built; the parity entry `sign.field-assignment` is partial (headless only). Follow-up, not done here: the Sanitize document code (`crates/redact/src/sanitize.rs`, around its `OWNED` list) does not strip `/PdfCraftSigner`, so a sanitized file keeps the address; the sanitizer was not edited in this slice, and an issue should be opened for it. Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 2) needs a field to say whose it is before a signing view can show a recipient only their fields; this is that label and its report, small and testable on its own, with no service, account or network. Cost: one small read of the field's dictionary per field in a report (at most 1000 reads each); a set is one dictionary write but, like every edit, is followed by a refresh of the whole document (the edit's scope is the full one), including validating every signature again; a set refused for a signature costs a second full re-inspection for the roll back; the undo step that rolls back is a copy of the working document kept only until the edit is accepted; a text string of at most 254 bytes in the file; no network, no setting, no asset, no dependency and no translated text (the tool results are English; the undo label is English). Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/field_signer.rs`, nineteen tests, and seven unit tests in `crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`: `a_label_is_set_read_saved_reopened_undone_redone_and_cleared`, `a_call_that_changes_nothing_adds_no_undo_step`, `the_label_survives_an_incremental_save_over_a_signed_file` (a signed signature field is read but refused for a write; an unsigned one in the same file is labelled and saved incrementally with the signed bytes in front, and the audit report does not say the changes are forbidden), `a_label_that_the_signature_forbids_is_refused_and_leaves_it_whole` (a certification with P=1 made by `sign_document`: the label is refused with the validator's sentence, no undo step, the file and the audit report's `covers` whole_file, status and empty `changes_after_signing` are as they were), `a_label_on_a_field_a_signatures_lock_names_is_refused_and_one_it_does_not_name_is_not` (a signed Sig2 whose `/Lock` includes `name`: refused; `city` is written, the status is as before and the audit report says the changes are allowed; a lock of all fields refuses any field; a refused label leaves an undone step redoable), `a_label_is_written_where_the_signature_allows_form_filling` (an approval signature and a P=2 certification: written, status not worse than before, `covers` changes_allowed, saved incrementally, and a clear is allowed and one undo step), `with_a_plan_only_a_signer_or_an_approver_of_it_can_be_named`, `without_a_plan_a_well_formed_address_is_accepted_and_says_it_is_unchecked`, `a_plan_that_cannot_be_read_refuses_a_set_and_still_allows_a_read` (now also the report: `plan.state` error and the plan parts empty), `an_unknown_or_shared_field_name_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written`, `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads_and_reports`, `a_read_only_field_and_a_field_a_lock_names_can_be_labelled_when_the_document_allows`, `the_report_lists_fields_signers_unassigned_signature_fields_and_the_plans_gaps`, `a_label_that_is_not_a_string_is_reported_as_unreadable_and_can_be_replaced`, `an_empty_label_is_no_label_in_both_tools_and_a_clear_takes_it_out`, `hidden_characters_in_a_stored_label_are_not_echoed_and_the_label_is_flagged` (an override and a zero-width character in a stored label, in both tools, with `well_formed`) and `the_labels_not_in_the_plan_are_capped_and_the_report_says_it_was_cut` (505 labels not in a plan: count 505, 500 rows), `a_refused_label_keeps_the_oldest_undo_step_of_a_full_history` (101 steps on a signed document with a lock, then a refused label: 100 undo steps are still there and the oldest one kept is unchanged) and `the_refusal_names_its_cause` (the certification and the lock sentences); `hidden_characters_in_a_stored_label_are_not_echoed_and_the_label_is_flagged` also checks that an address with spaces round it is not well formed. The unit tests of `signature_damage` use hand-built signatures: forbidden changes with the status already invalid, a status that falls, changes that could no longer be checked with the status staying unknown, the sentence for each cause (a certification, a lock, other), a signature that already forbade the changes passing, an unmatched signature, and a batch holding a label edit counting as one. The tests use fictional people only. The hostile-input panic sweep (`no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments`) and the damaged-file sweep now cover both tools, and its fixture gives them a saved document with a field `name` and a plan beside it so that hostile values reach the `signer` argument and the plan check; `no_tool_escapes_the_root` is not claimed to cover them (they take no path argument: the document's path and the plan beside it go through `resolve_for` and `check_roots`), and `tools_do_not_touch_the_file_system_without_resolving_the_path` passes with no allowlist entry. Mutations: 25 distinct ones were run over two rounds, 22 killed and 3 survived on the final code. Killed: the viewer refusal removed; the address comparison made case-sensitive; an empty or white-space string treated as a read; the same-value no-change check removed; the signed-field refusal removed; the `signers_not_in_plan` condition inverted; the unreadable-plan refusal on a set replaced by treating it as no plan; the whole signature-damage check turned off; the redo steps not restored after a refusal; echoed labels not cleaned; `well_formed` always true; the report failing on an unreadable plan; the not-in-plan list uncapped; an empty string treated as unreadable; and, in the second round on the final code (eight runs, eight killed): the forbidden-changes term removed, the status-fell term removed and the changes-no-longer-checked term removed (these three survived the first round and are now killed by the unit tests, so they are not redundant), the dropped undo step not put back, a batch not counted as a label edit, the certification sentence off, the lock sentence off, and `well_formed` ignoring spaces. Survived: the check that the object is a field with a `/T` removed from the engine's change check, in two forms (the `settable` refusal removed, and `settable` always true), because every field the tools offer has a name and no test passes the engine another kind of object; and the `signers_not_in_plan` term removed from `truncated` (it cannot be the only cut list: more than 500 rows there means more than 500 assigned fields, which are cut as well). Not mutated: the 500-row cap of the other lists, the 254-character read cut and `exact`, the 1000-read allowance, the `/Parent` walk that finds a field's type, the root checks, and the plan-state wording. Not tested: a signature whose changes the validator cannot compare, through a real file (only the hand-built unit test covers that term); an unmatched signature through a real file (only the unit test); a non-field object given to `Edit::SetFieldSigner` through the engine (not reachable through the tools); a label on a field whose type is inherited from a parent and which holds a signature; a field with widget kids; more than 500 fields in the other lists; a label longer than 254 characters in a file; an exhausted read allowance; the roots refusing the sidecar for a document outside the read root; Save As to another folder than the plan's; an undo or redo through the user interface (no UI is built); the label read by another PDF reader; the sanitizer follow-up; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run); and that the label restricts nobody, which is true because no code reads it for that and is not asserted by a test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0 each); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (89 library tests passed, 767 integration tests passed and 1 failed, 2 in another target passed); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed, 906 engine library tests among them); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of the other crates (`pdfcraft-sign` was not changed and its tests were not run; `pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (not run), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), the mutations listed under Not mutated, and a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | minor | Signing plan, local record: What: four new headless tools keep a local signing plan beside a PDF: `signing_plan_set`, `signing_plan_get`, `signing_plan_advance` and `signing_plan_clear`. A plan is recipients (name, email, role `signer`, `approver` or `viewer`, order number), a message and each recipient's status with its history (`pending`, `sent`, `signed`, `approved`, `declined`, each with a time in seconds from the host's clock, 0 when the host has none; a step recorded by a host whose clock reads earlier than the last event takes that last time, so a history never runs backwards), kept as plain serde data in a sidecar file named from the PDF's whole file name, `<file name>.pdfcraft-sign.json` (`a.pdf` has `a.pdf.pdfcraft-sign.json`, so `a.pdf` and `a.PDF` never share a plan); the PDF's bytes are never touched. The plan assumes the sidecar: keeping it inside the PDF as an attachment is not built. It is a local record only: nothing is sent anywhere, nobody is notified, no signature is collected, and `signing_plan_advance` writes down what the caller says happened without looking for a signature in the PDF; every result says so (`local_record_only` true and a `note`), and so do the tool descriptions and the parity notes (rule 15). `sent` is in the file format for a later sending route and no tool sets it. Arguments: `path` (the PDF, which must exist and end in .pdf, read through the roots like every path); `signing_plan_set` takes `recipients` (an array of `{name, email, role?, order?}`), `message?` and `replace?`; `signing_plan_advance` takes `email` and `status`: a signer's step is `signed`, an approver's is `approved`, and either may be `declined`; the other combination is refused. Limits and rules, all checked before any write: at most 50 recipients, a name of 1 to 200 characters, an email of at most 254 bytes with a local part of at most 64 bytes (octets, as the SMTP limits are; a refusal for length says so in its own sentence naming both limits), a message of at most 2000 characters; names and emails refuse every control character, the line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029), and the characters that hide, reorder or disguise text, which is the list of `hides_text` in `crates/engine/src/links.rs` (copied, since that function is private to the engine crate) without its other whitespace: U+200B to U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2060 to U+2064, U+2066 to U+2069, U+FEFF, U+061C, soft hyphen, U+034F, the Hangul fillers U+115F, U+1160, U+3164 and U+FFA0, U+180E and the tag characters U+E0000 to U+E007F; the message refuses the same except that it may hold line feeds, tabs and carriage returns; the email check is shallow on purpose (one @, something either side, no spaces, a dot inside the domain; it is not checked that the domain exists or that the address can receive mail); a duplicate address is refused, compared without regard to case; at least one recipient must be a signer or approver; the order is a strict list, 1, 2, 3 ... with none missing or repeated, given to every recipient or to none (none means the list order), one recipient at a time, a viewer has no turn, and parallel groups are not supported (not implemented, so not offered). `signing_plan_advance` refuses a recipient whose turn it is not, a viewer, an address not in the plan, a status that does not fit the role, a missing plan and a finished plan (everyone who signs or approves has done so, or someone declined); `signing_plan_set` over a plan in which any recipient has a status other than pending (signed, approved, declined or sent) needs `replace: true`, and its result says whether a plan was replaced (`replaced`) and whether that plan had history that is now gone (`discarded_history`); the tool is not marked destructive, because that flag is for the whole tool and most calls only make a plan. A sidecar that cannot be read as a valid plan (not JSON, wrong shape or version, unknown fields, repeated addresses, order numbers off, a name or address that is not valid or not already trimmed, a history that does not start pending, run forward in time and end at the recipient's status, a turn taken out of order or in the wrong role, a message that breaks the rules, over 1 MiB, or a link or folder instead of a file) is refused by all four tools with "left alone" and is never overwritten or deleted. A sidecar that is a link is not followed: the name is looked at without following it, the file is opened once, what was opened must be a plain file within the size limit and (on unix) the same file the name showed, and the read stops one byte past the limit; `O_NOFOLLOW` is not used because it needs `libc` or `rustix` and `rustix` is only a macOS test dependency of this crate, so a swap between the look and the open is caught by the same-file comparison on unix and not on other systems. A refused call writes nothing; the one write is an atomic file write (`write_atomic`). The sidecar is derived from the resolved PDF path and checked against the read root and, for a write, the write root as well (it does not itself pass through `resolve`; a root refusal names the sidecar), so with separate roots the plan works only where the PDF's own folder is in both (a refusal, not a silent success). Where the code lives: `crates/automation/src/signing_plan.rs`, a module of the shared tool layer, because the model is small, plain data with its validation and nothing below the tool layer needs it yet; no new crate, and when the app needs the same plan it moves into a crate under `crates/` in its own PR. `serde` (already a workspace dependency, derive enabled) is added to `crates/automation/Cargo.toml`, and `Cargo.lock` changes by that one line; nothing else in the manifests. `crates/automation/tests/it/main.rs` gained `mod signing_plan`, and `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` a fixture arm for the four tools, which puts them in the hostile-input sweep with a real PDF and a real plan so hostile values reach their validators; no other existing test code changed (the lint test in `file_access.rs` that wants a `resolve` before a file-system call is satisfied by `resolve_plan_paths` and by the parameter name `resolved_sidecar`, with no allowlist change: the sidecar is derived from the resolved PDF path and root-checked, so the call itself satisfies the scan, which is why the helper was renamed instead of being added to `RESOLVED_BY_CALLER`). The design is the first item of "Local design" in `docs/esign-design.md`; its attachment storage, field assignment, sign-as-next-recipient, hand-off package, audit page and screen are not built, and the feature is called a signing plan, not after any product. Why: asking other people to sign a document, and tracking who has, needs a record of who, in what order, with what status, before any route that sends it; this is that record and its order rules, small and testable on its own, with no service, account or network. The parity entry `sign.request-plan` is partial (headless only, no screen yet). Cost: one small file read (at most 1 MiB, normally under 20 KB) and one atomic write per call, parsing at most 50 recipients; no rendering, no network, no setting, no asset and no translated text (the tool results are English). Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_plan.rs`, fourteen tests: `a_plan_round_trips_through_the_sidecar_and_leaves_the_pdf_alone` (a set then a get give the same plan with the recipients in order, the message kept, everyone pending with one history entry, the state in progress with the first recipient next, the result saying it is a local record that does not send or collect signatures, the PDF's bytes unchanged, clear removes the sidecar and not the PDF, clearing nothing is not an error), `listed_order_numbers_decide_the_turns`, `turns_are_taken_in_order_and_the_plan_finishes` (out of turn, a viewer, an unknown address and a status other than signed, approved or declined are refused with the folder byte for byte unchanged; a signer cannot approve and an approver cannot sign; the address is matched without regard to case; the turn passes over the viewer; a recipient who signed cannot sign again; the history grows by one event that is not earlier than the first; the plan completes with no next recipient and then refuses a further step with nothing changed), `a_declined_plan_is_finished_and_needs_replace_to_be_remade`, `an_unprogressed_plan_is_replaced_without_replace`, `a_refused_set_writes_nothing_new_and_keeps_the_old_plan` (35 bad recipient lists including hidden characters (U+061C, soft hyphen, U+2028, U+2029, U+2060, U+2066, U+FEFF, U+034F, U+115F, U+180E, U+3164, a tag character, a zero-width space in an address, a line feed and a right-to-left override; not every character of the list) and the byte limits, eight bad messages and four bad paths, each refused with the folder unchanged, first with no plan and then with a plan in place; the duplicate address is named as such; the limits of 50 recipients, 200 characters and 2000 characters are accepted, and a message with line feeds, tabs and carriage returns is accepted), `a_sidecar_that_is_not_a_plan_is_refused_and_never_overwritten_or_deleted` (eighteen files, each a valid plan with one thing changed, or garbage: another version with a valid recipient, an unknown field, an order number off, an address twice, signed out of turn, approved in a signer's role, a name or address not trimmed, a hidden character in a name, a bad message, a history not starting pending, running backwards in time or ending at another status, and a valid plan padded with spaces past 1 MiB; each refused by all four tools with the file unchanged and with the sentence of that rule; the unaltered plan is then read; the order-numbers and repeated-address refusals have different sentences, as have signed out of turn and approved in the wrong role, and a history running backwards has its own), `a_sidecar_that_is_a_link_to_a_valid_plan_is_not_followed` (unix only: the target is a valid plan, all four tools refuse with "not a plain file", the target's bytes are unchanged and the link is still there), `a_plan_with_a_recipient_marked_sent_is_not_overwritten_without_replace`, `a_first_plan_replaces_nothing_and_discards_nothing`, `a_clock_set_back_does_not_make_the_history_run_backwards` (a plan made at a real time and then advanced by a host whose clock reads 0: the new event is not earlier than the first and the file is still read back), `the_limits_count_characters_for_names_and_messages_and_bytes_for_addresses` (200 two-byte characters in a name and 2000 three-byte characters in a message are accepted and 201 and 2001 refused; an address of exactly 254 bytes and a local part of exactly 64 bytes are accepted and one more refused with the sentence naming the limits), `files_that_differ_only_in_the_case_of_the_extension_keep_separate_plans` (returns early, saying so, in a case-insensitive folder) and `the_plan_stays_inside_the_roots` (with a read root and a write root that differ the write is refused and nothing is made; the refusal names the sidecar; a PDF outside both is refused; with one folder for both it works). The two hostile-input tests (`no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments`, `no_tool_escapes_the_root`) now include the four tools: the escape test loops over every tool's path properties, so it covers `path` of these tools, but for the tools that also need `recipients` or `email` the placeholder values may be what refuses the call, so only the get and clear refusals are certain to come from the path. Mutations: on the final code, twelve were run: the turn-order check removed from `signing_plan_advance` (killed by `turns_are_taken_in_order_and_the_plan_finishes`); the validation of a sidecar when read removed (killed by the not-a-plan test); the `replace` check removed (killed by the declined-plan test); the duplicate-address check in `signing_plan_set` removed (killed by `a_refused_set_writes_nothing_new_and_keeps_the_old_plan`, after its first version survived because the plan's own validation also refuses a duplicate with another sentence, so the test asserts the sentence); the link refusal removed, both the look at the name and the same-file comparison together (killed by the link test); the size limit removed, both the check of the file's size and the check of the bytes read together (killed by the padded-plan case); the version check removed (killed by the another-version case, whose file holds a valid recipient); `started` not counting `sent` (killed by the marked-sent test); the time of an advance taken as the clock reads, without the last event as a floor (killed by the clock test); `replaced` and `discarded_history` always false (killed by the declined-plan and unprogressed-plan tests); and the address length and the local-part length counted in characters instead of bytes (each killed by the byte-limit test and the bad-lists test). Survived: none of the mutations run survives. Not mutated: the link look alone and the same-file comparison alone, and the size check alone and the read limit alone (each pair covers the other, so one removed alone is not seen by any test, by design), the email shape rules other than their use in the tests above, the hidden-character list entry by entry, the 50, 200 and 2000 limits, the history-length limit, the root checks, the role-and-status match in `signing_plan_advance`, the trimmed-name check, the history-order and history-start checks read back, and the validity rules that read a sidecar back other than as one group. Not tested: parallel signing groups (not implemented); a recipient's `sent` status other than in a hand-made file (no tool sets it); a sidecar that is a folder or a FIFO (a named pipe would be opened and could block; the look at the name sees it is not a plain file first, but no test makes one); an unreadable sidecar (permissions) or a full disk; a link swapped in between the look and the open (the same-file comparison is not exercised by a race); two callers writing the same plan at once (the last write wins, the write itself is atomic), and a file swapped in between the checks and the delete in `signing_plan_clear` (the delete is of the name, not of the opened file); a PDF path through a link; the time 0 of a host without a clock; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run, and the link test is unix only); the claim that an email address belongs to a person (not checked); and that nothing is sent, which is true because no code here opens a connection and is not asserted by a test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (89 library tests passed, 748 integration tests passed and 1 failed, 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of the other crates (only `pdfcraft-automation` and its manifest and the lock changed; the `cargo xtask` checks above built what they need), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (not run), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), the mutations listed under Not mutated, and a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | minor | Signature audit report: What: `signature_audit_report` (new, headless) summarises the signatures of the open document as PdfCraft's validator read them (the same validation `sign_list` gives; nothing is validated again). Argument: `doc`, and optionally `output`, a path inside the write root (an existing file there is replaced). It returns the document's name, page count, size and the SHA-256 of the document as PdfCraft holds it now (`unsaved_changes` says there are changes not saved, and `changed_on_open` that opening the file made PdfCraft add changes of its own, such as a dynamic XFA form's layout or data sync or a repaired page tree: the signatures were checked against those bytes, so changes after signing may include PdfCraft's own, the fingerprint may differ from the file on disk, and saving does not clear the flag; `document_cut` says the name was cut at 240 characters, and `stand_in_counted` that the counts include a stand-in row for fields a too-large form kept unread), the counts of signatures (`found`, `signed`, `valid`, `unknown`, `invalid`), the number of certificates on the trust list, and for each signature field, document timestamp, empty signature field or stand-in row for fields a too-large form kept the validator from reading (`kind`: `signature`, `document_timestamp`, `unsigned_field`, `unread_fields`): the field name, the status (`valid`, `unknown`, `invalid` or `unsigned`) with the validator's own explanatory sentences (`reasons`; `reasons_cut` when more than 12 were left out), the signer and the certificate's subject and issuer, whether the certificate is self-signed, the signing time written in the file (`/M`) and the signing time used for checks (a signed attribute, else `/M`; both claimed by the signer), any timestamp (for a signature its time is called validated when the token's authority chains to the trusted list; for a document timestamp only when the status is `valid`), the hash algorithm, the signature format, the revision covered, whether the signature covers the whole file (`covers_whole_file`: true, false, or null when not determined) or changes follow it (`covers`: `whole_file`, `changes_allowed`, `changes_disallowed`, `not_determined` when validation stopped before the changes were classified or could not compare the fields the signature locks, or `not_applicable`; `changes_after_signing` names them, from the existing change classification), the certification level (1 to 3) of a certification signature, the page, and the reason and location the signer gave. `status_meaning` says that unknown is not shown invalid but not shown valid either (an untrusted signer, or something that could not be checked, including signatures PdfCraft cannot check yet), and invalid includes unreadable signature data and a missing or revoked signer certificate. `checked` and `not_checked` list what was and was not done: revocation is read only for a signature's signer certificate, only from information stored in the file's /DSS and when the signing time is known, nothing online is contacted, revocation of intermediate certificates and of timestamp authorities' certificates is not checked, revocation data archived inside the signature (adbe-revocationInfoArchival) is not read, who the signer really is in the world is not checked, and nothing says whether a signature has legal effect. The text is English (`language`). With `output` the same facts are also written as a new plain-text PDF titled `Signature audit report` (made with the existing text-to-PDF code, `Session::create_report`) that says it is a summary generated locally from the signatures in the file and is not itself signed. A document with no signed signature gives an empty report on a read (`signed` 0) and is refused for `output`, with nothing written; a document whose structure could not be read is refused, since no signature was looked for; an `output` outside the write root, or the open document's own file, is refused before anything is written. The report changes nothing in the document and signs nothing. At most 50 entries are listed (`truncated` says there were more; the counts cover all of them), text taken from the file is made one line, stripped of characters that hide, reorder or disguise text and cut at 240 characters (`text_cut`), and at most 12 of a signature's sentences are repeated. The code is `crates/engine/src/signature_audit.rs` (the report and its text) and `Session::signature_audit` and `signature_audit_pdf`, and the tool in `crates/automation/src/signing.rs`; the signature crate gained `SignatureInfo::changes_checked` (whether the change check ran and could be finished) and the document `Document::changed_on_open`; the parity entry `sign.audit-report` is partial. Why: PdfCraft signs and validates digitally, but a person who has to hand a signed file to someone else had no one-page record of who signed it, when, with what, whether the file changed afterwards and what was not checked. This is the first, local slice of that: a report read from the file, with no service, account or network. The feature is called a signature audit report and not a certificate of completion: `certificate` already means an X.509 certificate here, and a certificate of completion reads as an assurance this report does not give (the name also avoids another company's product wording, AGENTS.md rule 22). It follows rule 15: it says what the validator did and did not check, that the report is not signed, and says nothing about legal effect. There is no screen yet (rule 14 is met by the tool, the screen comes later), so the entry is partial. Cost: One pass of SHA-256 over the document's bytes, one comparison of the bytes at open with the input (once per open), and a copy of at most 50 entries holding about 21 texts each (nine of at most 240 characters and at most 12 sentences), so at most about 250,000 characters, about 1 MB as UTF-8; in the report PDF a character the font cannot draw is written as a code point of up to 8 characters, so the worst case is about 2 million characters and a PDF of several hundred pages. Reading the signatures is free: they were read when the document was opened under the validator's own limits (20,000 fields, 100,000 entries) and not read again. No dependency, asset, setting or translated text (the report is in English, and `language` says so); the tool's description and schema are new; the output is one atomic file write. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_audit_report.rs`, nine tests on files signed with the signing tools (`sign_id_create`, `sign_document`): `a_signed_file_is_reported_with_the_signer_hash_time_coverage_and_the_files_fingerprint` (one signature: the SHA-256 and size equal the file's, `changed_on_open` false, signer, subject, self-signed, SHA-256 hash algorithm, status unknown and no timestamp, the revision, the claimed and read time, whole-file coverage, no certification level, reason, location and page, the validator's sentences passed on, `not_checked` saying no online revocation check and not signed, then trusting the ID makes the status valid exactly as `sign_list` says and the trusted count 1), `the_first_of_two_signatures_permits_the_second_and_only_the_last_covers_the_whole_file` (two signers, the first covered with changes allowed and the second whole-file, revisions in order), `changes_a_certification_forbids_are_reported_as_changes_after_signing` (a no-changes certification covers the whole file, a comment added afterwards turns it into changes disallowed and invalid with the changes named and the same revision, `unsaved_changes` true, the fingerprint no longer the file's, and the written PDF says the changes are not saved and are not permitted), `an_unsigned_document_gives_an_empty_report_and_no_report_file` (an empty report with the page count and the file's SHA-256, `output` refused with nothing written, an empty signature field listed as unsigned with no revision and `not_applicable` coverage), `the_report_file_is_a_pdf_that_says_what_it_is_and_what_it_did_not_check` (the written PDF opens, has no signature of its own, and its text holds the title, the sentence that it is a summary generated locally and not itself signed, the SHA-256, the signer, the page count, the coverage sentence, the no-online-revocation, intermediate-certificate and no-legal-effect statements, the meaning of unknown and the trust sentence, and does not say certificate of; an `output` outside the write root and over the document's own file are refused, the signed file untouched), `hostile_signature_data_is_cut_cleaned_counted_and_never_a_panic` (a signer name with a line break and 300 characters is one line of at most 240, `text_cut` is true and `reasons_cut` false and the PDF says so; a signer name and a file name holding bidirectional controls, zero-width characters, a byte order mark and a line separator come out without them; 60 signature fields are counted, 50 listed and `truncated` true), `a_signature_with_no_usable_byte_range_is_invalid_with_reasons_and_no_coverage_claim` (a signature value with a byte range of [0 0 0 0], which is refused before its /Contents is read, is invalid with sentences, its reason is one clean line, and its coverage is `not_determined`, its revision and `covers_whole_file` null), `a_document_timestamp_that_does_not_validate_is_not_called_validated` (a document timestamp with an empty byte range is listed as a document timestamp, is not valid, has coverage not determined, and the PDF neither says `Timestamp: validated` nor gives it a certification level or calls it an approval signature) and `the_report_file_goes_to_the_write_root_and_never_to_the_read_root` (separate read and write roots: the report is written inside the write root and refused inside the read root, with nothing written there). The engine's unit tests (`crates/engine/src/signature_audit.rs`) hold five hand-built cases: `a_document_timestamp_time_is_validated_only_when_the_status_is_valid_but_a_signatures_when_its_authority_is_trusted`, `a_report_of_a_document_without_its_structure_is_refused`, `changes_the_signature_cannot_be_compared_with_are_not_reported_as_permitted`, `only_the_listings_own_stand_in_row_is_called_unread_fields` (a real field named UnreadFields stays a signature) and `a_document_name_cut_at_the_limit_says_so`. Mutations: the first 20 were run on the code before the last two revisions and are not repeated here (the lines they touch changed); on the final code, 11 were run for what the first revision added or changed (coverage taken from `modification` without `changes_checked`, the revision always shown, the certification line for every kind, hidden and reordering characters kept, the document name not cleaned, the intermediate-certificate statement removed, `output` resolved as a read path, `covers_whole_file` true when not determined, `text_cut` never set, `Timestamp: validated` printed whatever the status, and the read-only refusal removed): 9 failed a test and 2 survived, and the two survivors are killed since by engine unit tests; 3 more were run on the final code: the document-timestamp rule `Timestamp: validated` whatever the status (killed), the read-only refusal removed (killed) and the stand-in row recognised without the listing's own sentence (killed). Survived: none of the mutations run on the final code survives. Not mutated: `changed_on_open` and its comparison at open, the setting of `changes_checked` false when locks cannot be compared (the engine test sets the flag by hand; no fixture reaches it), the 12-sentence limit and `reasons_cut`, the 240 and 12 limits themselves, `stand_in_counted`, `document_cut`, `self_signed`, the certificate issuer, the signature format and `algorithm` fields, the wording of the certification level, and the added hidden characters of `hides_text` (U+061C, soft hyphen, fillers, Mongolian vowel separator, tags). Not tested: a document that opening changes (a dynamic XFA form): `changed_on_open` is not asserted true anywhere, and neither is its sentence in the report; a form with too many fields (the real stand-in row is built by hand only); a signature whose locked fields cannot be compared (no fixture); a signature with more than 12 sentences; a signature with a validated timestamp or a document timestamp that validates (the time rule is tested on hand-built entries only); a signature with revocation information stored in the file; a certification of level 2 or 3; a file signed by another product; an encrypted document; more than 50 signed signatures (the limit is tested with empty fields); a signer name in a script the report font cannot draw (the text-to-PDF code writes such characters as code points and says so; not asserted here); a report long enough to need several pages; the report in a viewer other than PdfCraft; and the report on Windows or macOS. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-sign -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of pdfcraft-sign (135 library tests passed (1 ignored), 157 integration tests passed), pdfcraft-engine (899 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored)) and pdfcraft-automation (89 library tests passed, 734 integration tests passed and 1 failed, 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not run; the ui-egui crate was not changed in this revision), the organize and create crates' tests (not touched), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (not run), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft opening the report file, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-22 | minor | Split name template: What: `doc_split` takes an optional `name_template` that names each part (without `.pdf`) from the placeholders `{name}` (the document's file name without its extension, as `file_stem` gives it), `{bookmark}` (the top-level bookmark on the part's first page, empty when there is none; it works with `every`, `before` and `max_mb` as well as `bookmarks: true`), `{n}` (part number from 1), `{first}` and `{last}` (its page numbers from 1), with `{{` and `}}` for literal braces; an unknown placeholder, a stray brace or a template over 1000 bytes is refused before anything is written, and an expansion is cut at 4096 bytes so a huge bookmark title costs nothing. The expanded name is made a safe file name (`crates/automation/src/split_name.rs`): characters other than letters, digits, space and `-_.,()` become `_` (every path separator, `:` and control character); every `..` becomes `_`, repeated until none is left, so `...` becomes `_.`; dots and spaces at either end are dropped; a Windows device name (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM0-9, LPT0-9, COM and LPT with a superscript 1, 2 or 3; with or without an extension) gets a `_` in front, checked again after the cut; and the name is cut to 200 bytes between characters. A closing `.pdf` of the template's own text (`x.pdf`, `x.pdf.`, `x.pdf .`, any case) is not doubled, and a `.pdf` inside a `{bookmark}` or `{name}` value is left alone. A name that is empty after that falls back to the part's default name. The default names (`<name>-partK`, `<name>-<bookmark>`) get the same device-name guard, so a document called `nul.x.pdf` gives `_nul.x-part1.pdf`. Parts that end up with the same name get -2, -3 as before, compared as `to_uppercase().to_lowercase()` so case and sigma forms count as equal. Without `name_template` the names are unchanged except for that device-name guard. What the code does about an existing file, and the tool text now says so in its own sentence: a file already in `out_dir` with a part's name is replaced (written atomically), not skipped or renamed around; only parts of the same call are de-duplicated; on a later failure a replaced file stays with the new content. The parity entry `organize.split-naming` (title: never overwrite) is now `partial`, with `doc_split` and four tests, and its notes say there is no never-overwrite mode and that the split dialog in the app has no name template and is unchanged. Why: the entry was `planned` with no tool although `doc_split` already named parts after bookmarks, and its tool text mentioned replacing an existing file only inside the failure sentence. Cost: one new source file and one optional string argument; no dependency, asset, setting or translated text; the argument is in the tool's schema and description. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/split_name_template.rs`: `a_template_names_every_part_from_its_placeholders` (`every`), `a_template_can_use_the_bookmark_a_part_starts_at` (`bookmarks: true`), `bookmark_placeholder_works_with_before_and_without_bookmarks_true` (`before`, no `bookmarks: true`), `without_a_template_the_default_names_are_unchanged`, `a_template_with_nothing_usable_falls_back_to_the_default_name`, `parts_that_get_the_same_name_stay_distinct_files`, `bookmark_titles_that_differ_only_in_case_get_distinct_files` (Intro and INTRO give `Intro.pdf` and `INTRO-2.pdf`), `a_template_cannot_put_a_part_outside_out_dir` (nine hostile templates, parts listed and the parent folder compared, nothing new beside `out_dir`), `windows_device_names_and_trailing_dots_are_made_safe`, `a_device_name_stem_is_guarded_in_the_default_names_too`, `a_long_template_is_cut_to_a_name_the_system_accepts`, `a_template_over_the_length_cap_is_refused`, `a_bad_template_is_refused_and_writes_nothing`, `a_file_with_a_parts_name_is_replaced`; and nine unit tests in `split_name.rs` (sanitizer cases, `.pdf` strip, device name after a cut, huge value, sigma fold). `hostile_args.rs` needed no named entry: `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` walks every property of every tool, so `name_template` gets the whole value zoo, and the template is parsed first so those values reach the parser. Mutations: each reverted by the tests, run one at a time. On the final code: `{bookmark}` filled only with `bookmarks: true` (the `before` test fails); no device-name guard in the default names (the nul.x test fails); fold by `to_lowercase()` only (the sigma unit test fails); superscript and 0 digits dropped from the device check (the unit test and a tool test fail). On the first version of the code, before the review fixes, whose changed parts are not all re-mutated: no device-name prefix, separators allowed by the sanitizer, no de-duplication (at least three tool tests fail), an empty name returned instead of the fallback, the template ignored, no length cap; each failed at least one test. Not mutated: on the final code, the de-duplication, the length cap, the empty-name fallback, the ignored template and the `.pdf` strip (the last has unit tests but was not mutated); the `..` collapse and the dot trim were not mutated one by one. | 2026-11-21 | minor | Signature seed values, `signature_seed_values`: What: `signature_seed_values` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the seed values of a signature field: its `/SV` dictionary (ISO 32000-2, signature fields), which tells whoever signs what the field's author wants the signature to be. This tool models four of them: `/Reasons` (text strings to pick from), `/SubFilter` (signature formats allowed, names), `/DigestMethod` (digest algorithms allowed, names) and `/AddRevInfo` (a boolean: revocation information must be embedded), and the `/Ff` flags that make an entry required and not only suggested (a set bit for each: bit 2 for `/SubFilter`, bit 4 for `/Reasons`, bit 6 for `/AddRevInfo`, bit 7 for `/DigestMethod`, counted from 1, so the values 2, 8, 32 and 64). Arguments: `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` lists it), and one of nothing (read), any of `reasons`, `sub_filters`, `digest_methods`, `add_rev_info` and `required` (set), or `remove` true. A set writes a new direct dictionary (`/Type /SV`, `/Ff` only when something is required, and the entries given) in the field, replacing `/SV`; a removal takes `/SV` out of the field's dictionary; an indirect `/SV` dictionary that is replaced or removed is left in the file unreferenced. A set is deliberately narrow: `reasons` is 1 to 16 text strings of 1 to 127 characters, none twice; `sub_filters` is 1 to 4 names of `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `adbe.pkcs7.sha1`, `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` and `ETSI.CAdES.detached`, `digest_methods` is 1 to 3 names of `SHA256`, `SHA384` and `SHA512` (both compared byte for byte, none twice, so there are at most 4 and 3); `add_rev_info` true is refused when `sub_filters` is given and holds neither `adbe.pkcs7.detached` nor `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` (revocation information is not assumed to be embeddable by `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` or `ETSI.CAdES.detached`; with no sub-filter named it is accepted); an explicitly empty array for any argument is refused by the tool (leave it out); `required` names entries (`sub_filter`, `reasons`, `add_rev_info`, `digest_method`), each of which must be given in the same call and none twice; a set with no entry is refused (use `remove`). A set replaces the whole dictionary, so it is refused while the field's `/SV` holds a key this does not write (`/Filter`, `/V`, `/Cert`, `/MDP`, `/TimeStamp`, `/LegalAttestation`, `/LockDocument`, `/AppearanceFilter` or any other key besides `/Type`, `/Ff` and the four; counted in the read as `other_entries`, null values included); a removal is not refused for them and drops them too. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a `field` that names no field, names a field that is not a signature field, or names more than one field (a read is refused too); `remove` combined with a seed value; a signed field (its `/V` is not null), and an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (found by object number). A removal of seed values the field does not have is no change (`changed` false, no write, no undo step). A write is one undo step and needs permission to modify the document. A read returns `seed_values`: `has_entry` (`/SV` is there and not null), `present` (a dictionary after references), `flags` (`/Ff` when an integer after references), `required` (the four entries whose bit is set), `reasons`, `sub_filters` and `digest_methods` (each `items`, the strings or names among the first 16 entries after references in file order, strings cut at 127 characters and names at 127 bytes, any name listed and not only the ones a set accepts; `count`, the entries the array has; `truncated`, it has more than 16 so the rest were not looked at; `skipped`, entries among the first 16 that are not of the right kind), `add_rev_info` (when a boolean after references), `other_entries`, `exact` (false when a listed reason was cut at 127 characters or a name was cut at 127 bytes or is not valid UTF-8; a reason that is not valid text is shown as it decodes and not detected; true when none is listed), `signed` (`/V` is read from the field's own dictionary only; one inherited from a parent is not counted), `settable` and `capped` (the allowance of reads was spent and what follows was not read). The tool does not check whether the document already has signatures that a change to this field would invalidate (a certification, or a signature that locks this field, can make the validator flag a change after signing); check sign_list afterwards. It does not apply them: `crates/sign` does not read `/SV`, so sign_document does not offer a reason or hold a signature to a field's values, and the validator does not check a signature against them; PDF/A is not checked either (a set does not look for the PDF/A identification). Why: `sign.seed-values` was planned with nothing behind it, and the spec-parity row for seed values said none. A headless agent preparing a form for signature could not say what a field asks of its signer, or set it, and a file made elsewhere with seed values could not be read. A read, set and remove of the four common seed values is the data the seed-value tab of Field Properties edits, it has a clear definition in the standard, and the module follows `signature_field_lock` (the sibling `/Lock` of the same field). It is read, set and remove only, of four values: no UI, no application of the values when signing and no check when validating, so the entry is partial. Cost: A read has one allowance of 10,000 reads of the field's own, shared by everything the read makes and charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`: one for the field's object, up to 33 for each reference followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged) for the `/SV` entry, `/Ff`, `/AddRevInfo` and each of the three lists, and one more for each list entry looked at, charged before the entry is looked at. At most 16 entries of each list are looked at, so the real limits cost at most 1 + 33 + 33 + 3 * (33 + 16 * 34) + 33 = 1,831 reads and cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). Counting the keys of `/SV` for `other_entries` is an iteration over the dictionary that makes no read and is not charged. Separate from that, whether the field is a structural object is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of a field that is a dictionary, holds no signature and was read within the allowance, so such a read makes one walk (a read that was capped, of a signed field or of an object that is not a dictionary makes none). A call that sets or removes seed values that are there makes three (the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of none that are there makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the field's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the field's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. The list of the form's fields comes from the form reader under its own limits, which this tool does not charge; the tool's name check is a comparison in memory. Strings are cut at 127 characters before they are kept and entries past 16 are counted and not looked at; no stream is decoded. A set allocates its at most 23 strings or names (16 reasons, 4 sub-filters and 3 digest methods), checked first, and one dictionary with the arrays; that is not charged against the allowance. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/signature_seed.rs`, 394 lines), one new edit and one new tool (`crates/automation/src/forms.rs`: 94 lines added and 1 removed), 310 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (eight tests and their helpers) and 325 in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_seed_values.rs` (six tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `signature_seed_values_are_read_with_their_lists_flags_and_other_entries` (no seed values; a null is no entry; a number is an entry and no dictionary; a dictionary with `/Ff`, `/Reasons`, `/AddRevInfo` behind references, a reason behind a reference, a number among the reasons and a string among the sub-filter names skipped, a name a set would refuse listed, `/Cert` and a null `/Filter` counted as other entries and `/Type` and `/Ff` not; `/Ff` 74 read as `/SubFilter`, `/Reasons` and `/DigestMethod` required; an `/Ff` that is a string and an `/AddRevInfo` that is a number read as none; a field with a `/V` that is a reference is signed and not settable and one whose `/V` is null is not signed), `signature_seed_values_are_set_replaced_removed_and_survive_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/SV` dictionary in the field with `/Type` and an `/Ff` of 72, leaves the old indirect one as it was, and after a full save and reopen reads back every list, the flag, `/AddRevInfo` false and a reason with a non-ASCII letter; a second set with nothing required writes no `/Ff` and drops `/Reasons`, `/DigestMethod` and `/AddRevInfo`; a removal takes only `/SV` out and keeps the field's other entries; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal), `a_set_is_refused_over_seed_values_it_would_lose_and_a_removal_is_not` (a set over an `/SV` that holds a `/Cert` is refused with its own reason and writes nothing, a removal of it is allowed, and a set afterwards works), `signature_seed_values_are_refused_on_a_signed_field_and_on_structural_objects` (the signed field is reachable from the catalog, so a save keeps it and the byte comparison guards it; 16 reasons are listed whole and 19 are listed as 16 with a count of 19 and `truncated`, the last listed being the 16th; a reason of 127 characters is exact and one of 128 is cut and not exact; a sub-filter name of 200 bytes is cut at 127 and not exact and a digest name that is not valid UTF-8 is not exact), `signature_seed_values_are_checked_before_they_are_written` (16 reasons accepted and 17 refused; `add_rev_info` true refused with only `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` or only `ETSI.CAdES.detached`, accepted with `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, with `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` beside the CAdES name, with no sub-filter, and `add_rev_info` false accepted with `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1`; The fixture of the last two is a file whose cross-reference table is written with its real offsets. Mutations: 32 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 32 failed a test and none survived. An earlier run of 24 of them on the tests as they were then let two survive (a required `/AddRevInfo` that did not have to be given, and a reason limit of 128 characters, which the test compared through the constant); two assertions were added, then clippy asked for a collapsed `if let` and a smaller test function, and the set was run again; a review then added a rule for `add_rev_info`, a refusal of empty arrays in the tool and two more tests, clippy asked again for a smaller test function, and the whole set, with three mutations for those, was run once more on the code after that, which is the run counted here. 26 in the organize module, against the eight organize tests whose names contain `seed` (all new): the bit of `/SubFilter` moved, no charge for a list entry, a null `/V` counted as signed, `/Ff` counted among the other entries, `truncated` at 16 entries, a set allowed over one other entry, `/Ff` written when nothing is required, the allowlists compared without case, a required `/AddRevInfo` that need not be given, a reason limit of 128 characters, a removal of nothing counted as a change, a removal refused over other entries, skipped entries not counted, a repeated reason accepted, the exactness of a name ignored, `capped` never set, `/Type` not written, the required entries not folded into `/Ff`, the structural objects settable, the exactness of a string ignored, an entry required twice accepted, an empty reason accepted, a repeated digest method accepted, the `add_rev_info` rule dropped, `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` not counted as a PKCS#7 name, and `required` read without the `/DigestMethod` bit; and 6 in the tool and the edit, against the six tests of `signature_seed_values.rs`: a field that is not a signature field accepted, `remove` accepted with seed values, empty arrays accepted, `changed` always true, `flags` not reported and the undo label changed. Survived: none. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones, and the real limits cost at most 1,831, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared and covered by the chain test here), the walk of the page tree cut short, the bit values of `/Reasons`, `/AddRevInfo` and `/DigestMethod` (asserted by the last organize test but mutated only for `/SubFilter`), the contents of the two allowlists (the organize test loops over the constants, so a name taken out of one would not fail it; the tool test uses `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `SHA256`, `SHA384` and `SHA512` as literals), the read of `/AddRevInfo` and of `/Reasons` taken one at a time, the tool's own check of the names in `required` (its message is asserted but the check was not mutated), the schema's `maxItems`, `Document::signature_seed` and `Document::signature_seed_change` when a document has no editor, the permission check of the edit in `check_permission` (the modify-restriction test covers it but it was not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (the undo label the test reads comes from the match, which was mutated), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts. Not tested: the bit numbers of `/Ff` against a file written by another product (they are taken from the specification as read, and no fixture here was made elsewhere with `/Ff` set), a removal on a PDF/A file (nothing checks it), an `/SV` or a list that is a stream, a negative or very large `/Ff`, a field whose widget is a kid of the field (every field here has its widget merged into its dictionary, so a seed value on the field object with a separate widget is not covered), an `/V` inherited from a parent field (`signed` reads the field's own `/V` only, so a signed field whose `/V` is inherited reads as unsigned), whether the validator flags a signature after a seed-value change on a file that already has signatures (`sign_list` is not read in the tests), a reason that is not valid text (shown as it decodes and not detected, so `exact` stays true), a seed value read by Acrobat or another viewer when signing (the tool does not apply them, and nothing here signs against them), a page-tree walk over its limits, a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable), and the user interface (none).
