| 2026-11-21 | minor | Screen annotation media action, `screen_actions`: What: `screen_actions` (new, headless) reads and removes the sound, movie, rendition and rich-media actions of screen annotations, and never sets one: a screen annotation is a dictionary whose `/Subtype` is `Screen` (the area of a page where a rendition plays), and its `/A` is the action that starts a clip; the four kinds are `/S` `Sound`, `Movie`, `Rendition` or `RichMediaExecute` (compared byte for byte; a rendition action can carry a `/JS` script). Arguments: `doc`, and `remove` (a position from 0 in the listing; leave it out to read). The file is scanned by object number (`Document::scan_objects`), not through the pages, so a screen annotation that no page lists is listed too and one written as a direct dictionary inside another object is not; only a dictionary (not a stream) whose `/Subtype` is a direct name `Screen` counts. A read returns `screen_actions`: `count`, `truncated` (more than 256 were found; the first 256, in object-number order, are listed), `capped` and `items` with `index`, `object`, `has_entry` (`/A` is not null), `present` (`/A` is a dictionary after references), `action` (the `/S` name, cut at 127 bytes; any kind of action is named), `exact` (false when it was cut or is not valid UTF-8), `kind` (`sound`, `movie`, `rendition` or `rich_media_execute`, null for any other action), `has_script` (a rendition's `/JS` is stored and not null; the script is not read), `has_next` (a `/Next` chain), `has_additional_actions` (the annotation has an `/AA`; it is only reported) and `removable`. An `/S` that is an indirect reference is not followed and reads as no kind. There is no set and no argument that could write one, because a document written by this tool must not start playback or carry a script, and a rendition needs media clips and players this crate does not build. A removal takes `/A` out of the annotation, with any `/Next` chain it has (the chain goes with the action), only when `/S` is one of the four names; an annotation with another kind of action (a JavaScript or web action included, which are kept), a null `/A`, no `/A`, an `/S` that is not a direct name, or a position with nothing listed (also 256 and more) is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. The annotation's `/AA`, appearance, `/MK`, the rest of its dictionary, the page's `/Annots`, the rendition or clip an action named and an indirect old `/A` dictionary are left as they are (an indirect `/A` stays in the file unreferenced); the result's `removed` says what went (`action`, `kind`, `next`). A write is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveScreenAction`, labelled Remove screen annotation media action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. A removal is refused, before anything is written, for an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number) and when the page-tree walk was cut short, and when the scan could not find the position within its allowance. It does not play anything, does not read a rendition (`/R`, media clips, player requirements), and does not check for PDF/A, which does not allow sound, movie or rendition actions (this tool can take them out, and says nothing about whether a file then conforms). Sibling tools that exist already: `link_media_action` reads and removes the same four kinds of action on a link annotation (it reads and removes, and sets none), `link_launch_action`, `link_named_action`, `link_embedded_action` and `link_thread_action` read, set and remove other kinds of action on a link, and `reference_xobjects` has the same scan-by-object-number shape. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::screen_action` (`read_screen_actions`, `screen_action_change`, `remove_screen_action`, `MAX_LISTED`, `MAX_GETS`), run by the new edit, with `Document::screen_actions` and `Document::screen_action_change`. `misc.legacy-multimedia` (Legacy multimedia (screen annotations, renditions)) moves from planned to partial and its notes say what is missing; the Screen, Movie, RichMedia, 3D row and the Sound, Movie, Rendition, RichMediaExecute row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now say what is there. Why: `misc.legacy-multimedia` was planned with nothing behind it. A headless agent could not see that a screen annotation would start a rendition, a sound or a movie, or run a rendition's script, or take that out of a copy, and `link_media_action` only covers links. The screen annotation is where a rendition action normally sits, its `/A` has a small definition in the standard, and a removal can be tested in a saved file by what the annotation's `/A` holds. Only reading and removing the action are done, so the entry is partial and its title is only partly met: renditions are not read, nothing creates or deletes a screen annotation, no viewer here plays anything, and there is no UI. Cost: each scan (`scan`, used by `read_screen_actions`, `screen_action_change` and `remove_screen_action`) has one allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each read is made: one for each object scanned (the last is paid for only when it is there to read) and, for each screen annotation, up to 33 for the reference chain behind `/A` (the first object and up to 32 more, as `Document::resolve` follows them, each hop charged); when it cannot pay the scan stops with `capped`, and an annotation whose chain was not fully paid for is not listed. A rendition's `/JS`, `/Next` and `/AA` are looked at where they are written and make no read. Not charged by this module: the first read of the catalog in a page-tree walk, and the read `Document::update_dict` makes of the annotation when a removal writes. The page-tree walk that decides whether an object is a structural one is `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read; it runs once for a read when something is listed and once for a change check or a removal that found its position. Through the tool, with `remove`, a call makes a scan before the edit (to say what goes), one for the change check and one for the final read, so a read makes 1 scan and, only when something is listed, 1 walk, and a removal through the tool makes: with a position of 256 or more, 2 scans (the check and the edit make none, the position is refused as no entry before scanning) and 2 walks when something is listed (0 when nothing is); with a position below 256 where nothing is listed, 3 scans and 2 walks when something is listed (0 when nothing is); at an annotation with no media action, 3 scans and 3 walks; and at one whose action goes, 4 scans and 4 walks (the same three, and the applied edit's own). Each scan reads the whole file's objects, so a call on a very large file does up to four passes over it. A name is kept cut at 127 bytes, and at most 256 annotations are listed. No content, media or script stream is decoded (`Document::scan_objects` does decode object streams, to reach the objects in them). No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/screen_action.rs`, 245 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 226 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests) and a new `crates/automation/tests/it/screen_actions.rs` (247 lines, four tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `screen_annotations_are_listed_with_what_their_action_holds` (the four kinds; `/Next`, a rendition's `/JS` and an `/AA` noticed, `/JS` on a movie not counting, null `/JS`, `/Next` and `/AA` reading as none; six other actions (`URI`, `JavaScript`, `ImportData`, `sound`, `Sounds`, `GoTo3DView`) listed and not media; the action behind a reference; an `/S` behind a reference reading as no action; no `/A`, a null `/A` and an `/A` that is a number; a 300-byte `/S` cut at 127 and not exact, and an `/S` that is not valid UTF-8 not exact; a link, a widget, a stream, a `/Subtype` behind a reference and a `/Subtype` that is a string not listed; positions counted from 0 in object-number order), `a_screen_annotations_media_action_is_removed_with_its_chain_and_survives_a_save` (a removal over an action behind a reference takes `/A` out, keeps `/AA`, `/T`, `/Rect` and the `/Subtype`, leaves the old object, keeps the other annotations at their positions with their actions, and reads back after a full save; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a `/Next` chain goes with the action; a JavaScript action, a web action whose `/Next` is a sound action, an indirect `/S`, a null `/A`, no `/A`, and positions 8, 255, 256, 1000 and `usize::MAX` are `NoChange` or `NoEntry` and leave the saved bytes equal, each annotation reachable from the catalog so a change would show), `a_screen_annotation_that_is_a_structural_object_is_refused` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node and a page, each marked as a screen annotation with a media action, are listed, not removable and refused, the reason containing the words page tree, with the saved bytes equal and `/A` kept; an ordinary one is removable) and `the_screen_annotation_listing_stops_at_256_and_every_read_is_paid_for_first` (257 annotations: 257 counted, 256 listed, `truncated`, and position 256 `NoEntry`; a scan with exactly as many reads as there are objects lists the last annotation and with one fewer is `capped` with nothing listed; a change check is allowed with the first and refused naming the allowance with the second; an action behind a reference needs one more read and is not listed without it; chains of 32 references are followed with 33 reads and one of 33 is not, and with 32 reads the scan is `capped`); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/screen_actions.rs`, through `screen_actions`: `the_screen_annotations_are_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file whose page lists five screen annotations and a link with a sound action: the link is not listed; the listed kinds, a rendition's whole item including `has_script`, `has_next` and `has_additional_actions`, a URI action as `action` with no kind, an annotation with no `/A`; a read leaves no `changed`; three removals found in the saved file as no `/A` while the `/AA` and the URI action stay, `removed` naming the action, kind and `/Next`; removals of a URI action, no action and positions 5, 6, 256 and 1,000,000 `changed` false with saved bytes equal and an action already out `changed` false; three undo steps labelled Remove screen annotation media action, the saved bytes after the last equal to the file before the first removal, and three redos equal to the file saved after the removals; the clock is pinned), `there_is_no_way_to_set_a_media_action` (calls with extra arguments `kind`, `action`, `script` and `set` write nothing and add no undo step, the schema's only properties are `doc` and `remove`, and the description says there is no set), `a_bad_argument_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (a negative, a string and a fractional `remove` named; `i64::MAX` and a null `remove` leave the file as it was) and `a_screen_annotation_that_is_a_page_keeps_its_action_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal that would change it, still reads, and a removal that finds no media action is `changed` false; a page object marked as a screen annotation with a sound action is listed first, not removable, refused with the words page tree, with the saved bytes equal and no undo step). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep in `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 19 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 19 failed a test, 18 of them counted: 15 in the organize module, against the four organize tests whose names contain `screen_annotation` (all new), the `Movie` kind taken out, the `/Subtype` not checked, a stream accepted as a screen annotation, `has_script` set for any kind of action, a null `/Next` counted as present, a removal that also takes `/AA` out, an annotation with no media action counted as a change, the structural check dropped from the change check, the structural check dropped from the read, the read of each object not paid for, `exact` never cleared, `truncated` never set, a scan stopped by its allowance reported as no entry, the listing not cut at 256 and an `/A` behind a reference not followed; and 3 in the tool, against the four tests of `screen_actions.rs`, `removed` never reported, a call that changes nothing reporting `changed` true and a refusal swallowed. The mutations were run by two scripts that apply each one, run the tests of that crate that name the module or the tool, and put the file back; the final gates were run after the last one, on the restored files. | 2026-11-21 | minor | Link media action, `link_media_action`: What: `link_media_action` (new, headless) reads and removes the sound, movie, rendition and rich-media actions of a link, and never sets one: an action dictionary in the link annotation's `/A` whose `/S` is `Sound`, `Movie`, `Rendition` or `RichMediaExecute` (compared byte for byte; the first two are deprecated in PDF 2.0, and a rendition action can carry a `/JS` script). Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and `remove` true or nothing (read). A read reports what is stored: `is_media` and `kind` (`sound`, `movie`, `rendition` or `rich_media_execute`), the `/S` name as `action` (any kind of action is named; `exact` false when it or a movie's operation is cut at 127 bytes or is not valid UTF-8), a movie's `/Operation` name (`operation`, when it is a name after references), a rendition's `/OP` (`rendition_op`, when it is an integer after references; its range is not checked), `has_script` (a rendition's `/JS` is stored and not null; the script is not read), `has_target` (a movie's `/Annotation` or `/T`, a rendition's `/AN` or a rich-media action's `/TA` is stored and not null; the value is not followed, so a reference counts as present), `has_next` (a `/Next` chain), `destination` (the link also has a `/Dest`) and `settable`. An `/S` that is an indirect reference is not followed and reads as no action. There is no set and no argument that could write one, because a document written by this tool must not start playback or carry a script, and a rendition needs media clips and players this crate does not build. A removal takes `/A` out of the link, with any `/Next` chain it has (the chain goes with the action: a media action reached only through another action's `/Next` is not touched), only when `/S` is one of the four names; a link with no action, with another kind of action (a JavaScript, a launch, a web or a menu action included, which are kept), with a null `/A` or with an `/S` that is not a direct name is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. The link's `/Dest` and the rest of the link are kept, the screen, movie or rich-media annotation an action points at is not touched, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `removed` says what went (`action`, `kind`, `next`). A write is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveLinkMediaAction`, labelled Remove link media action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number). It does not play anything, and does not check for PDF/A, which does not allow sound, movie or rendition actions (this tool can take them out, and says nothing about whether a file then conforms; the preflight crate already lists the sound, movie and rendition actions as forbidden). Sibling tools that exist already: `link_launch_action`, `link_named_action`, `link_embedded_action` and `link_thread_action` read, set and remove other kinds of action, `link_run_javascript` runs a link's script, `link_add` and `link_edit` write web, page and remote-document links, and `link_list` reports a link with one of these actions as `other` (the `/S` name) unless the link also has a `/Dest`, which it shows instead. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::media_action` (`read_media_action`, `media_action_change`, `remove_media_action`, `MAX_GETS`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_media_action` and `Document::link_media_action_change`. `edit.action-media` (Play sound / media / rich-media actions) moves from planned to partial and its notes say that only reading and removing exist; the Sound, Movie, Rendition and RichMediaExecute row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now says what is there (GoTo3DView stays none). Why: `edit.action-media` was planned with nothing behind it, and the spec table said none. A headless agent could not see that a link would play a sound or a movie, start a rendition or run a rich-media command, or take that out of a copy. The four actions have a small definition in the standard, and a removal can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` holds. Only reading and removing are done, so the entry is partial and its title (playing) is not met: no viewer here plays anything, nothing creates a media action or a clip, and there is no UI. Cost: each call of `read_media_action` and `media_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link and up to 33 for each reference chain behind `/A` and behind the one entry read of the action (a movie's `/Operation` or a rendition's `/OP`; the other kinds read none), as `Document::resolve` follows them (the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), so at most 1 + 2 * 33 = 67 reads, far below the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`); a change check needs nothing more than that read. `remove_media_action` runs a change check and then writes. Separate from that and not charged by this module: the page-tree walk that decides whether the object is a structural one is `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for each read of an object that is a link: once for a read, 3 times for a removal through the tool that changes nothing (the read before the edit, the check and the answer) and 4 times for one that changes the link (those three and the applied edit), counted by a temporary counter in `structural` that was taken out again. The checks of `/Next`, `/Dest`, `/JS`, `/AN`, `/TA`, `/Annotation` and `/T` look at the stored value and make no read. The tool finds the link with `Document::links`, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs`. A name is kept cut at 127 bytes. No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/media_action.rs`, 234 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 237 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests) and 228 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_media_action.rs` (four tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_media_action_is_read_with_its_kind_and_entries` (the four kinds; a movie's operation, `/T` and `/Annotation` as target and a `/Next`; a rendition's `/OP`, `/JS` and `/AN`, an `/OP` that is a real, a name or a string reading as none; a movie operation that is a string reading as none and a 300-byte one cut at 127 and not exact; `/JS` on a movie not counting; null `/JS`, `/AN`, `/OP` and `/Next` reading as none; a rich-media `/TA`; nine other names (`URI`, `JavaScript`, `Launch`, `Named`, `GoToE`, `sound`, `Sounds`, `RichMedia`, `SetOCGState`) reported as `action` and not media; the action behind a reference; an `/S` behind a reference reading as no action; a link with only a `/Dest`, a null `/A` and an `/A` that is a number; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link), `a_media_action_is_removed_with_its_chain_and_survives_a_save` (a removal over an action behind a reference takes `/A` out, keeps `/Dest`, `/Border` and the `/Subtype`, leaves the old object, and reads back after a full save; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; all four kinds go with a `/Next` chain; a JavaScript, a launch, a web action whose `/Next` is a sound action, an indirect `/S`, a null `/A` and a link with no action are `NoChange` and leave the saved bytes equal, with each link reachable from the catalog so a change would show), `a_media_action_is_refused_on_what_is_not_a_link` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a widget, a number and a missing object refused for a removal with the saved bytes equal; a page marked as a link and carrying a media action still refused, the reason containing the word page, and its `/A` kept) and `every_read_a_media_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a direct movie action is read with exactly 1 read and with 0 not even the link; an action and an operation behind references need 3 reads in all and with each one fewer the next field in that order is not read; a rendition's `/OP` the same; a sound action needs only the link's read; chains of 32 references are followed with 1 + 33 reads and one of 33 is not; the same for an operation; a change check is allowed with 1 read and refused naming the allowance with 0, and with a reference to the action it needs 2); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_media_action.rs`, through `link_media_action`: `a_media_action_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (reads of a sound, a movie with a `/Next`, a rendition with a script and a rich-media action with a `/Dest`, and of a JavaScript action as not media; `link_list` reporting `other` Sound, Movie and Rendition and a page destination for the link that also has a `/Dest`; a read leaving the saved bytes equal; four removals found in the saved file as no `/A`, no `/Next` and the `/Dest` kept where there was one; a removal of a JavaScript action, of a web action and of a link with no action `changed` false, with a sound action behind a web action's `/Next` kept in the saved file; four undo steps labelled Remove link media action, the saved bytes after the last equal to the file before the first removal, and four redos equal to the file saved after the removals; the clock is pinned), `there_is_no_way_to_set_a_media_action` (calls with extra arguments `kind`, `operation` and `script` write nothing and add no undo step, and the schema's only properties are `doc`, `index`, `page` and `remove`), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (a `remove` that is not a boolean, a link and a page that do not exist, and an inline link for a removal and for a read; saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_removal_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal, still reads, and a removal of nothing is `changed` false). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep in `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 15 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 15 failed a test: 13 in the organize module, against the four organize tests whose names contain `media_action` (all new), the `Sound` kind taken out, the kind matched case-insensitively, a removal that also takes `/Dest` out, a link with no media action counted as a change, a null `/Next` counted as present, a rendition's `/OP` read without following a reference, a movie's `/Operation` read without following a reference, a rendition's `/JS` not noticed, the structural check dropped from `settable`, `exact` not cleared for a cut operation, the `/Subtype` of the link not checked, a movie's `/T` ignored as a target and the read of the link not paid for; and 2 in the tool, against the four tests of `link_media_action.rs`, `removed` never reported and a removal that reports `changed` false after writing. The mutations were run by a script that applies each one, runs the tests of that crate that name the tool or the module, and puts the file back; the four organize tests passed again after the last one. Survived: none. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared and covered by the chain tests here), the walk of the page tree cut short (`!cut_short` in `settable`), the tool's result fields other than `removed` and `changed`, `Document::link_media_action` and `Document::link_media_action_change` when a document has no editor, the permission check of the edit in `check_permission` (the modify-restriction test covers it but it was not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (its text is asserted by the undo test but was not mutated), and the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`. Not tested: a removal on a PDF/A file (nothing checks it), a rendition `/OP` outside 0 to 4 (read as the integer it is), an `/S` that is an indirect reference through the tool (tested only in the organize module, where it reads as no action and a removal changes nothing), a rich-media action in `link_list` without a `/Dest` (the one tested has one), a link object that is itself a reference to another object (it reads as not a link), an `/A` or `/Annots` array that is a stream or behind a reference chain on a page, a link shared by two pages, a page-tree walk over its limits, an action followed or removed by another viewer, the optimizer's handling of these actions, and a sound action whose `/Sound` stream is present (the test files have none; no stream is read). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 377 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 698 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (it skipped: no craft-i18n checkout); the 15 mutations above. After an amendment that changed only text (the tool's description in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`, the parity notes and the docs), `cargo fmt --check`, clippy on the three crates and the automation tests were run again; the organize and engine tests, the mutations, layers, assets, wasm and i18n-report were not run again (no code line changed; the organize and engine crates do not depend on the description). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the optimize and preflight crates' tests (not touched), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file this tool changed, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-21 | minor | Link embedded-file action, `link_embedded_action`: What: `link_embedded_action` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the go-to-an-embedded-file action of a link: an action dictionary `/S /GoToE` in the link annotation's `/A` (ISO 32000-2, go-to-embedded actions), whose `/T` is a target dictionary (`/R` says whether the file is in a parent (`P`) or a child (`C`) of this document, `/N` is its name in the `/EmbeddedFiles` name tree, `/P` and `/A` name a file attachment annotation, and a nested `/T` is the target of the target), whose `/D` is the place in that file (a name, a string or an array whose first item is an integer page index), and whose optional `/F` (a file specification) and `/NewWindow` put it in another file or ask for a new window. Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `embedded_file` with `target_page` (and optional `new_window`) or `remove` true. A read reports what is stored, of any kind: `/F` as a string (its text, cut at 255 characters) or as a dictionary (`file_kind` says which; a dictionary's name is not read), `/D` as `destination_kind` (`name`, `string` or `array`) and, for an array that starts with an integer page index of zero or more, `destination_page` (1-based; a negative index or an item that is not an integer reads as none), `/NewWindow` when it is a boolean, the target's `/R` name (`relationship`) and `/N` string (`embedded_file`, cut at 255 characters, `exact` false when the name, the relationship, `/S` or a string `/F` is cut), whether the target has a `/P` or `/A` (`has_annotation_target`) or a nested `/T` (`has_nested_target`) whose stored value is not null (not followed, so a reference counts as present), and whether the action has a `/Next` chain. A set is deliberately narrower than a read: `embedded_file` must be the name of an attachment this document has at that moment (compared byte for byte with the raw keys of the `/EmbeddedFiles` name tree, as read by `attachments::entries`, which is how a viewer looks a name up: a key stored as UTF-16 reads as the same text in `doc_info` but is not found by an ASCII name, and a set naming it is refused) and be 1 to 255 bytes of letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, space and parentheses (the name `add_attachment` gives a second file of one name, `name (2)`), with no space at either end, no leading `-` and at least one letter or digit, and `target_page` is 1 to 1,000,000; it writes `/T << /R /C /N (name) >>` and `/D [page-1 /Fit]` and nothing else, so no `/F`, no `/P`, no `/A`, no nested `/T`, no named destination and no target in a parent document. A set replaces the link's whole `/A` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /Action`, `/S /GoToE`, `/D`, `/T`, and `/NewWindow` only when given) and takes `/Dest` out, which a link with an action must not have; a `/Next` chain, an `/F` or any other entry of the old action is not kept, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `replaced` says what was there (`action`, `has_entry`, `destination`, `next`). A removal takes `/A` out, with any `/Next` chain, only when it is a GoToE action; a link with no action, or another kind of action (a launch or a remote go-to included), is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A set that repeats what the link has is written again, as one more undo step. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` with `embedded_file`, `target_page` or `new_window`, `new_window` without both `embedded_file` and `target_page`, one of them without the other, a name or page that fails the rules above, a name that is not an attachment of the document, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number). A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetLinkEmbeddedAction`, labelled Set link embedded-file action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. It does not check that the attachment is a PDF file or has that many pages, does not check for PDF/A (which limits embedded files and some actions), and does not run the action. Sibling tools that exist already: `link_launch_action`, `link_named_action` and `link_thread_action` do the same for the other three actions, `link_add` and `link_edit` write web, page and remote-document links, and `link_list` reports a link with a GoToE action only as `other: "GoToE"`; the optimizer's Discard external cross-references already removes these actions from a copy. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::embedded_action` (`read_embedded_action`, `embedded_action_change`, `set_embedded_action`, `EmbeddedTarget`, `MAX_NAME_BYTES`, `MAX_PAGE`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_embedded_action` and `Document::link_embedded_action_change`. `edit.action-goto-embedded` (Go to a target in an embedded file (GoToE)) moves from planned to partial; the GoToE row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now says what is there. Why: `edit.action-goto-embedded` was planned with nothing behind it, and the GoToE row of the spec table said none. A headless agent could not give a link an action that goes to a page of a file attached to the document, or see that a link had one. The action has a small definition in the standard and can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` holds. It is read, set and remove only, with a deliberately narrow set (a page of an existing attachment of this document), no viewer that follows it, no UI and no other target, so the entry is partial. Cost: each call of `read_embedded_action` and `embedded_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link and up to 33 for each reference chain behind `/A`, `/NewWindow`, `/F`, `/D`, the first item of a `/D` array, `/T`, and the `/R` and `/N` of `/T` (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), so at most 1 + 8 * 33 = 265 reads, far below the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`); a change check needs nothing more than that read. `set_embedded_action` runs a change check and then writes. Separate from that and not charged by this module: the page-tree walk that decides whether the object is a structural one is `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for each read of an object that is a link: once for a read and 4 times for a set through the tool (its check, the extra read before the edit, the applied edit and the answer); the listing of the raw attachment keys (`attachments::entries`, the `/Names /EmbeddedFiles` name tree under the limits of `crate::import::name_tree_entries`) runs once for each change check of a set (a removal makes none), is not charged against the allowance, and for a tree past its limits lists only what was read, so a set can refuse a name that is there; and the tool finds the link with `Document::links`, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs`. A name is kept cut at 127 input bytes (`/S`, `/R`) or 255 characters (`/N`, `/F`). No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/embedded_action.rs`, 362 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 421 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests) and 290 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_embedded_action.rs` (four tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_go_to_embedded_action_is_read_with_its_target_and_destination` (a string `/F` with `/D [2 /Fit]`, `/NewWindow`, `/T` with `/R /C` and `/N`, and a `/Next` chain; a parent target with `/P`, `/A` and a nested `/T` stored, and a name and a string `/D`; only `/A`, only `/P`, and null `/P`, `/A` and `/T` reading as none; a first item of `/D` that is a page object, `-1`, `i64::MIN` or a name, an empty array and `i64::MAX` (no overflow) all reading no page; a file specification dictionary reported as one and not read, an `/F` and a `/T` that are numbers, an `/R` and an `/N` of the wrong type and a `/NewWindow` that is a string reading as none; the file, destination, target, relationship and name behind references; a 300-byte name cut at 255 and not exact; the names `GoToR`, `GoToEx`, `GoTo` and `Launch` reading as other actions with no target; a link with only a `/Dest`; null `/A` and `/Dest`; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link), `a_go_to_embedded_action_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set over an indirect web action and a `/Dest` writes a direct `/Action` dictionary with `/S /GoToE`, `/D [2 /Fit]` for page 3, a `/T` of exactly `/R /C` and `/N`, `/NewWindow`, no `/F` and no `/Next`, takes `/Dest` out, leaves the old object and the rest of the link, and reads back after a full save; a set without a window writes none and drops an old `/Next`, `/F`, `/P` target and window; a removal takes only `/A` out; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a web action, a remote go-to and a launch are `NoChange` for a removal and kept; a set naming no attachment is refused and the saved bytes are equal), `an_embedded_target_is_a_plain_name_and_a_page` (ten accepted names, among them the longest allowed, one made only of a digit, ones with `.`, `_`, `-` and two spaces inside, and two with parentheses, and 28 refused ones: empty, a space at either end, a leading `-`, only dots and spaces, `-` alone, `/`, a backslash, `:`, `;`, a backtick, `$(`, a vertical bar, `&`, both quotes, `*`, `?`, `<` and `>`, `%`, `~`, non-ASCII, a newline, a NUL, a backslash with a parenthesis and 256 bytes; a page marked as a link still refused, the reason containing the word page), `every_read_a_go_to_embedded_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a direct action with window, file, destination and target is read with exactly 1 read and with 0 not even the link; The hostile-argument sweep in `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 9 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 9 failed a test: in the organize module, against the eight organize tests whose names contain `embedded` (seven of them new), the attachment check compared as decoded text, the attachment check dropped, parentheses taken out of the allowlist, a semicolon admitted, the leading `-` refusal dropped, the letter-or-digit rule dropped, a trailing space admitted, the 255-byte limit dropped and page 0 admitted. | 2026-11-21 | minor | Link file action, `link_launch_action`: What: `link_launch_action` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the open-a-file action of a link: an action dictionary `/S /Launch` in the link annotation's `/A` (ISO 32000-2 12.6.4.6, Table 207), whose `/F` is a file specification and whose optional `/NewWindow` asks for a new window. Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `file` (with optional `new_window`) or `remove` true. A read reports what is stored, of any kind: `/F` as a string or as a file specification dictionary (its `/UF`, else its `/F`, as text, cut at 255 characters; `file_kind` says which; `url` is true when the dictionary's `/FS` is `URL`), `/NewWindow` when it is a boolean, and whether the action has a `/Next` chain or a `/Win`, `/Mac` or `/Unix` entry whose value is not null (`platform`; a key whose value is null counts as none here, while the render crate's `launch_pdf` counts a `/Win` key even when its value is null, and then does not read the launch as a link). A set is deliberately narrower than a read: `file` must be a relative path to a `.pdf` file (only letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, space and `/`, 1 to 255 bytes, so no backslash, no colon, no drive, no URL and no shell character; no leading `/` and no space at either end; no part between slashes that is empty, made only of dots or spaces (so no `.` or `..`) or starting with `-`), because a document written by this tool must not name a program or leave its folder; the render crate already reads a launch of a `.pdf` file without `/Win` as a link to that file (`launch_pdf` in `crates/render/src/inspect/annotations.rs`) and no other launch, and a set writes only what that reads. A set replaces the link's whole `/A` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /Action`, `/S /Launch`, `/F` a string, and `/NewWindow` only when given) and takes `/Dest` out, which a link with an action must not have; a `/Next` chain, platform entries or any other entry of the old action are not kept, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `replaced` says what was there (`action`, `has_entry`, `destination`, `next`, `platform`). A removal takes `/A` out only when it is a launch action, whatever it launches (including a program); a link with no action, or another kind of action, is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A set that repeats what the link has is written again, as one more undo step. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` with `file` or with `new_window`, `new_window` without `file`, a `file` that fails the rules above, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number). A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetLinkLaunchAction`, labelled Set link file action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. Sibling tools that exist already: `link_named_action` and `link_thread_action` do the same for the other two actions, `link_add` and `link_edit` write web, page and remote-document links, and `link_list` reports a link with a launch action only as `other: "Launch"`. PDF/A does not allow launch actions and a set does not check for that. It does not run the action, and writes no `/Win`, `/Mac` or `/Unix` entry, no file specification dictionary and no non-PDF target. A headless agent could not give a link an open-a-file action or see that a link had one. The action has a small definition in the standard and can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` holds. It is read, set and remove only, with a deliberately narrow set (PDF targets only), no UI and no other target, so the entry is partial. Cost: each call of `read_launch_action` and `launch_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link and up to 33 for each reference chain behind `/A`, `/NewWindow`, `/F` and, when `/F` is a dictionary, behind its `/UF`, `/F` and `/FS` (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged; the `/F` of the dictionary is not read when its `/UF` is a string), so at most 1 + 6 * 33 = 199 reads, far below the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`); a change check needs nothing more than that read. `set_launch_action` runs a change check and then writes. Separate from that, whether the object is a structural one is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for each read of an object that is a link, and the tool makes it as many times as `link_named_action` does (once for a read, and for each check, applied edit and answer of a write). Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `name` (exactly one of `NextPage`, `PrevPage`, `FirstPage`, `LastPage`, `Print`; nothing else is written) or `remove` true. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` combined with `name`, a `name` that is not on the list, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number; a link object that is itself a reference to another object reads as not a link). The Named row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now says what is there for a link. Why: `edit.action-named` was planned with nothing behind it, although the entry's safe list already ran for form buttons. five undo steps all labelled Set link menu action, the unchanged call adding none, the saved bytes after the last undo equal to the file before the first set, and five redos equal to the file before them), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (nine refusals by message: `remove` with `name`, a `name` of `Quit`, of `nextpage` and of the empty string, a `name` that is a number, a `remove` that is a string, a link and a page that do not exist, and a link written in the page; the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a set and still reads; a removal where there is nothing to remove is `changed` false rather than a refusal). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep of `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 23, restoring after each, all run on the final code (the module, the tool and the tests were not edited after; only the docs and the commit text changed after the gates): 22 failed a test and 1 survived. In the organize module, 19 (each run against the four organize tests whose names contain `named_action`): `/S` always read as a named action, `/Dest` not taken out by a set, a removal of a link with no named action counted as a change, a removal that takes out `/Dest` instead of `/A`, the cut of a name not making the read inexact, every name safe, any name accepted as a target, `/Type` not written, the structural objects settable, a null `/Next` counted, a null `/Dest` counted, a null `/A` counted, `/N` not written (`Print` written for every target), a read cut short still settable, `/S` matched by its first letter, an object that is not a link settable, `/Next` never reported, an allowance of one more read than given, and the walk of the page tree cut short ignored: 18 failed a test and the last one survived. In the tool, 4 (each run against the 3 automation tests): a removal of nothing reported as a change, `replaced` never reported, the name dropped from the target (`Print` always), and `remove` accepted with `name`; all 4 failed a test. History, for the record: on an earlier run, before the read test was given a null `/Dest` and the names `Name`, `Namedx` and `N` (fixtures, not only assertions), two of the organize mutations (a null `/Dest` counted and `/S` matched by its first letter) survived, which is why those were added; every mutation was then run again on the final code. | 2026-11-21 | minor | Link article action, `link_thread_action`: What: `link_thread_action` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the article action of a link: an action dictionary `/S /Thread` in the link annotation's `/A` (ISO 32000-2 12.6.4.7), which tells a viewer to go to an article thread of the document and, optionally, to one of its beads. `/D` is the thread (the thread dictionary as an indirect reference, its position in the catalog's `/Threads`, or the title of the thread as a text string) and `/B` the bead (the bead dictionary, or its position along the thread); an `/F` (a file specification) puts the thread in another file. PdfCraft listed and removed article threads (`article_threads`) but nothing could point a link at one, and no tool read such an action: `link_list` reports a link with this action only as `other: "Thread"`. Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `thread` (1 to 256: the `index` that `article_threads` lists plus 1, the position in `/Threads`) with an optional `bead` (1 to 256, the position along the thread from its `/F` along `/N`), or `remove` true. A set replaces the link's whole `/A` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /Action`, `/S /Thread`, `/D` as the reference of the thread object and, for a bead, `/B` as the reference of the bead object) and takes `/Dest` out, which a link with an action must not have; a `/Next` chain or any other entry of the old action is not kept, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `replaced` says what was there (`action`, `has_entry`, `destination`, and `next`, whether a `/Next` chain went with it). A removal takes `/A` out of the link only when it is a thread action; a link with no action, or with another kind of action, is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A set that repeats what the link already has is written again, as one more undo step. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` combined with `thread` or `bead`, `bead` without `thread`, a `thread` or `bead` outside 1 to 256, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number; a link object that is itself a reference to another object reads as not a link), a `thread` that is not an entry of `/Threads` that is an indirect object whose object is a dictionary, and a `bead` that the thread does not have among its first 256 (a thread whose `/F` is not a reference to a dictionary has none). A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetLinkThreadAction`, labelled Set link article action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`; a removal has the same label) that needs permission to modify the document, so a copy protected against changes refuses a write and still reads. The result is `page`, `index` and `thread_action`: `has_entry` (an `/A` that is not null), `present` (it is a dictionary after references), `action` (the name of `/S`, cut at 127 bytes and shown with replacement characters when not valid UTF-8, or null), `exact`, `is_thread`, `remote` (the action has a non-null `/F`, so its thread is in another file and `thread` and `bead` are not read), `title` (when `/D` is a text string, the string cut at 127 characters; it is not matched against the titles of the threads, so `thread` stays null), `has_next` (the action has a non-null `/Next`), `thread` and `bead` (1-based positions that `/D` and `/B` name, among the first 256 entries of `/Threads` and the first 256 beads, as a reference or as an integer position in range; null when `/D` or `/B` is missing, names none of them, is a title or a direct dictionary, or the action is remote), `destination` (the link has a `/Dest` that is not null), `settable` (it is a link and not one of the structural objects above, that walk was not cut short, and the read was not `capped`) and `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent); a call that writes also has `changed`, and a set that wrote has `replaced`. It does not run the action: nothing in the viewer goes to the thread or bead, no thread or bead is added, no other kind of action is read, a title-named or remote `/D` is reported and not resolved, and there is no Link Properties page for it. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::thread_action` (`read_thread_action`, `thread_action_change`, `set_thread_action`, `ThreadTarget`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_thread_action` and `Document::link_thread_action_change`. Parity: `edit.action-thread` (Read-an-article action) moves from planned to partial with the tool and the tests, because the data of the action can now be made, read and removed; the action itself is not run and there is no UI, so it is not shipped. The Thread row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which said none) now says what is there. Why: `edit.action-thread` was planned with nothing behind it. The article threads of a document could be listed and removed, and the one object that makes a thread useful to a reader, a link that goes to it, could not be made or even read by a headless agent. The action has a small, fixed definition in the standard, it is the only place where `/Threads` is referred to by position or by reference, and it can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` names. It is read, set and remove only: no viewer behaviour, no UI, no threads or beads made, so the entry is partial. Cost: each call of `read_thread_action` and `thread_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link, up to 33 for each reference chain followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), one for the catalog, one for the thread's dictionary and one for each bead walked, at most 256 beads, each once. A read of a link that is a thread action naming a thread and a bead therefore costs at most 1 + 33 + 1 + 33 + 1 + 256 = 325 reads, and a change check that sets one 325 + (1 + 33 + 1 + 256) = 616, so the real limits cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`). `set_thread_action` runs a change check and then reads the target again with an allowance of its own (at most 291 reads). At most 256 entries of `/Threads` are looked at, compared as references with no read of their own, and at most 256 bead references and one action name of at most 127 bytes are kept. Separate from that, whether the object is a structural one is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of an object that is a link. A read call makes one walk; a call that sets a link makes four (the read of the link's old action for `replaced`, the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of a thread action makes three, a removal of nothing makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the link's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the link's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. Not charged by this module: the tool finds the link with `Document::links`, the link list the other link tools use, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs` and is kept until the document changes; and nothing else reads. Argument checks of the tool allocate nothing of note. No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/thread_action.rs`, 355 lines), one new edit and one new tool (69 added lines in `crates/automation/src/links.rs` and 10 in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`), 319 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests and their helpers) and 226 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_thread_action.rs` (three tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_thread_action_is_read_with_its_thread_and_bead` (a thread named by reference and a bead by reference; positions as integers; an integer thread out of range or negative, a thread named by the reference of a bead and a name each reading no thread and so no bead; a bead of another thread not found; a thread action with no `/D`; a web action whose `/D` is ignored; a link with a `/Dest` and no action; a null `/A` and `/Dest`; the action behind a reference; an entry of `/Threads` that is not a reference not blocking the next one; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link, not settable and not a thread action), `a_thread_action_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/Action` dictionary with `/S /Thread` and the bead's reference, takes `/Dest` out, leaves the old indirect action as it was and keeps the rest of the link, and after a full save and reopen reads back thread 0 and bead 1; a target with no bead drops the old `/B`; a removal takes only `/A` out; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a link with a web action is `NoChange` for a removal and keeps it), `a_thread_action_is_refused_on_what_is_not_a_link_and_for_a_target_that_is_not_there` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a widget, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes; a page that is marked as a link is still refused and the reason names the page; a thread position with no entry, an entry that is not a reference, an entry that is a number, a bead the thread does not have, and a thread with no beads are each refused with the bytes equal; a catalog with no `/Threads` refuses), `what_a_thread_target_accepts` (255 and 256 for a thread, 255 and 256 for a bead), `every_read_a_thread_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (with exactly the 5 reads it takes the link, thread and bead are whole; with 4 the second bead is not paid for, with 3 the first bead is not, with 2 the thread's dictionary is not, with 1 the catalog is not, with 0 not even the link; an action behind a chain of 32 reference objects is followed with 35 reads (the link, the 33 reads of the chain and the catalog) and one behind 33 is not followed, and with 33 reads, one short of the link and the chain, both are capped; a change check that sets a bead is allowed with exactly 5 reads, refused naming the thread with 4 and with 1, and naming the link with 0; a removal needs only the read of the link) and `a_remote_or_title_named_thread_action_is_reported_and_not_resolved` (an action with an `/F` reads as remote with no thread, bead or title even with `/D` 0 and `/B` 0; a null `/F` is no file; a `/D` string is reported as `title`, not matched against the thread of that title, and with no bead; a title of 128 characters is cut at 127 and not `exact`; a `/D` that is a direct dictionary names no thread; `has_next` is true for a thread action and a web action with a `/Next` and false for a null one; a read cut short after the link is not settable) and `a_ring_of_beads_with_a_loop_and_a_long_one_are_walked_once_and_at_most_256_beads` (a loop of beads that does not lead back to the first is walked once for each bead, so a fourth bead is refused; the 256th bead of a ring of 300 is reached and written, the 257th is not read; the 256th entry of `/Threads` is looked at and the 257th is not, by reference or by position); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_thread_action.rs`, through `link_thread_action`: `a_thread_action_is_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (a read of a link with a web action; a set of the second thread whose `replaced` says the web action and no `/Next`, found in the saved file as `/S /Thread` naming the thread titled Second, and listed by `link_list` as `other` Thread; a set of the first thread and its second bead replacing that, the saved file naming the bead of width 20; a link with a `/Dest` losing it, with `replaced` saying a destination; a link with nothing getting an action; a removal that takes `/A` out, a second removal and a removal on a link with another kind of action both `changed` false with the other action kept and the saved bytes equal; five undo steps all labelled Set link article action, none added by the unchanged calls, the saved bytes after the last undo equal to the file before the first set, and five redos equal to the file before them), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (sixteen refusals by message: `remove` with `thread` or with `bead`, `bead` without `thread`, a `thread` of 0, 257 and the smallest integer, a `thread` that is a string, a `remove` that is a string, a `bead` of 0 and 257, a thread the document does not have, a bead the thread does not have (two cases), a link and a page that do not exist, and a link written in the page; the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a set and still reads; a removal where there is nothing to remove is `changed` false rather than a refusal). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep of `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 31, restoring after each, on the final code (the module and the tool were edited after earlier runs, and every mutation was run again after the last edit of the file it changes; only the docs and the commit text changed after): 31 failed a test and none survived.
