| 2026-11-21 | minor | Signature field lock, `signature_field_lock`: What: `signature_field_lock` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the lock of a signature field: its `/Lock` dictionary (ISO 32000-2 12.7.5.5, Table 236), which names the form fields that become read-only once the field is signed. `/Action` is `All` (every field of the form), `Include` (only the fields in `/Fields`) or `Exclude` (every field but those), and `/Fields` is an array of fully qualified field names as text strings, required for `Include` and `Exclude`. The signature validator already read these locks to judge changes made after signing (`sign.field-mdp`, `crates/sign/src/pdf/locks.rs`), but nothing could write one: a lock could only come from a file made elsewhere. Arguments: `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` lists it), and one of nothing (read), `action` (`all`, `include` or `exclude`) with `fields` (set), or `remove` true. A set replaces the field's `/Lock` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /SigFieldLock`, `/Action`, and `/Fields` for `include` and `exclude`; the PDF 2.0 `/P` entry of an old lock is not kept, and a read does not report `/P`); a removal takes `/Lock` out of the field's dictionary; an indirect `/Lock` dictionary that is replaced or removed is left in the file unreferenced. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a `field` that names no field, names a field that is not a signature field, or names more than one field (two fields sharing a fully qualified name; a read is refused too); `remove` combined with `action` or `fields`, `fields` without `action`, an unknown `action`, `fields` with `all`, an `include` or `exclude` with no names or more than 256, a name that is empty, over 127 characters or listed twice, and a name that is neither the fully qualified name of a field of the form nor the name of a field above others (`address` for `address.city`), which covers the ones below it, as the validator reads it; a field that holds a signature (its `/V` is not null, so a lock read from the signed revision cannot be edited afterwards); and an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page, compared by object number. A removal of a lock the field does not have is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetSignatureLock`, labelled Set signature field lock, now in `EDIT_LABELS`; a removal has the same label) that needs permission to modify the document, so a copy protected against changes refuses a write and still reads. The result is `field` and `lock`: `has_entry` (a `/Lock` that is not null), `present` (it is a dictionary after references), `action` (the name written in the dictionary, cut at 127 bytes and shown with replacement characters when not valid UTF-8, or null), `fields` (the strings among the first 256 entries of `/Fields`, after references, each cut at 127 characters, in file order), `count` (the entries of `/Fields`), `truncated` (more than 256, so the rest were not looked at), `not_names` (entries among the first 256 that are not strings), `exact` (the action and every listed name are whole; true when none is listed, and also for the read of a document that cannot be edited), `signed`, `settable` (the field is a dictionary, holds no signature and is not one of the structural objects above, and that walk was not cut short) and `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent; a set or removal on such a read is refused with its own message that names the allowance, not the structural objects); a call that writes also has `changed`. It does not check whether the document already holds signatures that the new lock or the change to this field would invalidate (a signature that locks this field, or a certification, can turn invalid, as the test with a signed file shows): no warning is given, and the tool description says to check `sign_list` afterwards. It does not apply the lock: PdfCraft does not make the locked fields read-only after a signature, it reads no FieldMDP transform in a signature's `/Reference`, `sign_document` writes no lock, and there is no Signed tab in Field Properties. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::signature_lock` (`read_signature_lock`, `signature_lock_change`, `set_signature_lock`, `LockSpec`, `LockAction`), run by the new edit, with `Document::signature_lock` and `Document::signature_lock_change`; `Gets`, `Spent` and `structural` of `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs` are now `pub(crate)` and `Gets` has a `charge`. Parity: `form.props-signed` (Field properties: signed, lock fields when signed) moves from planned to partial with the tool and the tests, because the tool is the lock a signature field's Signed tab sets (all fields, all but some, only some); `sign.lock-after-signing` (Lock document or fields after signing) stays planned, since it covers the lock applied as part of signing and the document-level lock, which nothing here does, and `sign.field-mdp` is unchanged. The FieldMDP row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which said none, though validation reads locks) now says what is there.  Why: `form.props-signed` was planned with nothing behind it. A headless agent preparing a form for signature could not say which fields a signature locks, and a lock could be seen only after the signature was made. A read and a set of the lock is the data the Signed tab of Field Properties edits, it has a clear definition in the standard, and the validator that enforces it is already here, so it can be tested against a real signature: the tests sign a file and see the signature stay intact or become invalid depending on the lock the tool wrote. It is read, set and remove only: no UI, no `/P`, no FieldMDP transform and no change to how a locked field behaves in the viewer, so the entry is partial.  Cost: A read has one allowance of 10,000 reads of the field's own, shared by everything the read makes and charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`: one for the field's object, up to 33 for each reference followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), and one more for each entry of `/Fields` looked at, charged before the entry is looked at. At most 256 entries are looked at, so the real limits cost at most 1 + 33 + 33 + 256 * 34 = 8,771 reads and cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). Separate from that, whether the field is a structural object is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of a field that is a dictionary and holds no signature, so a read makes one walk. A call that sets or removes a lock that is there makes three (the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of a lock that is not there makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the field's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the field's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. The list of the form's fields comes from the form reader under its own limits, which this tool does not charge; checking each name of a set against that list is a comparison in memory of up to 256 names against the fields, with no read. Strings are cut at 127 characters before they are kept and entries past 256 are counted and not looked at; no stream is decoded. A set allocates its at most 256 names, checked first, one dictionary and array of them, and in the tool a `format!` of one name and a dot for each name checked (at most 128 characters each) and the field's dictionary copy named above; none of these is charged against the allowance. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/signature_lock.rs`, 277 lines), one new edit and one new tool (82 lines in `crates/automation/src/forms.rs`), 247 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests and their helpers) and 287 in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_field_lock.rs` (six tests).  Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_signature_lock_is_read_with_its_action_and_names` (no lock; a null is no entry; a number is an entry and no lock; a lock and its `/Fields` behind references listing a string, a string behind a reference and a number, read as two names, a count of 3 and `not_names` 1; an `All` lock listing nothing; an action that is not a name not reported; a field with a `/V` that is a reference is signed and not settable and one whose `/V` is null is not signed), `a_signature_lock_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/SigFieldLock` dictionary in the field, leaves the old indirect one as it was, and after a full save and reopen reads back the action and three names, one with a non-ASCII letter; an `All` set replaces it and drops `/Fields`; a removal takes only `/Lock` out and keeps the field's other entries; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal), `a_signature_lock_is_refused_on_a_signed_field_and_on_structural_objects` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a signed field, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes, and a signed field says why), `every_read_a_signature_lock_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a field with its lock and list behind references and two entries, the second behind a reference: with exactly the 6 reads it takes it is whole, with 5 the second entry's reference is not paid for, with 4 the second entry is not looked at, with 3 the first entry is not, with 2 the list is not read, with 1 the lock is not read, with 0 not even the field; a lock behind a chain of 32 reference objects is followed with 34 reads and a chain of 33 is not, one read short being capped) and `a_read_that_spent_its_allowance_is_refused_with_its_own_reason` (a set and a removal with 0 or 1 reads are refused naming the allowance and with 2 the set is allowed), `a_signature_lock_action_is_cut_and_the_default_read_is_exact` (the default read is exact; an action name of 200 characters is cut at 127 bytes and the read is not exact) and `a_signature_lock_lists_256_names_cuts_long_ones_and_checks_what_it_writes` (exactly 256 entries not truncated, 259 listed as 256 with a count of 259 and `truncated`, the last listed being the 256th in file order; a name of 127 characters whole and one of 128 cut and not `exact`; what `LockSpec::new` accepts and refuses: 256 names but not 257, an empty `include` or `exclude`, names with `all`, a repeated name, an empty name, 127 characters but not 128); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_field_lock.rs`, through `signature_field_lock`: `a_lock_is_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (a field with none reads exactly as empty; an `include` set of two names read back and found in the saved file with `/Type /SigFieldLock`, the other signature field untouched; an `all` set replaces it; a removal takes it out, a second removal is `changed` false with the saved bytes equal; three labelled undo steps and a fourth with nothing to undo, the saved bytes equal to before the first, and a redo equal to the first set), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (fifteen refusals by message: `remove` with `action`, `remove` with `fields`, `fields` without `action`, an unknown action, `all` with names, `include` with no names and `exclude` with an empty list, a repeated name, a name that is no field and one that is only the start of a field's name, `fields` that is a string, `remove` that is a string, a missing field, a text field, and a `field` that is only the parent of a field's name; 257 names; the saved bytes equal and no undo step), `a_signed_field_is_read_but_not_edited_and_an_unsigned_one_still_is` (a file is made with `form_add_field`, a lock set that excludes `address.city`, and the field signed with a new digital ID: the signed field's lock is still read with `signed` true and `settable` false, a set and a removal on it are refused naming the signature and add no undo step; the unsigned field of the signed file is locked and saved incrementally, with both locks in the file; when the signature's lock also excludes that field the signature is still intact (status unknown, the signer being untrusted), and when it does not, locking that field makes the signature invalid, so the lock the tool writes is the one the validator enforces), `a_lock_of_many_names_lists_the_first_256_and_counts_the_rest` (a hand-made file whose lock has 300 entries, a number, a name of 128 characters and 298 short ones: 255 names listed, the long one cut at 127, the last listed being the 256th entry in file order, a count of 300, `truncated`, `not_names` 1, `exact` false), `a_name_two_fields_share_is_refused_rather_than_guessed` (a hand-made file with two signature fields named Dup: a read, a set and a removal are refused naming the name, nothing is written) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal and still reads). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist.  Mutations: 43, restoring after each, on the final code (the run was repeated after the last code edits; only the description text of the tool, the docs and the commit text changed after it): 43 failed a test and none survived. In the organize module, 29 (each run against the organize tests of the module): no charge for an entry, a null `/V` counted as signed, `signed` never set, the structural objects settable, `truncated` at 256, 257 entries looked at, `not_names` not counted, the exactness of a name ignored, a cut that is always whole, the cut at 128 characters, `all` with names accepted, an empty list accepted, 257 names accepted, an empty name accepted, a 128-character name accepted, a repeated name accepted, `/Type` not written, `/Fields` written for `All`, `Include` written as `Exclude`, a signed field not refused, an object that is not settable not refused, a removal of no lock counted as a change, `has_entry` always true, the action not read, `capped` never set, the removal doing nothing, a read that spent its allowance not refused with its own message, the exactness of the action ignored, and the default read not exact. In the tool and the edit, 14 (each run against the 6 automation tests): the permission check, redone as the edit moved to an arm that allows it (dropping its arm does not compile, the match being exhaustive), a field that is not a signature field accepted, a name shared by two fields accepted, a name that matches no field accepted, a name that is only an ancestor refused, `include` mapped to `exclude`, the text of the `remove` with `action` refusal changed, `fields` without `action` accepted, `changed` not reported, `settable` reported as `signed`, `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed, `not_names` always 0, and a removal of no lock reported as a change. Earlier, on a previous version of the code, some mutations survived and were fixed: a signed field not refused (a test now reads the reason), a name shared by two fields accepted, `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed and `not_names` always 0 (tests added: the shared name and the 300-entry lock), and the refusal of a read that spent its allowance, which was unreachable then and is now reached through `change_with` with a small allowance. One run of the last mutation reported a survivor because its test filter did not match the new test's name; the test was run by hand against the mutation and failed it, which is the result counted.  Not mutated: the values of the limits (256 is tested through the constant in the module tests and as a literal in the tool test, and 127 through the constants and in the tests; the allowance of 10,000 reads is tested only through `read_with` with small ones, and the real limits cannot reach it, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared with that module and covered by the chain test here and by that module's tests, not mutated for this tool), the cut-short walk of the page tree (a field is not settable when the walk was cut, which needs a tree over its limits), a field that is a stream (its dictionary is edited like a dictionary), `Document::signature_lock` and `Document::signature_lock_change` when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, the schema, and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts.  Not tested: a lock's `/P`, a lock or `/Fields` that is a stream, a field whose widget is a kid of the field (every field here has its widget merged into its dictionary, so a lock on the field object with a separate widget is not covered), FieldMDP transform references in a signature's `/Reference` (not read by this tool), a lock written by this tool read by Acrobat or another viewer, the user interface (none), and a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable).  Ran: `cargo fmt --check` (after `cargo fmt` had reformatted the new code); `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without the `-A clippy::collapsible_match` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 350 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 681 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Set signature field lock", which is English only).  Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root).  Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a lock this tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-21 | minor | Flaky tests, fixed clock: What: the `setup` of six automation integration test files (`article_threads`, `class_map`, `named_destinations`, `portfolio_collection`, `reference_xobjects`, `role_map`) builds its `Automation` with a fixed clock, `with_clock(|| 1_700_000_000)`, as `form_bulk` already did. No product code changed. Why: a save stamps `/ModDate` (and the matching XMP date) from the wall clock to the second, and these tests compare the bytes of two saves of the same document ('a call that changes nothing writes nothing', 'undone equals before'). When the two saves straddled a second boundary the bytes differed by one digit in the compressed object stream (length 227 against 226) and the test failed, once in a full run and again in one of 40 runs of the two binaries alone, in `portfolio_collection::the_collection_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone`, `reference_xobjects::a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` and one more; the temp directories are per test, so no shared path or global state was involved. Cost: none at run time; the saved files of these tests carry one fixed date.  Tested: reproduced before the fix (run 10 of 40 of `it portfolio_collection reference_xobjects --test-threads=8` failed on the byte comparison at `reference_xobjects.rs:183`); after the fix 60 runs of the seven byte-comparing files (those six and `form_bulk`) at 8 threads, none failed. No new test: the existing tests are the regression tests and cannot fail on a pinned clock.  Mutations, 1, restoring after: a clock that adds one second on every read in `reference_xobjects` and `portfolio_collection` made 5 of their 8 tests fail, so the byte comparisons do depend on the clock and the fixed one is what keeps them equal.  Not mutated: the other tests of the six files, which do not compare saves or are covered by the same one-line `setup`.  Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test -p pdfcraft-automation --no-fail-fast`; `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`.  Failed, not related: `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` fails in this sandbox, which runs as root (a read-only folder does not stop root), and fails the same on an unchanged tree.  Not run: `cargo xtask i18n-report`, `cargo xtask wasm`, the other crates' tests, the application (no user-facing text, no product code). Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-21 | minor | Portfolio collection, `portfolio_collection`: What: `portfolio_collection` (new, headless) reads the document's portfolio collection, the catalog's `/Collection` dictionary that makes a PDF open as a portfolio of its embedded files (ISO 32000-2 7.11.6 and 12.3.5), and, given `remove` true, takes that entry out of the catalog. The application already used `/Collection` for the view (`doc_info` reports a portfolio's initial document and view, `organize.portfolio-view`), but nothing read its `/Schema`, the columns of the file list, or could end the portfolio. It is read and remove only: it adds no collection, changes no column, reads no `/Sort`, `/Folders` or file specification `/CI`, and extracts no file, so `organize.portfolio-extract` stays planned and the tool is evidence for the partial `organize.portfolio-view`. The result is `collection`: `present` (the entry is a dictionary after references), `has_entry` (the catalog has a `/Collection` that is not null, which is what `remove` takes out, so a `/Collection` that is a number or a dangling reference is an entry and no portfolio), `view` (`/View` when it is a name written in the dictionary and not through a reference, cut at 127 bytes; the specification has D, T, H and C, details, tile, hidden and custom), `initial_document` (`/D` when it is a string written in the dictionary, cut at 127 characters), `initial_document_cut` (`/D` was cut), `count` (the entries of `/Schema` other than `/Type`), `truncated` (more than 256, so the first 256 are listed), `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent) and `fields`, in file order: `key` (cut at 127 bytes), `is_field` (the entry is a dictionary after references; when false the rest is null or default), `name` (`/N`, a string after references, cut at 127 characters), `subtype` (a name written in the field), `order` (an integer written in the field), `visible` (`/V` when a boolean is written in the field, true when there is none), `editable` (`/E` likewise, false when there is none) and `exact` (false when `key` or `name` was cut, or when the key is not valid UTF-8 and is shown with replacement characters; a cut `subtype` or `view` is not flagged). With `remove` true the result also has `removed`: true when the catalog had the entry and it is gone, false when it had none, which is no change: nothing is written and no undo step is added. `remove` is a boolean; `false`, a null and no value only read, and a string or a number is refused (`remove must be true or false`). A removal changes only the catalog: an indirect `/Collection` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced, and the embedded files, `/Names` and the pages are not touched, so the file becomes an ordinary PDF whose embedded files are its attachments. Because only the catalog is written, a `/Collection` that is the same object as the `/Pages` node or a page is read as written and removed from the catalog without the object being changed, so there is no `removable` flag and no refused removal. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveCollection`, labelled Remove portfolio collection, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`; with no catalog the edit fails with `OrganizeError::NoPageTree`, the error the other catalog edits use) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of a present entry is refused and the read still works. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::collection` (`read_collection`, `collection_removes`, `remove_collection`), run by the new edit, with `Document::collection` and `Document::collection_removes`. Parity: `organize.portfolio-extract` stays planned (its note says what is missing); `organize.portfolio-view`, already partial, gains the tool and the tests as tools and evidence and a note that `/Schema` is now read headless with no view built from it, so no entry changes status. The §7.11 row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which also wrongly said attachments cannot be added, deleted or described; `organize.attachments-add`, `-delete` and `-description` are shipped) and the portfolio row of `docs/file-format-parity.md` (which wrongly said there is no portfolio view; its header date is updated) now cite `organize.portfolio-view`. Why: `organize.portfolio-extract` is a planned P2 feature that extracts the files of a portfolio, and this slice does not do that, so it stays planned. What was missing next to it was a headless read of a portfolio's columns and a way to end the portfolio: an agent could not see which columns a portfolio defines or how its list is set up, and could not turn a portfolio into an ordinary file that shows its attachments. The Attachments panel of the application already lists and saves attached files (`view.attachments-panel`, tested by `crates/render/src/inspect/tests.rs::attachments_of_both_kinds_are_listed_and_extracted`); only the headless `attachment_*` tools do not save an attached file (they add, delete and describe). Extracting a portfolio's files headless would need a tool that saves an embedded file, a naming and overwrite policy and folders (`/Folders`), which is not small, so this slice is the read and the removal, which need no such model. `/Sort`, `/Folders` and `/CI` are not read because each needs its own model of a tree or of typed values. Cost: A read has one allowance of 100,000 reads, shared by everything the call reads (`/Collection`, `/Schema` and every column and name), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`; a column is listed only when all its reads were paid for, so the columns before the one that could not be read stay and that one is dropped whole. References are followed as `Document::resolve` follows them (the first object and up to 32 more, so at most 33 reads for one value, each charged), and each `/Schema` entry looked at costs one more read, charged before it is looked at. A column costs at most 1 + 33 (the entry behind a reference) + 33 (`/N` behind a reference) = 67, so with at most 256 columns listed a read is at most 33 + 33 + 256 * 67 = 17,218 reads: the real limits cannot spend the allowance of 100,000, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). The catalog's own read is not charged (one in `read_collection`, one in `collection_removes`). Counting the entries of `/Schema` walks a dictionary that is already in memory (twice: once to count, once to list) and makes no read, so it is not charged. `/View`, `/D`, `/Subtype`, `/O`, `/V` and `/E` are read as written and cost no read. At most 256 columns are kept, each with a key of at most 127 bytes, a name of at most 127 characters and a subtype of at most 127 bytes, so the strings are cut before they are copied; every column past 256 is counted and not kept, and no stream is decoded. A call without `remove` reads once; a call with `remove` reads the catalog in the tool, again in the edit, copies the catalog dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`, and reads once more for the answer. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/collection.rs`, 194 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 216 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (five tests and their helpers) and 237 in `crates/automation/tests/it/portfolio_collection.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_collection_is_read_with_its_view_first_file_and_columns` (a document with no `/Collection` reads as empty; a saved and reopened indirect collection with `/View` T and a `/D` string lists four `/Schema` entries with `/Type` not counted: a column written in the schema, one behind a reference whose `/N` is behind a reference and whose `/O` is 3, `/V` false and `/E` true, an entry that is a number (`is_field` false), and a dictionary whose `/N` is a number, `/V` and `/E` are integers and `/O` is a string, each with its key, name, subtype, order, `visible`, `editable` and `exact`; a `/View` and a `/D` behind references are not read; a `/Collection` that is a number or a dangling reference has an entry and is no portfolio, a null has no entry, and `collection_removes` follows `has_entry`), `removing_the_collection_takes_only_the_catalog_entry` (no entry: the saved bytes are equal; an indirect collection removed: the entry is gone, the page count is the same and a saved copy reads as no portfolio; a direct one removed and saved incrementally, reopened with none; a `/Collection` that is the `/Pages` node: the entry goes, the node keeps its `/Kids` and the pages stay), `the_collection_listing_is_bounded_and_counts_what_it_does_not_list` (exactly 256 columns not truncated; 300 listed as 256 with `truncated`, a count of 300 and the last listed one being the 256th in file order), `every_read_a_collection_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a collection and schema behind references and two columns, the second behind a reference with `/N` behind a reference: with exactly the 6 reads they take it is whole and not capped, with 5 the second column's `/N` read is not paid, so that whole column is dropped (one column listed) and it is capped, with 3 only the first column is listed, with 2 none, with 1 the collection is read and the schema is not, with 0 nothing is; a chain of 32 reference objects ends in the dictionary, which `Document::resolve` also reaches, and is paid for with 33 reads, one fewer being capped; a chain of 33 is not followed by either) and `a_collection_text_is_cut_at_127_characters` (127 characters whole, 128 cut and not `exact`, for a name and for a key, and `/D` cut at 127 with `initial_document_cut` set and a `/D` of 127 whole; a key that is not valid UTF-8 is not `exact`); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/portfolio_collection.rs`, through `portfolio_collection`: `the_collection_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with none reads exactly as empty; a file with a collection, three schema entries and an embedded file read exactly; `remove` false only reads and the saved bytes are equal; `remove` true with `removed` true, the saved file with no `/Collection` and its `/Names` still there, a second removal with `removed` false and the saved bytes equal and no undo step, undo with the label Remove portfolio collection and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal), `a_schema_of_many_columns_lists_the_first_256_and_counts_the_rest` (303 entries: 256 listed, a count of 303, `truncated` true, `capped` false, the 256th listed being the one in that position of the file; a direct `/Collection` with `/View` C and a `/D` of 128 characters read as `C`, 127 characters and `initial_document_cut` true, and one of 127 characters with it false), `a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with none, and one whose `/Collection` is a null, `removed` false with the saved bytes compared before and after and no undo step; a `/Collection` that is a number reads as an entry and no portfolio and is removed; a string and an integer refused with `remove must be true or false`, a null read, the saved bytes equal and no undo step), `a_collection_that_is_a_page_tree_node_is_read_but_only_the_catalog_changes` (a `/Collection` that is the `/Pages` node read as written, removed from the catalog, the saved file with the node and its `/Kids`) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_removal_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal of a present entry and still reads). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, 41, restoring after each, on the final code: 41 failed a test and none survived. In the organize module, 30 (each run against the 5 organize tests): no charge before the first read of a value, no charge in a hop, the hops one short and one long, no charge for an entry, `/Type` counted and `/Type` listed, `truncated` at 256 and one extra column listed, `visible` false by default, `/V` ignored and `/E` ignored, the exactness of a name ignored and of a key ignored, `has_entry` always true, a null counted as an entry by `collection_removes`, the removal doing nothing, `is_field` always true, `capped` never set, `/O` ignored, `/Subtype` ignored, `/N` not read, `/View` ignored, `/D` ignored, `present` set before the dictionary check, the text limit 128, the allowance not stopping a read (a charge that never fails), `initial_document_cut` inverted and never set, and the UTF-8 check of `shown` in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs` dropped (run against the 5 collection tests only).
