| 2026-11-21 | minor | Structure tree maps, guard against a map that is another live object: What: the structure tree's role map and class map (`role_map` #147, `class_map` #148) are changed where they are stored, and the one finder they share (`with_map` in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs`) refused only a `/RoleMap` or `/ClassMap` that is the catalog, the tree root or a stream, and a tree root that is the catalog; it now also refuses one that is the same object as the `/Pages` node or a node under it (an inner `/Pages` node or a page, found by walking `/Kids` from the `/Pages` node, each node put on the stack once, when it is first seen; a walk that sees more than 1,000,000 nodes, or scans more than 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries in all, before it finds the object answers that it is one, because it could not show it is not, while exactly 1,000,000 nodes and exactly 2,000,000 entries still answer by what was found), and, for a map, the tree root's other map (`/RoleMap` and `/ClassMap` that are one object are each refused), and a tree root that is the `/Pages` node or a node under it. Such a value is named in `unreadable` (`role_map` or `class_map` for a map, `struct_tree_root` for the tree root), reads as empty, and is never written: before this change a `/RoleMap` that was the `/Pages` node was read as a role map, so `role_map` with `role: "Kids"` and a `maps_to` would have written a name into the page tree's `Kids` (reasoned from the code and from the two tools' own text, not run before the change). This is a guard and no new feature: no tool, edit, argument, undo label or permission changed, and the parity statuses are unchanged (only the notes of `a11y.role-map` and `a11y.class-map` say which objects are refused). The refusal text of `role_map` for an unchangeable `/StructTreeRoot` or `/RoleMap` now names the objects (it says "is the same object as the catalog, the /Pages node, a page or inner /Pages node, or (for a map) the tree root or its other map, or the page tree has over 1000000 nodes and could not be checked"), and the descriptions of `role_map` and `class_map` say what is guarded and what is not. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::role_map::reaches_page_tree_node` (new, crate-private, with its two caps as arguments so that they can be tested, and `walk_page_tree`, which also returns the stack's peak for the tests), used by `with_map` for the tree root and for an indirect map. Why: both tools said in their own text that nothing refused a map that is another live dictionary, which let a file whose `/RoleMap` pointed at a page tree object have that object changed as a role map, with a tool call as the only thing needed; the fix belongs in the shared finder so that both maps and every later user of it are covered at once, and it is the next narrow, testable item after the two tools it guards. No parity entry was moved to a higher status, so no weighted number changed. Other candidates looked at and left: the `xmp-editor`, `change-page-size`, `split-naming`, `struct-tree-merge` and preflight entries (each too large for one reviewable slice), `organize.associated-files` (a duplicate of `core.pdf20-associated-files`, annotated in #148 and not touched), and the page labels, destinations, optional content and bookmark entries, none of which is smaller than this guard. Cost: nothing is walked for a document with no structure tree, a direct tree root or a direct map. A real tagged file has an indirect `/StructTreeRoot`, so every `with_map` walks the page tree at least once for the tree root, and a second time when the map is indirect too (the catalog, the tree root and the other map are compared first and save the walk when they match): so a read is one `with_map` (one or two walks), and a call that writes calls it four times (the tool's own check, the edit's check, the edit's find and the listing read back), so up to eight walks. A walk keeps a stack and a seen set of at most 1,000,001 references (a node is put on the stack once, when first seen, and the walk answers true as soon as it sees one more than the cap), scans at most 2,000,001 `/Kids` entries (each entry of a `/Kids` array is counted, a shared array once for each node that lists it), and makes one `Document::get` per node visited and one `Document::resolve` of its `/Kids`, followed as `Document::resolve` follows it; nothing is copied but the references. Before this was bounded, a shared `/Kids` array listed by the `/Pages` node and by each of its k kids made the stack about k * k / 2 entries and the scan k * k; now it stops at the scan cap. An ordinary file scans about one entry per node, so the caps are reached only by a tree of over a million nodes or by a hostile one, and such a file's maps are refused as unreadable. The time of a walk was not measured: it is linear in the nodes and entries up to the caps, so a document with a very large page tree pays for it up to eight times per writing call. The walk is not skipped for an object that looks as if it cannot be a page tree node (no `/Type`, no `/Kids`, no `/Parent`), because a hostile file can omit all three and the guard would not hold. A map also looks the other map's entry up (at most 33 gets, as `locate` does). No new dependency, asset, workspace crate or edit, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tools. 89 lines added and 11 removed in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs`, 4 and 3 in `class_map.rs` (text), 175 and 2 in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests, a fixture helper and the two refusal texts), 10 and 2 and 14 and 1 in the two automation test files, and the two tool descriptions and two parity notes (the `a11y.role-map` note also no longer lists the class map as missing, which #148 shipped). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_map_that_is_the_pages_node_an_inner_node_or_a_page_is_unreadable_and_never_written` (the fixture's `/Pages` node, its inner `/Pages` node and two pages, each as the tree root's `/RoleMap` alone and then its `/ClassMap` alone: the map named in `unreadable` and the other map not, nothing listed, `Kids`, `Parent` and `Type` refused for a role and not removable as a class, `role_map_changes` and `class_map_removes` false, the object's dictionary equal to what it was and the saved bytes equal; and a tree root that is the `/Pages` node, the inner node or a page: `struct_tree_root` named for both tools, a role refused, a class removal no change, the saved bytes equal and no `/RoleMap` written into the node), `a_role_map_and_a_class_map_that_are_one_object_are_unreadable_and_apart_they_are_not` (one dictionary as both maps: each named, nothing changed, bytes equal; two equal dictionaries as the two maps: each read, the role changed and the class removed independently) `the_page_tree_walk_ends_on_a_cycle_and_at_its_cap` (all five nodes found, objects outside the tree not, a node that lists the root and itself and a kid twice ends, `/Kids` behind a reference followed and a kid taken out no longer found, the node cap giving up at 2 and 4 nodes of 5 and finding it is not a node at 5, the entry cap giving up at 3 of 4 entries and answering by what was found at 4, and a document with no `/Pages` node answering false) and `a_shared_kids_array_cannot_make_the_page_tree_walk_hold_or_scan_a_square` (the `/Pages` node and each of 1000 kids listing one shared `/Kids` array: with room, the answer is by what was found and the stack's peak is at most 1000 entries, where pushing every entry scanned would give about 500,000; a scan cap one below the 1,001,000 entries scanned gives up and the cap itself does not; a node cap of 1000 gives up and 1001 does not; ten entries that are not references are counted: a cap of 9 gives up and of 10 does not; this is a counter and a peak, not a real exhaustion of memory); the two existing role map tests that compared the refusal text now compare the new text. In `crates/automation/tests/it/role_map.rs`, `a_tree_root_or_role_map_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` gained a `/RoleMap` that is the `/Pages` node, a page and the `/ClassMap` dictionary (the call refused with the new text, `unreadable` named, the unmapping of `Kids` no change, the saved bytes equal and no undo step), and in `crates/automation/tests/it/class_map.rs`, `an_unreadable_class_map_is_named_and_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` gained a `/ClassMap` that is the `/Pages` node, a page and the `/RoleMap` dictionary, with `Kids`, `Count`, `Parent`, `O` and `Placement` asked for (each `removed` false, the saved bytes equal and no undo step). Each guard mutation below failed at least one of these tests. Mutations, 14, restoring after each, on the final code: all 14 failed a test and none survived. In the walk: the scan cap removed, the scan cap compared with `>=` instead of `>`, entries counted only when they are references, a kid put on the stack without the seen check (the stack then grows as before the fix), the node cap removed, the node cap compared with `>=`, and the `/Pages` node as the target not answered true. In `with_map`: the guard for the tree root removed, the guard for the map's walk removed, the sibling guard removed, the sibling never looked up, the sibling's key swapped (it failed five tests), `/Kids` behind a reference not resolved, and a document with no `/Pages` node answering true. A first version of the guard (before the stack bound and the scan cap) was mutated with eleven mutations, ten caught and one equivalent survivor; its walk was replaced, so those results are not counted here. No permission arm or undo label changed. Not mutated: the walk's treatment of a `/Kids` entry that is not a reference, the 1,000,000 and 2,000,000 values of the caps (the caps are tested as arguments with small values, the constants are not mutated), the order of the comparisons (the catalog, the tree root and the sibling are compared before the walk, which saves a walk and changes no answer), the two refusal texts' wording, the tool descriptions and the parity notes (data and text the tests do not read), and the unchanged code of `locate`. Not tested: a page tree of more than 1,000,000 nodes or 2,000,000 entries (the caps are tested as arguments, and the shared array as a counter and a peak, not by exhausting memory), a `/Kids` entry that is a direct dictionary and not a reference, a page that is in no `/Kids` list (an orphan, which is not guarded: it is read and changed as a map), and any other dictionary a map or tree root might be (a `/ParentTree`, an attribute object, the document information dictionary, an annotation, a font), none of which is guarded and the two tool descriptions say so; the time of a walk on a large page tree. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match` (organize and engine together, then automation); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 317 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 659 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of the four with exit status 0). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the changed functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file after a tool call, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-21 | minor | Class map of the structure tree, `class_map`: What: `class_map` (new, headless) reads the structure tree's class map, the `/ClassMap` dictionary of the catalog's `/StructTreeRoot` (ISO 32000-2 14.7.5.4), which maps a class name to an attribute object or to an array of attribute objects, and, given `remove`, takes the entry of that class name away. It is the sibling of `role_map` (#147), and the accessibility tools of this application did not read it. It is read and remove only: it adds no class and changes no attribute object, because what an attribute object may hold depends on its owner (`/O`), which this tool does not model, and it checks no structure element's `/C`. The result is `class_map`: `has_structure_tree`, `count` (every entry), `truncated` (more than 256 entries, so the first 256 are listed), `entries` and `unreadable`. An entry is `class` (the key, cut at 127 bytes before it is copied), `kind` (what the value is after references: `dictionary`, `stream`, `array`, or `other` for anything else, including a null and a reference that ends in nothing), `items` (1 for a dictionary or a stream, the length of an array whatever its items are, 0 for `other`), `owners` (the `/O` name of the first 16 attribute objects, null for an item that is not a dictionary or a stream or has no `/O` that is a name, cut at 127 bytes) and `exact` (false when the class or a listed owner is not valid UTF-8 or is over 127 bytes, so that it is listed cut; a class over 127 bytes can be removed only by its full name, and one that is not valid UTF-8 cannot be removed at all). `unreadable` is `struct_tree_root` when `/StructTreeRoot` is there but is neither a dictionary nor null, is behind more than 33 references, or is the catalog itself, and `class_map` the same for `/ClassMap`, which is also unreadable when it is a stream, whose keys are the stream's and not classes, or is the catalog or the tree root itself; a stream's dictionary counts as a dictionary for `/StructTreeRoot` only. With `remove` the result also has `removed`: true when an entry with exactly that key was stored and is gone, false otherwise. Any string is looked up as it is, so a stored key that is empty, has a NUL byte or is over 127 bytes is taken away by its exact name, and a stored key that is not valid UTF-8 cannot be asked for. A class that is not stored, a `/ClassMap` or `/StructTreeRoot` that is absent (missing, null or dangling) and one that is unreadable as above are no change: `removed` is false, nothing is written, no undo step is added, and an unreadable value is named in `unreadable` and never replaced. A `remove` that is not a string is refused (`remove must be a string`), and a null is read as left out. A removal keeps the other entries, leaves an indirect attribute object the entry pointed at where it is (a direct one goes with the entry), and takes an emptied `/ClassMap` away (an indirect one is taken out of the tree root and its object is left unreferenced); a dictionary behind references is changed where it is. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveClassMapEntry`, labelled Remove class map entry, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of a stored entry is refused and the class map is still read. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::class_map` (`read_class_map`, `class_map_removes`, `remove_class_map_entry`), run by the new `Edit::RemoveClassMapEntry`, with `Document::class_map` and `Document::class_map_removes`. It reuses the role map's way of finding the tree root and the map: `with_map` in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs` now takes the key of the map (`RoleMap` or `ClassMap`), the write of an entry into or out of a map is extracted from `set_role_map` as `apply_to_map`, and `Where`, `locate` and `shown` are `pub(crate)`; `role_map` behaves as before (its ten tests and four tool tests pass unchanged). Parity `a11y.class-map` goes from planned to partial. No guard refuses a `/ClassMap` that is another live dictionary (the `/Pages` node, a page, the `/RoleMap` dictionary): it is read and changed as a class map, and `remove: "Kids"` on one that is the `/Pages` node would break the page tree. The same is true of `/RoleMap` in `role_map` (#147), whose tool text now says so. Also in this commit, the parity entry `organize.associated-files` (planned, no tool), which duplicates `core.pdf20-associated-files` (partial, crediting `af_list`, `af_add`, `af_remove` and `af_set_relationship`), keeps its status, tools and evidence and gets a note saying it duplicates that entry, which tracks the feature; merging or deleting it is left to the owner. No code or test changed for it. Why: `a11y.class-map` was a planned P3 feature with no tool, so an agent could not see which classes a tagged file defines, what each maps to or which attribute owners it uses, and could not take away a class that nothing uses or that a repair left behind; the role map next to it had just gained its tool (#147). Writing a class would need an attribute object of a shape that depends on its owner, with no safe small form, so the first slice is the read and the removal, which need no such model. The associated-files entry was left alone by #147, which noted it as a duplicate; it is annotated here and not moved to partial, because that would add half a feature to the weighted share with no new work, in another area and tier than the core entry, so merging or deleting it is left to the owner. Cost: Reading borrows the catalog, the tree root and the class map where they are direct; only a reference is resolved, up to 33 gets as `Document::resolve` does, and a class name or an owner is cut at 127 bytes before it is copied. The listing takes at most 256 entries. For each entry the value is resolved (at most 33 gets); a dictionary or a stream then resolves its `/O` (at most 33 more), and an array is measured by its length and then its first 16 items are each resolved (at most 33 gets) and each of their `/O` resolved (at most 33 gets), so an entry costs at most 33 + 16 * 66 = 1089 gets and the listing at most 256 * 1089 = 278784 gets, with at most 4096 owner names and 256 class names copied. The count of all entries is the length of the dictionary. A call that removes nothing looks one key up and reads the listing once. A call that removes looks the key up in the tool and again in the edit, finds where the tree root and the map are once more, copies the dictionary it changes once (and the tree root again when an emptied indirect `/ClassMap` is taken out), and reads the listing once for the answer. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/class_map.rs`, 135 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 33 added and 13 removed lines in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs`, 401 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (eight tests and their helpers) and 212 in `crates/automation/tests/it/class_map.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_class_map_is_listed_with_what_each_entry_maps_to` (a document with no tree and one with a tree and no map read as empty; a saved and reopened map lists a direct dictionary, one behind two references, a stream, an array of six items (a dictionary, one with its `/O` behind a reference, one with no `/O`, one whose `/O` is a number, a number and a dangling reference), an empty array, a number, a null and a dangling reference, each with its kind, items and owners, and reading changes no byte), `removing_a_class_map_entry_keeps_the_rest_and_the_last_takes_the_dictionary` (one entry of three removed, saved and reopened it is still gone, a name of another case or not stored is not offered for removal and removing the one already gone changes no byte, the last takes the `/ClassMap` and keeps the rest of the tree root, and a role of the same name is left in the role map), `a_class_map_is_changed_where_it_is_through_references_and_in_a_direct_tree_root` (a tree root behind two references, a map behind two more and an entry behind one: read, changed where it is with the catalog and the tree root still holding their references and the attribute object left, an emptied indirect map taken out of the tree root; a tree root written in the catalog with a direct and then an indirect map; a tree root that is a stream), `a_class_map_that_is_a_stream_the_catalog_or_the_tree_root_is_unreadable_and_never_written` (a stream map lists no key and its keys cannot be removed, a map that is the tree root and one that is the catalog are unreadable with `/K` and `/Pages` kept, and a tree root that is the catalog is unreadable, each with the saved bytes equal), `a_tree_root_or_class_map_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_and_never_replaced` (four non-dictionaries each as the tree root and as the map, named and the saved bytes equal; a null and a dangling map and tree root absent and nothing unreadable), `a_stored_class_goes_by_its_exact_name_whatever_it_is_and_one_that_is_not_stored_is_no_change` (stored keys of 200 bytes, empty and with a NUL byte taken away by their exact names, the 127-byte cut of the long key, the lossy text of a key that is not UTF-8, another case and a trailing space asked for and no change in the full and the incremental save, the key that is not UTF-8 left and listed not exact), `the_references_followed_are_those_of_document_resolve_for_the_class_map` (chains of 33 and 34 references for the map, the tree root, an entry's value, an array item and an owner, the first two compared with `Document::resolve`) and `the_class_listing_is_bounded_names_are_cut_and_an_array_lists_only_its_first_owners` (300 entries listed as 256 with `truncated`, an entry past the 256th removed by its name, exactly 256 not truncated, an array of 1000 items counted as 1000 with 16 owners listed, exactly 16 items, an owner of 200 bytes cut at 127 and not exact, a name with a space, a slash, brackets, parentheses and a number sign saved and read back); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/class_map.rs`, through `class_map`: `the_class_map_is_read_an_entry_is_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with six classes read exactly, one removed with `removed` true, the saved file's map, undo with the label Remove class map entry and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal, four calls for a name that is gone, of another case, never stored or empty each with `removed` false and no new step, the rest removed one step each, the emptied map and the key gone from the tree root, five undos), `a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with no tree, a number and an array as `remove` refused with `remove must be a string`, a null read, the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `an_unreadable_class_map_is_named_and_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (a map that is a number, a stream, the tree root and the catalog each named in `unreadable`, five keys asked for with `removed` false, the saved bytes equal and no undo step; a copy protected against changes refuses the removal of a stored entry, still reads, and an entry that is not stored is no change there). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, 30, restoring after each, on the final code: 29 failed a test and 1 survived. In the organize module, 22: never reading the owner, not following a reference for the owner, reading a stream as `other`, reporting an array's length as 1, the owner cap at 17 and at 15, no cap on the listed entries, `truncated` always false and true at exactly 256, `exact` ignoring the owners, not cutting a class name, the removal check always true, a stored empty key that cannot be removed, reading the role map's key, taking an emptied map out of the tree root by the wrong key, naming `role_map` instead of `class_map` as unreadable, not naming `struct_tree_root`, dropping a dictionary's owner, not following a reference for an array item and for an entry's value, and `has_structure_tree` always true: each of these 21 failed one or more of the eight organize tests, and the 22nd, removing the check that the entry is stored before `remove_class_map_entry` writes, survived: on a readable map the edit then finds nothing to take away and writes a dictionary equal to the stored one, which the document does not count as a change, and a map that is unreadable is not handed to `apply_to_map` by anything else but that check, so it is a guard whose effect no test sees (the tests of an unreadable map and of a name that is not stored, in the full and the incremental save, pass with and without it). Removing the permission arm instead did not compile (the match is exhaustive) and is not counted. Not mutated: the boundary at exactly 127 bytes of a listed name (200 and 127 are tested, a 127-byte name is not mutated at its edge), the `kind` of a dictionary taken alone against a stream (a stream read as `other` was mutated), the 0 items of `other`, a null or dangling `/ClassMap` or tree root as absent (tested, not mutated), the reference hops of `locate` for this map (the 32 further references are unchanged code that the role map's mutations covered), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list), the edit's scope for the history (the default scope is used, as for `SetRoleMap`), the `Document::class_map` and `Document::class_map_removes` defaults when a document has no editor, the schema, and the associated-files entry (a data file that the parity checker reads, not code). Not tested: an owner above the ASCII range that is valid UTF-8 (the owners are ASCII), an array item that is itself an array (it is listed with a null owner by the same code as a number), an owner of a stream that is behind references (a dictionary's is tested), the class map's use by any viewer, that a structure element's `/C` names a class that is stored, and a `/ClassMap` that is another live dictionary: one that is the `/Pages` node, a page or the `/RoleMap` dictionary is read and changed as a class map and nothing refuses it, so `remove: "Kids"` on the `/Pages` node would break the page tree (the same holds for `/RoleMap` in `role_map`). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match` (organize and engine together, then automation); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 313 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 659 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of the four with exit status 0). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a class map after the tool took an entry away, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-20 | minor | Role map of the structure tree, `role_map`: What: `role_map` (new, headless) reads and changes one entry at a time of the structure tree's role map, the `/RoleMap` dictionary of the catalog's `/StructTreeRoot` (ISO 32000-2 14.7.4.2), which maps a structure type that a file made up to another type and which the accessibility tools of this application (`bookmark_from_structure`, `accessibility_tags`, the reading order and the checks) already follow but nothing could write. With `role` and `maps_to` together it maps `role` to the name `maps_to`; an empty `maps_to` takes the entry away; with neither it only reads, and one without the other is refused (`role needs maps_to (an empty string takes the entry away)`, `maps_to needs role`). A name is 1 to 127 bytes with no NUL byte, which is this tool's limit and not the file format's (`a role must not contain a NUL byte`, `a role must be 1 to 127 bytes, not 128 bytes`). A role that would map to itself (`the role A cannot map to itself`) or to a name whose entries lead back to the role within 64 steps (`B already leads back to D, so mapping D to it would make a loop`) is refused; that refusal and the 64 steps are this tool's rule, not the file format's, and a loop longer than 64 steps is not seen. A document with no structure tree is refused too (`the document has no structure tree, so there is no role map to change`). Taking away an entry checks the role as a new mapping does when the entry is not stored (a role that is empty, has a NUL byte or is over 127 bytes is refused, and a good name that is not stored is no change), but a stored key is taken away whatever it is, so that an entry this tool could not write can be cleaned up. A stored entry sent back as it is, including one that is a loop or has a name over 127 bytes, is accepted and changes nothing, and so is taking away an entry that is not there; a call that changes nothing adds no undo step, and one change is one undo step (`Edit::SetRoleMap`, labelled Change role map, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. The result is `role_map`: `has_structure_tree`, `count` (every entry), `truncated` (more than 256 entries, so the first 256 are listed), `entries` (`role`; `maps_to`, null when the value is not a name; `exact`, false when a name is not valid UTF-8 or is over 127 bytes and so is listed cut and cannot be asked for; `loops`, true when following the names from `maps_to` gets back to `role` within 64 steps) and `unreadable` (`struct_tree_root` when `/StructTreeRoot` is there but is neither a dictionary nor null, is behind more than 33 references, or is the catalog itself; `role_map` the same for `/RoleMap`, which is also unreadable when it is a stream, whose keys are the stream's and not roles, or is the catalog or the tree root itself, since editing it would change `/Pages` or `/K`). References are followed as `Document::resolve` follows them (the object and 32 more) for `/StructTreeRoot`, for `/RoleMap` and for the value of an entry, and a stream's dictionary counts as a dictionary for `/StructTreeRoot` only; a test compares the count with `Document::resolve` at 33 and 34 references. A `/StructTreeRoot` or `/RoleMap` that is unreadable as above is refused with `the structure tree's /StructTreeRoot or /RoleMap cannot be changed (it is not a dictionary, is behind more than 33 references, is a stream role map, or is the catalog or the tree root itself), so it was left as it is` and is never replaced; a null or dangling `/RoleMap` counts as absent and an entry starts a dictionary; a dictionary behind references is changed where it is; other entries are kept and an emptied `/RoleMap` goes (an indirect one is taken out of the tree root and its object is left unreferenced); a stored value that is not a name is listed with no target and replaced by a name asked for. Nothing is written by a refusal: a name of the wrong form (also for a removal of an entry that is not stored), a loop, no tree, a tree root or role map as above, or an argument that is not a string. The tool does not check that the role is used by the tree, that `maps_to` is a standard type, or that the role is not itself a standard type, and it edits no `/RoleMapNS` of a PDF 2.0 namespace (`ns_list` reads those) and no class map. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::role_map` (`read_role_map`, `set_role_map`, `role_map_changes`), run by the new `Edit::SetRoleMap`, with `Document::role_map` and `Document::role_map_changes`; the module reuses `print_presets::Held` and `held`. Parity `a11y.role-map` goes from planned to partial. Why: `a11y.role-map` was a planned P2 feature with no tool, so an agent could see through the accessibility tools that a type the file made up was or was not followed to a standard one, but could not map it, change the mapping or remove it, and a file whose custom types need one had to be edited by hand; the end-to-end test shows `bookmark_from_structure` finding a heading only after the tool maps its type. #146 set aside `organize.associated-files`, `organize.xmp-editor`, `organize.change-page-size`, `organize.split-naming`, `organize.struct-tree-merge` and the preflight family as bigger; they were looked at again from their entries and left: none was prototyped, and each needs more than one dictionary and one name (an editor and a schema for the XMP, new geometry and content scaling for the page size, naming rules and collision handling for splitting, a merge of two trees for combining). `organize.associated-files` is a duplicate of `core.pdf20-associated-files`, which already credits `af_list`, `af_add`, `af_remove` and `af_set_relationship` and is partial, while the organize entry still says planned with no tool; that entry was not changed here. This one was the smallest open parity entry that was unblocked and headless-testable: no hardware, licence, server, owner decision or renderer work, one dictionary of names, and the engine already had the edit, undo and permission machinery of `doc_base_url` (#146) and `doc_print_presets` (#145). Cost: Reading borrows the catalog, the tree root and the role map where they are direct; only a reference is resolved, up to 33 gets as `Document::resolve` does, and a name is cut at 127 bytes before it is copied, so a long name in the file is never copied whole. The listing takes at most 256 entries, and for each one the `loops` flag follows at most 64 names, so at most 16384 lookups in the same dictionary, each of which resolves the value it finds and so follows up to 33 gets when that value is behind references; the count of all entries is the length of the dictionary. A call that changes nothing looks one entry up; one that changes something looks it up again in the edit, follows at most 64 names (lookups as above) for a new mapping, and reads the listing once for the answer. Every change goes through `Document::update_dict`, which copies the dictionary it changes once (and the tree root again when an emptied `/RoleMap` is taken out), as every catalog edit does. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/role_map.rs`, 333 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 393 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (ten tests and their helpers) and 268 in `crates/automation/tests/it/role_map.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_role_map_entry_round_trips_through_a_save_and_removing_the_last_takes_the_dictionary` (two entries set, saved and reopened, the file holds a name, a new target replaces in place, one removed leaves the other, the last takes the `/RoleMap` and keeps the rest of the tree root), `a_role_map_is_changed_where_it_is_through_references_and_in_a_direct_tree_root` (a tree root behind two references, a role map behind two more and an entry's value behind one: read, changed where it is with the catalog and the tree root still holding their references, an emptied indirect map taken out of the tree root; a tree root written in the catalog with a direct and then an indirect map; a tree root that is a stream, read and changed by its dictionary), `a_role_map_that_is_a_stream_the_catalog_or_the_tree_root_is_unreadable_and_never_written` (a `/RoleMap` that is a stream lists no key and is refused with the exact message, a `/RoleMap` that is the tree root and one that is the catalog are unreadable and refused with `/Pages` kept, and a tree root that is the catalog is unreadable and refused, each with the saved bytes equal), `taking_away_a_role_that_is_not_stored_needs_a_name_the_tool_writes_but_a_stored_key_goes_whatever_it_is` (a removal of an unstored role that is empty, has a NUL byte or has 128 bytes refused with the exact message and nothing written, a good unstored name no change, and stored keys of 200 bytes, empty and with a NUL byte taken away by their exact names), `a_tree_root_or_role_map_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_and_never_replaced` (four non-dictionaries each as the tree root and as the role map: named, the exact refusal, taking away an absent entry changing no byte), `a_role_map_change_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` (nine refused changes with the exact messages (a NUL byte named as such) and the saved bytes equal, an empty target, a document with no tree, and the loop boundary: a loop that is seen after exactly 64 names and one that needs 65), `a_stored_entry_sent_back_is_no_change_and_a_value_that_is_not_a_name_is_listed_and_replaced` (a stored loop and a stored name of 130 bytes sent back change no byte, a stored loop is listed with `loops` and a long name with `exact` false and cut at 127 bytes, a different target for a loop is checked, values that are a number, a string and a null are listed with no target and replaced or removed), `the_references_followed_are_those_of_document_resolve` (chains of 33 and 34 references for the role map, the tree root and an entry's value, each compared with `Document::resolve` for the role map and the tree root), `a_null_or_dangling_role_map_is_absent_and_an_entry_starts_a_dictionary` (a null and a dangling `/RoleMap`, and a null and a dangling tree root) and `the_listing_is_bounded_names_are_cut_and_not_exact_and_names_that_need_escaping_survive_a_save` (300 entries listed as 256 with `truncated`, a key that is not UTF-8 listed lossily and not exact, a name with a space, a slash, brackets, parentheses and a number sign saved and read back); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/role_map.rs`, through `role_map`: `an_entry_is_set_read_saved_followed_by_the_readers_undone_and_redone` (a tagged file whose `Heading` type is not a heading until the tool maps it to `H1`: `bookmark_from_structure` does not list `Report` before and does after, the call that asks for what is stored adds no undo step, the saved file's own name, undo with the label Change role map, redo with the same saved bytes, undo and the reader no longer following it, an empty `maps_to` removing the entry and the dictionary), `a_refused_call_writes_nothing_and_adds_no_undo_step` (thirteen refused calls with the exact messages, three of them removals with a role that is empty, has a NUL byte or has 128 bytes, a document with no tree, the saved bytes before and after equal, no undo step, and a null `role` and `maps_to` read as left out), `a_stored_loop_is_listed_and_sent_back_unchanged_and_a_value_that_is_not_a_name_is_listed` (a stored loop and a number listed, the loop sent back accepted with no undo step and the saved bytes equal, a new loop refused, a number replaced by a name) and `a_tree_root_or_role_map_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (`/RoleMap 7` refused with the exact message, named `role_map`, the saved bytes equal, and a copy protected against changes refuses and still reads). None of them could fail before the change, because the tool and the module did not exist. Mutations, 38, restoring after each, on the final code: 34 in the organize module, each of which failed an organize test, and 4 more in the tool and engine. In the organize module: removing the NUL check, the length check and the empty check each failed `a_role_map_change_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` and `taking_away_a_role_that_is_not_stored_needs_a_name_the_tool_writes_but_a_stored_key_goes_whatever_it_is`; removing the self check and the loop refusal each failed the first of those, and so did changing the 64 steps to 63 and to 65; changing the 32 further references to 31 and to 33 each failed `a_tree_root_or_role_map_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_and_never_replaced`, `the_references_followed_are_those_of_document_resolve` and `a_role_map_that_is_a_stream_the_catalog_or_the_tree_root_is_unreadable_and_never_written`; reporting a change when the same name is stored, treating a value that is not a name as the same name, and checking the names before comparing with the stored entry each failed `a_stored_entry_sent_back_is_no_change_and_a_value_that_is_not_a_name_is_listed_and_replaced`; allowing a change in a document with no tree failed `a_null_or_dangling_role_map_is_absent_and_an_entry_starts_a_dictionary` and `a_role_map_change_that_is_refused_writes_nothing`; replacing a tree root or role map that is not a dictionary instead of refusing it failed the not-a-dictionary, references and stream tests; keeping an emptied indirect map failed `a_role_map_is_changed_where_it_is_through_references_and_in_a_direct_tree_root` and keeping an emptied direct one failed the round-trip test; accepting a stream as a role map, letting a role map be the catalog, letting a role map be the tree root and letting a tree root be the catalog each failed `a_role_map_that_is_a_stream_the_catalog_or_the_tree_root_is_unreadable_and_never_written`; reading a null as not absent failed `a_null_or_dangling_role_map_is_absent_and_an_entry_starts_a_dictionary`; removing the cap on listed entries, reporting `truncated` as false and listing `exact` for a name that is not UTF-8 each failed `the_listing_is_bounded_names_are_cut_and_not_exact_and_names_that_need_escaping_survive_a_save`; not cutting a long name and listing `exact` for a long name each failed the stored-entry test; reading an entry's value without following references failed the where-it-is test and the references test; reporting `loops` as false failed the stored-entry test; not writing into a tree root written in the catalog failed the where-it-is test; not naming `role_map` or `struct_tree_root` as unreadable each failed the not-a-dictionary, references and stream tests; counting the removal of an unstored good role as a change, not checking the role of that removal, and checking the key of the removal of a stored entry each failed `taking_away_a_role_that_is_not_stored_needs_a_name_the_tool_writes_but_a_stored_key_goes_whatever_it_is`. In the tool and engine: applying the edit even when nothing changes failed all four tool tests, letting any holder change the role map (an early `Ok(())` for `Edit::SetRoleMap` in `check_permission`) failed the tool's modify-restriction test, renaming the undo label failed the set-and-undo test, and writing an empty `maps_to` as an empty name instead of removing the entry failed three tool tests. Not mutated: the boundary at exactly 127 bytes of a written name (128 is refused in a test, but no test writes exactly 127), the order in which a NUL byte and a length are reported when both apply, the boundary at exactly 256 entries (300 are tested), the `first == role` branch of the loop walk taken alone (the self check of `set_role_map` was mutated), whether a loop is seen when a link of the chain is a value behind a reference (reading a value without following references was mutated for the listing only), a direct tree root whose indirect role map is emptied (it is tested, but the code that takes the entry out of a direct root was not mutated separately), the tool's mapping of an empty string to a removal taken alone against the `role needs maps_to` refusal, the `Document::role_map` and `Document::role_map_changes` defaults when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list), the edit's scope for the history (the default scope is used, as for `SetBaseUrl`), and the schema's `maxLength` and `minLength`. Not tested: a name above the ASCII range that is valid UTF-8 (the written names are ASCII), that the `/RoleMap` dictionary is not the tree root or the catalog itself (one that refers to either is read and changed there, and nothing refuses it), a role map in which a type is also a standard type, a loop longer than 64 steps that the tool then writes (a loop of 66 names is written in a test and not seen), a `/StructTreeRoot` that is a stream and a `/RoleMap` that refers to the page tree or another object that is not a tree (only the catalog, the tree root and a stream are refused), and the role map's use by a viewer other than PdfCraft. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match` (organize and engine together, then automation); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 305 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 656 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of the four with exit status 0). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a role map the tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-20 | minor | Base URL for relative links, `doc_base_url`: What: `doc_base_url` (new, headless) reads and sets the base URL that a relative web address of a link is resolved against, which ISO 32000-2 Table 211 keeps as `/Base` in the catalog's `/URI` dictionary and which `link_list` and the viewer already read (`pdfcraft_render::resolve_uri`) but nothing could write. `base` must start with `http://` or `https://` (in any case) followed by a letter, a digit or `[`, be printable ASCII with no space, and be at most 8192 bytes, the length the viewer reads a `/Base` up to (a test compares the two constants); that limit is this tool's, not the specification's. The address is not parsed (the organize crate has no URL parser), so some that links cannot be resolved against, such as `https://a:99999/`, are accepted, and `links_use_it` says whether a relative address is resolved against the stored base, as `link_list` does. An empty `base` takes the base away, and leaving `base` out only reads. A stored base sent back as it is is accepted and changes nothing even when it would be refused as a new value (`ftp://...`); a different one is checked. Other entries of `/URI` are kept and an emptied `/URI` goes. A call that asks for what is stored is not an edit and adds no undo step; one change is one undo step (`Edit::SetBaseUrl`, labelled Change base URL, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. The result is `base` (null for none), `links_use_it` and `unreadable`: `base` when the stored `/Base` is there but is not a string of at most 8192 bytes of UTF-8, and `uri` when the catalog's `/URI` is neither a dictionary nor null, or is behind more than 128 references. References are followed as far as the viewer's reader follows them, for `/URI` and for `/Base`: up to 128, and a stream's dictionary counts as a dictionary. An unreadable `/Base` is read as none and is left as it is unless a base is asked for, because only a value that differs from what is read is written. A `/URI` that is neither a dictionary nor null, or is behind more than 128 references, is refused with `the document's /URI is not a dictionary, or is behind more than 128 references, so it was left as it is` and is never replaced; a null or dangling `/URI` counts as absent and a base starts a dictionary; a dictionary behind references is changed where it is. A different base refused for a base that is not a web address of that form, has a space or a non-ASCII byte, or is over 8192 bytes, a `base` that is not a string, or a `/URI` as above writes nothing. It stores the base only: the Document Properties window has no field for it. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::base_url` (`read_base_url`, `set_base_url`, `check_base_url`), run by the new `Edit::SetBaseUrl`, and `Document::base_url`; `print_presets::Held` and `held` became `pub(crate)` to be shared. Parity `organize.base-url` goes from planned to partial. Why: `organize.base-url` was a planned P3 feature with no tool, so an agent could see a document's base through `link_list` (`resolved_url`) but could not set it, fix it or remove it, and a file whose relative links need one had to be edited by hand. It is the smallest open parity entry that was unblocked and could be tested headlessly: no hardware, licence, server, owner decision or renderer work (the viewer side already existed), one string in one catalog dictionary, and the engine already had the edit, undo and permission machinery that `doc_print_presets` (#145) uses. The other open entries that are headless-testable are larger (`organize.associated-files`, `organize.xmp-editor`, `organize.change-page-size`, `organize.split-naming`) or need design decisions. Cost: Reading borrows the catalog and the `/URI` dictionary: the `/Base` string is measured before it is looked at, so one longer than 8192 bytes is unreadable from its length and never copied, and one that is read is copied once (at most 8192 bytes). A direct `/Base` is borrowed; only a reference is resolved, up to 128 hops, for `/Base` and for `/URI`. The tool reads once when nothing changes and twice when it changes something, and `links_use_it` parses the base as a URL once per call. Every change goes through `Document::update_dict`, which copies the catalog (or the referenced dictionary) once, as every catalog edit does. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/base_url.rs`, 207 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 219 lines of tests in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` and 243 in `crates/automation/tests/it/base_url.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `base_url_round_trips_through_a_save_and_none_removes_the_entry` (set, saved, reopened and read, with the exact string the file holds; replaced; none removes the entry with the empty `/URI`), `a_base_url_beside_other_entries_and_through_references` (a `/URI` behind a chain of two references with `/Base` behind another: read, changed where it is with the catalog still holding the reference, `/IsMap` kept; none leaves the other entry and the dictionary; an emptied direct and an emptied indirect dictionary go from the catalog), `a_base_url_that_cannot_be_read_is_named_kept_and_replaced_only_by_a_real_value` (a number, a name, an array, a string of 8193 bytes and a string that is not UTF-8 are each named `base`; none changes no byte; a real base replaces it; a base of exactly 8192 bytes is written and read), `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` (eleven bases, each with the exact message and the saved bytes equal; a stored `ftp://` base sent back unchanged is accepted with no byte changed and a different `ftp://` one refused; four `/URI` values that are not dictionaries refused with the exact message, left, and asked for none without a change), `a_null_dangling_or_endless_uri_is_read_as_the_tool_describes` (a null and a reference to nothing are absent and a base starts a dictionary; a `/URI` and a `/Base` each behind 100 references are read and the dictionary at the end is the one changed; a `/URI` behind 200 is `uri`, refused with the exact message and left, and a `/Base` behind 200 is `base`), `a_uri_that_is_a_stream_counts_by_its_dictionary_like_the_viewers_reader` (a stream's dictionary is read and changed, its other entry kept) and `asking_for_the_stored_base_url_changes_no_byte`; and in `crates/automation/tests/it/base_url.rs`, through `doc_base_url`: `a_base_is_set_read_saved_used_by_links_undone_and_redone` (a link `page.html` has no `resolved_url` until the base is set, then resolves to `https://example.com/docs/page.html` through `link_list`, a call that asks for what is there adds no undo step, the saved file's own string, undo with the label Change base URL and `link_list` without it again, redo, and an empty string removing the dictionary), `a_refused_call_writes_nothing_and_adds_no_undo_step` (eight refused values, each with the exact message, the base still none, the saved bytes before and after equal, no undo step, and a null `base` read as left out), `a_stored_base_that_cannot_be_read_is_named_and_kept_through_none` (a stored `/Base 42` named, kept through an empty string with no undo step, replaced by a real base with `/IsMap` kept), `a_uri_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_refused_and_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (`/URI 7` refused with the exact message, named `uri`, the saved bytes equal, and a copy protected against changes refuses and still reads), `a_base_that_links_cannot_use_is_reported_as_not_used` (a stored `ftp://` base is read and `links_use_it` is false), `a_stored_base_sent_back_is_accepted_and_the_limit_is_the_viewers` (`MAX_BASE_URL_BYTES` equals `pdfcraft_render::MAX_URI_BASE_BYTES`; a stored `ftp://` base sent back is accepted with no undo step and the saved bytes equal, a different one refused; `https://a:99999/` is written and `links_use_it` is false) and `a_uri_that_is_a_stream_is_read_like_link_list_reads_it` (a file whose `/URI` is a stream and whose `/Base` is a reference: `link_list` resolves `p.html` to `https://stream.example/d/p.html` and the tool reads the same base). None of them could fail before the change, because the tool and the module did not exist. Mutations, 23, restoring after each, on the final code. In the organize module: removing the ASCII check, removing the host check and removing the length check each failed `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing`; removing the early return when the base equals what is read failed `a_base_url_that_cannot_be_read_is_named_kept_and_replaced_only_by_a_real_value` and `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing`; checking a base before comparing it with the stored one failed `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing`; removing the length cap on reading and reading a non-UTF-8 string lossily each failed the unreadable test; replacing a `/URI` that is not a dictionary instead of refusing it failed `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` and `a_null_dangling_or_endless_uri_is_read_as_the_tool_describes`; changing the reference cap from 128 to 256 and to 32 each failed that last test and `a_base_url_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` (which shows the cap in its message); refusing a stream's dictionary failed `a_uri_that_is_a_stream_counts_by_its_dictionary_like_the_viewers_reader`; not following a `/Base` that is a reference failed `a_null_dangling_or_endless_uri_is_read_as_the_tool_describes` and `a_base_url_beside_other_entries_and_through_references`; reading a null `/URI` and reading a reference to nothing as another object each failed `a_null_dangling_or_endless_uri_is_read_as_the_tool_describes`; keeping an emptied direct `/URI` failed `a_base_url_beside_other_entries_and_through_references` and `base_url_round_trips_through_a_save_and_none_removes_the_entry`; not taking an emptied indirect `/URI` out of the catalog failed `a_base_url_beside_other_entries_and_through_references`; writing an empty string instead of removing `/Base` failed those same two. In the tool and engine: applying the edit even when nothing differs failed `a_stored_base_that_cannot_be_read_is_named_and_kept_through_none`, `a_stored_base_sent_back_is_accepted_and_the_limit_is_the_viewers` and `a_base_is_set_read_saved_used_by_links_undone_and_redone`; checking a base even when it is the stored one failed `a_stored_base_sent_back_is_accepted_and_the_limit_is_the_viewers`; reporting `links_use_it` as true whenever there is a base failed `a_base_that_links_cannot_use_is_reported_as_not_used` and `a_stored_base_sent_back_is_accepted_and_the_limit_is_the_viewers`; changing `MAX_BASE_URL_BYTES` to 4096 failed `a_stored_base_sent_back_is_accepted_and_the_limit_is_the_viewers` and `a_refused_call_writes_nothing_and_adds_no_undo_step`; letting any holder change the base (an early `Ok(())` for `Edit::SetBaseUrl` in `check_permission`, since taking it off the list does not compile) failed the tool's modify-restriction test; and renaming the undo label failed `a_base_is_set_read_saved_used_by_links_undone_and_redone`. Not mutated: the tool's own call to `check_base_url` before the edit (the engine refuses with the same message, and the tests accept either error kind), the boundary at exactly 8192 bytes of the read cap (`<=` against `<`; only the cap's removal was mutated, and a base of exactly 8192 bytes is written and read in a test), the exact boundary of the 128-reference cap (100 and 200 are tested, not 128 or 129), the choice of `[` as a first character of a host (no test writes an IPv6 base), the case-insensitive scheme (`HTTP://` is written in one test, but the comparison was not made case-sensitive), the tool's mapping of an empty string to none, the early return of `set_base_url` taken alone against `asking_for_the_stored_base_url_changes_no_byte` (that test cannot fail from it, because a rewrite of the same value saves the same bytes; other tests did fail), the `Document::base_url` default when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list), the edit's scope for the history (the default scope is used, as for `SetPrintPresets`), and the `pub(crate)` change of `Held` and `held`. Not tested: that the `/URI` dictionary is not the catalog or the page-tree root itself (a `/URI` that refers to either is read and changed there, and nothing refuses it), and a base that `resolve_uri` accepts but a browser would not. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 295 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 652 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of the four with exit status 0). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app's Document Properties window does not use the new engine functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft opening a link against a base the tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-20 | minor | Print dialog presets, `doc_print_presets`: What: `doc_print_presets` (new, headless) reads and sets the five Print Dialog Presets of Document Properties ▸ Advanced, which ISO 32000-2 Table 147 keeps in the catalog's `/ViewerPreferences`: `scaling` (`/PrintScaling`: `default` or `none`), `duplex` (`/Duplex`: `simplex`, `flip_short_edge`, `flip_long_edge`, or `default` for no entry), `pick_tray_by_size` (`/PickTrayByPDFSize`: true, false, or `default` for no entry; with no entry the viewer decides, so an explicit false is not the same as none and is kept as false), `copies` (`/NumCopies`: 2 to 5, the values Table 147 lists, or `default` for no entry; a stored 1 is not one of them, so it is read as unreadable and kept, and a request for 1 is refused) and `page_ranges` (`/PrintPageRange`: up to 256 `[first, last]` pairs, 1-based, each inside the document, `[]` for no entry). Only the given settings change, and `default` removes the entry (an empty `/ViewerPreferences` goes with the last one); a call that asks for what is stored is not an edit and adds no undo step, and one change is one undo step (`Edit::SetPrintPresets`, labelled Change print dialog presets, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. The result is the presets after the call and `unreadable`: the settings whose entry is there but is not a value accepted here (a name or type Table 147 does not allow, `/NumCopies` outside 2 to 5, a range array that is odd, over 256 pairs, holds a non-integer, a page below 1 or a first page after its last, the last two being this tool's reading rather than the specification's), and `viewer_preferences` when the catalog's entry is neither a dictionary nor null. Such an entry is read as no entry and is left as it is unless a value other than what is read is asked for, because only a setting that differs from what is read is written. Every argument is checked before anything is written, so a call refused for a wrongly typed or unknown value, copies outside 2 to 5, more than 256 ranges, a range that does not start at page 1 or later, ends before it starts or goes past the last page (a stored range past the last page that is sent back unchanged is accepted; a different one is checked), or a `/ViewerPreferences` that is not a dictionary (it is not replaced) writes nothing, including the valid arguments beside the bad one. A null or dangling `/ViewerPreferences` counts as absent and a change starts a new dictionary. An indirect `/ViewerPreferences` and indirect values are read; a change is made in the referenced dictionary where it is, or in the catalog's own, and keeps the preferences that are not print ones (`FitWindow` and the rest). `/PrintArea` and `/PrintClip` are not read or set. It stores the presets only: the Document Properties window has no fields for them, and neither the app's Print dialog nor `doc_print` starts from them yet. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::print_presets` (`read_print_presets`, `set_print_presets`), run by the new `Edit::SetPrintPresets`, and `Document::print_presets`. Parity `organize.print-presets` goes from planned to partial. Why: `organize.print-presets` was a planned P2 feature with no engine API and no tool, so an agent could not read or fix what a file asks its Print dialog to start with (duplex, scaling, copies, page range, paper source), and the existing Optimize step that discards print settings had no counterpart that sets them. It is the smallest open parity entry that was unblocked and could be tested headlessly: no hardware, licence, server, owner decision or renderer work, one catalog dictionary, and the engine already had the edit, undo and permission machinery that `doc_initial_view` uses for the neighbouring preferences. Cost: Reading borrows the catalog and the preferences dictionary and copies nothing from the file: an array is measured before any item of it is looked at, so a direct `/PrintPageRange` of a million entries is read as unreadable from its length, and only an array of at most 512 items is walked. The tool reads the presets once when nothing changes and twice when it changes something. A change that touches the ranges walks the page tree once to count the pages, and every change goes through `Document::update_dict`, which copies the catalog (or the referenced dictionary) once, as every catalog edit does. A tool call that carries more than 256 ranges is refused before any range is converted. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/print_presets.rs`, about 324 lines), one new edit and one new tool, and about 489 lines of tests. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `print_presets_round_trip_through_a_save_and_a_default_removes_the_entry` (all five set on a 3-page file, saved, reopened and read back, with the exact objects the file holds; then each default removes its entry and the empty `/ViewerPreferences` goes), `print_presets_beside_other_preferences_and_through_indirect_values` (an indirect dictionary with indirect `/NumCopies` and a page number: read, changed where it is with the catalog still holding the reference, and `FitWindow` kept), `print_presets_that_cannot_be_read_are_named_kept_and_replaced_only_by_a_real_value` (five unusable entries are named in order, asking for the defaults changes no byte, setting one setting replaces that one only, a stored `/NumCopies` of 1 is named and kept, and the five malformed range arrays are named while a 256-pair one is read), `a_print_preset_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` (copies 0, 1 and 6, 257 ranges, `0-2`, `3-2` and `2-4` on 3 pages, each with the exact message and the saved bytes equal; a `/ViewerPreferences` of 7 is refused with `the document's /ViewerPreferences is not a dictionary, so it was left as it is` and left), `a_stored_page_range_past_the_last_page_does_not_block_another_change` (a stored `2-40` stays through a duplex change and a changed `2-41` is refused), `an_explicit_false_and_no_entry_are_different_and_each_is_kept` (a stored false is read as false, kept when the read value is sent back, removed by no entry, and written as false when false is asked for), `a_null_or_dangling_viewer_preferences_is_absent_and_another_object_is_named` (a null and a reference to nothing are read with nothing unreadable and a change starts a dictionary; a number, a name and an array are named `viewer_preferences`, refused and left) and `a_huge_direct_range_array_is_unreadable_from_its_length_and_kept` (a direct array of a million page numbers is unreadable and still there, whole, after a duplex change); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/print_presets.rs`, through `doc_print_presets`: `presets_are_set_read_saved_undone_and_redone` (defaults, a full set, a second call that keeps the rest, a call that asks for what is there adding no undo step, the saved file's own objects, two undos with the label Change print dialog presets, a third undo refused, redo, and every `default` removing the dictionary), `a_refused_call_changes_nothing_not_even_the_valid_arguments_beside_it` (twenty bad calls, each with a valid `duplex` of `simplex` beside it except the `duplex` `both` call, whose bad value is the duplex itself: the exact message, the presets still the defaults, and the saved bytes before and after equal, with no undo step), `entries_that_are_not_allowed_values_are_named_and_kept_through_a_change_to_another` (a hand-written file with `/PrintScaling /Shrink`, `/NumCopies 9`, `/PrintPageRange [2 1]` and `/Duplex 4`: named in `unreadable`, kept through `pick_tray_by_size` and saved, and `copies` 2 replaces only that one), `a_viewer_preferences_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_refused_and_left_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (the same refusal through the tool with `unreadable` naming `viewer_preferences`, and a copy protected against changes refuses and still reads) and `an_explicit_false_is_kept_and_a_stored_range_past_the_last_page_is_accepted_when_sent_back_unchanged` (false saved as false and `default` removing it; a stored `2-40` sent back is accepted and `2-41` refused, with the page count in the message). None of them could fail before the change, because the tool and the module did not exist. Mutations, restoring after each, on the final code: writing every setting instead of only those that differ (the five `!=` guards in `set_print_presets` made always true) failed `print_presets_that_cannot_be_read_are_named_kept_and_replaced_only_by_a_real_value`, `a_huge_direct_range_array_is_unreadable_from_its_length_and_kept` and the tool's `entries_that_are_not_allowed_values_are_named_and_kept_through_a_change_to_another`; removing the past-the-last-page check failed `a_print_preset_that_is_refused_writes_nothing`, `a_stored_page_range_past_the_last_page_does_not_block_another_change` and `a_refused_call_changes_nothing_not_even_the_valid_arguments_beside_it`; removing the 256-range cap of `set_print_presets` failed `a_print_preset_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` only (the tool has its own cap); making the tool always apply, even when nothing differs, failed the tool tests; dropping the tool's lower bound of 2 copies failed `a_refused_call_changes_nothing_not_even_the_valid_arguments_beside_it`; replacing a `/ViewerPreferences` that is not a dictionary instead of refusing it failed `a_print_preset_that_is_refused_writes_nothing` and the tool's `a_viewer_preferences_that_is_not_a_dictionary_...`; checking a stored range against the page count even when it is unchanged failed `a_stored_page_range_past_the_last_page_does_not_block_another_change`; letting any holder change the presets (an `Edit::SetPrintPresets(_) => Ok(())` arm in `check_permission`, since taking it off the list does not compile) failed the tool's modify-restriction test; reading a range whose first page is after its last as usable failed the organize `..._named_kept_...` test and the tool's `entries_that_are_not_allowed_values_...`; keeping an empty `/ViewerPreferences` and writing the default scaling as `/AppDefault` each failed `print_presets_round_trip_through_a_save_and_a_default_removes_the_entry`; and, new in this version, removing the length cap on reading failed `a_huge_direct_range_array_is_unreadable_from_its_length_and_kept` and the unreadable test, reading a stored `/NumCopies` of 1 as usable failed the unreadable test, deleting an explicit false failed `an_explicit_false_and_no_entry_are_different_and_each_is_kept` and the tool's explicit-false test, reading a null `/ViewerPreferences` as another object failed `a_null_or_dangling_viewer_preferences_is_absent_and_another_object_is_named`, not naming `viewer_preferences` failed that test and the tool's non-dictionary test, and treating a reference to a dictionary as absent failed `print_presets_beside_other_preferences_and_through_indirect_values`. Not mutated: that the length of a direct array is checked before it is copied rather than after (a test cannot tell: the cap mutation proves the cap, not the order, and the code never copies the array on a read), the tool's own 256-range cap and its test for a pair that is not two whole numbers (`[1]`, `[1, 2, 3]`, `-2`, `1.5`, a string are all refused by the passing tests, but the checks were not removed one at a time), the tool's choice of PDF name for each of `scaling` `none`, `simplex`, `flip_short_edge` and `flip_long_edge` (each is read back in a test, no name was swapped), the check that a first page is at least 1 apart from the `first > last` check (the two share one message, and `0-2` is the only case that reaches the first), the engine's own early return when nothing differs (the tool's was mutated), the saturating conversions in `set_print_presets` that the caps make unreachable, the edit's scope for the history (the default scope is used, as for `SetInitialView`), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list), and a `/ViewerPreferences` that is a reference to a reference (it is read as not a dictionary). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (organize: 288 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 645 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity` (exit status 0); `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app's Document Properties window and Print dialog do not use the new engine functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), the PDF in a viewer other than PdfCraft (no other viewer was asked to honour a preset), and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-20 | minor | Never crash, folders made by a failed multi-file tool: What: `Written::within` (`crates/automation/src/paths.rs`), the helper that `doc_export_images`, `doc_export_all_images`, `doc_create_multiple` in separate mode, `doc_split`, `page_extract` with `separate`, `action_run` and `ocr_recognize` with `output_mode` `editable` use to take back a failed call, removed only the deepest folder when the call had made several (`out_dir` of `a/b/c` with nothing there made `a`, `b` and `c`, and a failure left `a` and `b`), a limit that #141 and #142 recorded as known and not changed. It now lists, before it makes the folder, `dir` and each parent that does not exist (`dir.ancestors()` up to the first one that exists, checked with `symlink_metadata`, so a dangling link counts as existing and `create_dir_all` refuses it as before; the walk stops at the empty path that starts a relative `dir`, and a path ending in `..` or `.` is not listed, its parent is). On failure it removes the files it wrote and then those folders, deepest first, with `remove_dir`, which refuses a folder that is not empty: a parent that something else put a file in stays and is named in `; could not remove: <paths>` like any other folder that cannot be removed, and a listed folder that is not there is not reported. The same removal runs when `create_dir_all` itself fails part way (for `keep/one/<300 characters>/out` it made `one` and then failed, which was left behind). A folder that already existed is never listed, so it and what it holds stay. The tool descriptions of the five tools that said `only the deepest is removed` (`crates/automation/src/tools.rs`) now say the same thing in the same words (the folders the call made, the folder and any parent that was missing, deepest first, with the clause for a folder that something else put a file in), and so does the comment of `publish_editable` (`crates/automation/src/a11y/ocr.rs`). Why: A failed call now leaves the folder tree as it found it, not a stray chain of empty folders an agent has to find and clean up, which is what the helper was written to promise; the narrow limit was named in three earlier changes. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/partial_output.rs`: `a_folder_that_needed_several_new_levels_is_removed_whole` (Unix only, like the existing name-length tests: a document with a 244-character name of 10 pages and an existing `keep` folder holding `mine.txt`; `doc_export_images` with `folder` `keep/one/two/out` writes pages 1 to 9 and is refused at page 10 with a message ending `_page_10.png: File name too long (os error 36)`; afterwards `keep` holds exactly `mine.txt`, whose content is unchanged) and `a_folder_that_cannot_be_made_whole_removes_the_levels_it_made` (Unix only: `keep/one/<300 z>/out` is refused with a message ending `/keep/one/<300 z>/out: File name too long (os error 36)` and nothing after it, and `keep` is empty again); and in `crates/automation/src/paths.rs`, `a_failed_call_in_a_new_relative_folder_leaves_nothing_and_adds_nothing_to_the_error` (no root, so relative to the working folder: a body that fails with `boom` in `<top>/a/b`, `<top>/a/..` and `<top>/a/../b`: the error is exactly `boom`, with no `could not remove` after it, and `<top>` is gone). Before the change the first test failed with `one` left behind. Mutations, restored: limiting the list of made folders to the first one (`.take(1)`, the old behaviour) failed the first test with left `["mine.txt", "one"]` and right `["mine.txt"]`; returning the `create_dir_all` error without taking back failed the second test; making every path count as a made folder (no `file_name` check) failed the third with `boom; could not remove: <top>/a/..`. Not mutated: the `; could not remove` path for a parent that is not empty (no test makes a parent non-empty while the call runs), the order of removal (deepest first is what lets each `remove_dir` see an empty folder, which the passing tests depend on, but it was not reversed), the dangling-link rule for a parent (not tested), the empty-path stop (the empty path is also kept out by the `file_name` check and by the not-reported rule for a folder that is not there, so removing only one of the three does not change a result), and the other five tools (they share the helper; only `doc_export_images` was run through it with several new levels). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (78 library tests passed; 640 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep, the new Unix tests on Windows or macOS (no non-Linux system was run), a parent folder that another process creates between the check and `create_dir_all` (the call would then count it as its own, and remove it if it is still empty; not guarded against, as the tool descriptions say), and `doc_split`, `page_extract`, `doc_create_multiple`, `action_run`, `doc_export_all_images` and `ocr_recognize` through a folder of several new levels (they call the same helper; none was run with one). Estimates unchanged. |
| 2026-11-20 | minor | Never crash, null `field_id` of `form_set_image`: What: `form_set_image` (`crates/automation/src/forms.rs`) read `field_id` with `a.0.get`, which sees a JSON null as a value, while every `Args` helper (`Args::get`) sees it as left out, so `"field_id": null` was refused with `field_id must be an integer between 0 and 281474976710655`, the way `js_run` and `js_event` did before #142 (`crates/automation/src/a11y/form_tools.rs`). It now reads through `a.get`: a null is left out and the legacy name-only form is used, and a value of the wrong type is refused with the same message as before. The nested reads (`o.get`, `row.get`, `pair.get`) read keys of objects inside a list, not tool arguments, and were not changed. Not mutated: the `-1` and `"x"` messages (unchanged text, checked only by the exact comparison), the exact-ID path of `form_set_image` (tested in `crates/automation/tests/it/timesheet_compat.rs`, not changed), and `doc_bates_multi` (not changed). Also run once and not kept: a scratch sweep that called every tool with each property set in turn to null, a string, -1, 1e30, 2^32, a mixed array, an object, a boolean and 1e308 (with `doc`, `path` and `page` filled in): no panic from any tool; and a null-against-omitted comparison for every optional property, which found no difference (it is weak: a tool that refuses the call for a missing required argument before it reads the optional one hides a difference). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (77 library tests passed; 638 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`.
