| 2026-11-20 | minor | Never crash, `page_extract` with `delete`: What: `page_extract` (headless) with `delete: true` wrote or opened the copy first and removed the pages afterwards. A removal that was then refused returned an error with the copy already left behind: with every page of the document listed the call failed with `a document must keep at least one page` after `out` had been written, after the copy had been opened as a new document (`open`, which is on when `out` is not given), or after `separate: true` had written the page files into `out_dir` (and made that folder). Now, with `delete: true`, the removal is tried first on a copy of the document (new `Session::check_delete_pages` in the engine: the permission check and the edit itself run on a copy, so nothing changes, and no undo step or unsaved change is made), and a refusal is returned before anything is written or opened, with the same message as before. A call that works is unchanged: the copy is delivered, then the pages are removed as one undoable step (`Delete page`), as before; a call without `delete` does not try the removal. Changes in behaviour: the check also refuses, before anything is written, permissions that refuse page changes (the read-only case was already refused before anything was written) and removing every page. With `delete: true` and a document whose permissions refuse both extracting pages and page changes, the error is now the page-changes one (before, it was the extracting-pages one, which came first). A wrongly typed `delete` (for example the string `yes`) is now refused with `delete must be true or false` before anything is written or opened (before, it was read after the copy was delivered, so the copy was left behind). Limits: the check covers the refusals it lists (a read-only document, permissions that refuse page changes, a page that does not exist, removing every page) and cannot see a signed file with no revisions to append to (`SignedRewrite`), a write error while an XFA form's data is synced or laid out again, or a failure of the refresh after the edit (which rolls the edit back): such a removal behaves as before, and so does a copy that cannot be written (a refused write leaves the pages in place, because they are removed afterwards). Known, not changed: `deliver` in `crates/automation/src/assemble.rs` writes `out` before it reads `open`, so a mistyped `open`, or a failure to open the new document, leaves the file written with an error (it affects `page_extract`, `doc_combine` and the comments summary), and `doc_split` creates `out_dir` and leaves the parts written earlier if a later write fails. Changed sites: `page_extract` in `crates/automation/src/assemble.rs`; the new `Session::check_delete_pages` in `crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`; the `page_extract` tool text in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`. Undo-step count: unchanged (a refused call adds none, as before, because the removal never ran; a call that works adds one, as before). Why: A failed call must leave things as the caller had them (AGENTS.md section 4): an agent told that the extract failed assumes nothing was written, and here a complete copy of the document was on disk or open in the session, which a later run could pick up as the result. Cost: with `delete: true` one extra copy of the document's object table is made and one edit tried on it; without `delete` nothing is added; no limit is added. Tested: `extracting_every_page_with_delete_writes_nothing`, `extracting_every_page_with_delete_opens_no_copy`, `extracting_every_page_separately_with_delete_makes_no_folder` and `extracting_some_pages_with_delete_still_does_both` in `crates/automation/tests/it/extract_delete_refused.rs`. All use a two-page file with no content, `in.pdf`. The first calls `page_extract` with pages `[1, 2]`, `out` `all.pdf` and `delete: true` and requires the exact error `a document must keep at least one page`, no `all.pdf`, one document in `doc_list`, and `doc_info`'s `document` object (dirty flag, undo and redo labels) equal to its state before; it then repeats with pages `[2, 1, 2]` (every page, one given twice) and requires the same message and no file. The second passes `open: true` and no `out` and requires the same message and one document in `doc_list`. The third passes `separate: true` and `out_dir` `parts` and requires the same message and no `parts` folder. The fourth extracts page `[2]` to `one.pdf` with `delete: true` and requires the file, `original.pages` 1, one document in `doc_list` and the undo label `Delete page`. A fifth, `a_mistyped_delete_is_refused_before_anything_is_written`, passes `delete: "yes"` with `out` `one.pdf` and requires the exact error `delete must be true or false`, no `one.pdf` and one document in `doc_list`. Not asserted: the document state after the second and third, the redo list, and the bytes of the copy. Before the change the first three and the fifth failed and the fourth passed. Mutations, each restored: commenting out the call to `check_delete_pages` in `page_extract` failed the first test at its `all.pdf` assertion (the error message assertion before it passed; the `[2, 1, 2]` repeat was not reached), the second at the document count (2, expected 1) and the third at its `parts` assertion; the fourth passed. Reading `delete` after the copy is delivered again (the earlier order) failed the fifth at its no-`one.pdf` assertion (the error-message assertion before it passed, since the error came after the write). Not mutated: `check_delete_pages` itself (its permission check and its edit on a copy separately), and the tool text. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (automation: 74 library tests passed; 619 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed; engine: 888 library tests passed and 2 ignored; 9 and 2 in two other targets passed; 60 integration tests passed and 2 ignored; 0 doctests in either crate); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep, a `delete` refused for a read-only document or for permissions that refuse page changes (the same check refuses them; only the every-page refusal is exercised), a failure of the deletion that the check cannot see, and a copy written when a later step fails. | 2026-11-20 | minor | Never crash, `doc_save` with `flatten_fill_sign`: What: `doc_save` (headless) with `flatten_fill_sign: true` applied `Edit::FlattenFillSign` as its own undoable edit and only then made and wrote the bytes. If the save was refused (the bytes could not be made or checked, or the atomic write failed, for example because the target is a folder) the call returned an error while the document stayed flattened, with unsaved changes and one extra undo step. Now, when the flatten added an edit and the save then fails, that edit is taken back and forgotten (new `Session::discard_last_edit` in the engine: `undo`, then the entry it put on the redo list is dropped), so a refused save leaves the document and its unsaved-changes flag as they were, and its undo steps too (see Limits). A save that works behaves as before, with the same one extra undo step, `Flatten Fill & Sign`, and the Will Save script still runs after the flatten and before the bytes are made; a document with no Fill & Sign marks adds no step either way. Limits: the undo steps are as they were unless the undo stack was full (100 steps): the flatten then dropped the oldest step, and the take-back cannot restore it. Redo steps that the flatten cleared are not brought back. If a Will Save script changed the document after the flatten, the flatten is not taken back (the last step is then the script's, not the flatten's): the document is left as it is, the flatten stays as an undo step under the script's, and the error says so. A failure after the file has been written (`mark_saved` refusing the written bytes) is not covered separately: the take-back still runs, so the file on disk keeps the flatten while the document does not have it. Known, not changed: the desktop app's save-time flatten (`crates/ui-egui/src/editing/save.rs`, native path around lines 280-302 and wasm path around lines 128-137) has the same shape, applying the flatten before the write, and is untouched. Changed sites: `doc_save_with` and a new `save_prepared` (the Will Save actions, making the bytes and `publish_save`, moved out unchanged in order) in `crates/automation/src/documents.rs`; `Session::discard_last_edit` in `crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`; the `doc_save` tool text in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`. Undo-step count: a refused save with the flag adds none (before: one); a save that works adds one, as before. Why: A failed call must leave the document as the caller had it (AGENTS.md section 4): an agent told that a save failed assumes the document is as it left it, and here it was flattened and dirty, so a later save with the flag off wrote the flattened pages. Cost: One edit-generation read before the flatten, and one generation and undo-label read after it; an undo only on the failure path; nothing is copied and no limit is added. The third uses a form with a text field `log` and a Will Save action that sets it to `saving`: the refused call must fail with that message followed by `; the Fill & Sign flatten was kept, because the document changed after it (a Will Save script): undo it to take it back`, the comment list is empty, the flag is true, the undo label is `Run JavaScript` and `log` is `saving`; one `edit_undo` then leaves `Flatten Fill & Sign` as the label, and a second reports `Flatten Fill & Sign` and brings `Hello` back. Now each is refused with `InvalidArgs` naming the argument before anything changes: `form_set_actions` reads the lists with `Args::strs` (message `reset must be an array of strings`), `doc_optimize` copies the style of its `mono` setting (`color ppi must be a number, not "x"`, an object check, and a check for unknown keys, so a misspelled key is no longer ignored; `quality` must be a whole number, still clamped to 1 to 100; a null key inside `color` or `gray` is absent, while a null `mono` key is still a wrong type). | 2026-11-20 | minor | Gap 15: `form_submit_actions` (headless, read-only) takes a new count per action, `unmatched`: how many entries of an only or except field list match no field of the form. A name matches a field's fully qualified name, or a dotted ancestor of one (a parent whose children it stands for); names are compared exactly, letter for letter, each entry counts (repeats too), a partial name that itself holds a dot is read as two, and `unmatched` is 0 for all and too_many. The fields of the form are those the form reader returns (terminal text, button, choice and signature fields from the form's `/Fields`, and widgets only the pages list); a field with no or an unknown `/FT`, one deeper than 64 levels, or a parent whose only kids are such fields is not read, so a name for it counts as unmatched and the count can be too high. Nothing is written and nothing is sent. A reference in the list to a field under a parent whose `/T` is empty is now named without the empty part, as the form reader names that field (`x`, not `.x`; `qualified_name_within` joins the parts as the field walk does), which also changes the names of other callers that name a field by reference (Reset a Form, Show/Hide, and the like) in that one case. It replaces the old limitation that names were never checked against the form. Engine: `SubmitAction::unmatched` (`pdfcraft_forms`). Why: Gap 15: a Submit a Form action that names a field that does not exist (a typo, a renamed or deleted field) silently sends less or more than intended; an agent had to list every field and compare by hand. The check sorts the form's field names once per call, only when an action lists names, as slices of the names already read (nothing is copied), and looks each listed name up with a binary search plus one check of the next name for a dotted ancestor, so it takes O((F + N) log F) comparisons for F fields and N listed names; a list is already capped at 65,536 entries and 4 MiB. A first version that hashed every dotted prefix of every field name took 68 seconds on one field whose name was 300,000 dots. | 2026-11-20 | minor | Gap 15: `doc_javascript_replace` (new, headless) replaces the plain text `find` with `replace` in every JavaScript `doc_javascripts` lists (document scripts, open action, document actions, field, link, annotation, page and bookmark actions), as one undoable edit named Replace in JavaScripts that keeps every other key and action of each script; nothing is run. `find` is at most 1,024 bytes and `replace` at most 65,536 bytes and may be empty; case is matched unless `match_case` is false, which folds only the ASCII letters; matches are found left to right without overlapping. `dry_run` returns the same report and writes nothing. The result has `searched` (listed scripts read whole and exactly), `occurrences`, `changed` (each script that would read differently, once, with its `id`, `kind`, `trigger` and `occurrences`), `applied` and `skipped_unreadable`. A script is not searched, and is left as it is, when its code cannot be read whole and exactly: no readable text, longer than 1 MiB or past the listing's 16 MiB of code, a stream `/JS` that is not plain UTF-8 (PDFDocEncoding, UTF-16, or a byte-order mark) or a string that does not decode cleanly, since writing it back would damage it (new `exact` on the listing's entries and in `doc_javascripts`). A changed script is written as a string in its `/JS`: a stream `/JS` is not kept as a stream, and the old stream is left in the file, not referenced by that action. Scripts are written where they physically are: an action that several places refer to changes for all of them, two ids that lead to one shared dictionary are one script (listed once, under the first), and each object that holds changed actions (a catalog with thousands of direct named scripts, a page with direct annotations) is copied and rewritten once, not once per script. A replace that finds nothing, or whose result reads as the script already does (case ignored, a find equal to its replacement but for case), changes nothing and adds no undo step or unsaved mark; that holds for the engine edit alone, inside a Batch it stays part of the batch. It is refused, with nothing changed, for an empty `find`, a `find` equal to `replace` with case matched, a script that would be longer than 1 MiB after the replace or rewritten scripts that together would hold more than 16 MiB (both worked out with checked arithmetic before any script is built), and a script listing that hit a limit (over 5,000 scripts, the page annotation visit limit, or a chain, name tree, field tree, outline or array past its limit, which the listing now also reports as `capped`), since a replace could not reach every script. Every object that holds a changed action is built before the first is written, so a script that cannot be written back refuses the whole edit with the document as it was. A document that does not allow changes refuses the edit. | 2026-11-20 | minor | Gap 22: `af_list` (read-only, headless) takes two new counts of the entries it listed: `without_relationship`, those whose `/AFRelationship` is missing or not a name (ISO 32000-2 requires one on a file specification an `/AF` array lists; a reader treats the entry as Unspecified), and `not_embedded`, those with no embedded file stream (a link to a file outside the document, which a PDF/A-3 file may not rely on). | 2026-11-20 | minor | Gap 15: `form_submit_actions` (read-only, headless) reads what a field's Submit a Form actions ask for, and sends nothing: for each trigger (`mouse_up`, `mouse_down`, `mouse_enter`, `mouse_exit`, `on_focus`, `on_blur`) whose action is a Submit a Form action it returns the `url` (`/F`, a string or a file specification's `/F`; empty when `/F` is absent, a name, a number or a dictionary with no `/F` string; an address over 64 KiB is not copied and `url_too_long` is set), the `format` (`fdf`, or `html`, `xfdf`, `pdf` from `/Flags` bits 3, 6 and 9; when several are set the specification's own rules give PDF, then XFDF, then HTML: SubmitPDF makes all other flags ignored except GetMethod, and ExportFormat is meaningful only when SubmitPDF and XFDF are clear), `fields` (`all` when there is no `/Fields`; `only` or `except` with the names, by the Include/Exclude bit; `too_many` past 65,536 entries or 4 MiB of names, not listed) and the other choices of `/Flags` (`get_method`, `include_no_value_fields`, `submit_coordinates`, `include_append_saves`, `include_annotations`, `canonical_format`, `excl_non_user_annots`, `excl_f_key`, `embed_form`) with the raw `flags` (low 32 bits). A field named by reference is listed by its fully qualified name (the partial names up its `/Parent` chain, joined by dots); entries that name nothing (a reference to nothing, a dictionary with no `/T`, a number, `null`) are left out and counted in `fields_left_out`, so an empty list can come from a non-empty `/Fields`. | 2026-11-19 | minor | never crash, document parts: `dpart_list` lists the `/DPM` entries of all nodes together from one allowance of 4 MiB (`MAX_DPM_BYTES`), each entry costing its key's length, its raw length when its value is a string or a name, and 32 (`DPM_ENTRY_COST`), charged before it is listed and again for every node that lists it. Once one entry does not fit, the allowance is spent: from then on no node's `/DPM` is read at all, and every node with one is flagged in `metadata_truncated` (a direct `/DPM` only when it has entries; an indirect one is flagged without being resolved, so even an empty one is; also set past 32 entries, as before). Before, a `/DPM` shared by up to 5000 nodes was parsed again for every node (a string over 32 MiB is not kept by the document's cache, about 70 ms per node), every direct value in it was cloned per node (strings, arrays, dictionaries) and scanned in full, a long name or invalid-UTF-8 key was copied in full, and the 5000 x 32 x 400-character output was bounded by nothing the file held. Keys and names are now cut after the 800 bytes that 200 characters can take, and a direct `/DParts` array, group or `/DPM` is borrowed instead of cloned. Listed text is unchanged. What is charged: key length, string or name length, 32 per entry; numbers, booleans and containers cost their key and 32. Outside the allowance: while it lasts, each node resolves its `/DPM` and up to 32 values in it, and an indirect value is resolved before its length is known and charged, so the one entry that does not fit is read in full once (a 33 MiB string costs one parse, then the allowance is spent); each such node prepares up to 32 entries of text (keys and values cut to 200 characters) that are dropped with the node, and nothing is kept between nodes (no memo); the node's `/DParts` references are still read up to 5000 per node, and a `/DParts` array shared by 5000 nodes costs 25,000,000 reference reads and, if it is large enough to be dropped from the document's cache, a parse per node; the catalog's `/DPartRoot` and `/RecordLevel` are read once; no other reader of `/DPM` exists (grep of the workspace), and `dpart_list` flags per node. | 2026-11-19 | minor | never crash, fonts listing: `doc_info`'s fonts (the Fonts tab) no longer collect every page's font resources afresh. A page whose font dictionaries are the same ones, in the same order, as a page already read is skipped; the dictionaries are the ones the readers of a page's fonts take: the page's own `/Resources` written in it as a dictionary, and an indirect `/Resources` dictionary of the page or an ancestor (not an ancestor's direct one, not a stream), each with its `/Font` dictionary, indirect or direct. Many pages inheriting one shared font dictionary cost it once instead of its entries per page. The other pages are read within one allowance of 1 GiB of font resource entries, an entry costing its name's length plus 64 bytes and a shared font dictionary measured once, charged before the page's fonts are collected; a page that does not fit is skipped, and `doc_info`'s `warnings` then says `Some fonts may be missing from the list` (a refresh of the list after a content edit, `refresh_fonts`, replaces the note with the new reading's; for an XFA document the note is not kept among the notes carried across a full re-read). Each font dictionary is also turned into a listing entry once however many pages and resource names reach it, and a font's `/Subtype`, `/Encoding` name and descendant `/Ordering`, which can be indirect and so shared by many fonts, are read only when at most 256 bytes (a longer one is read as absent, and `warnings` says so with the same `Some fonts may be missing from the list` note, so its type, encoding or substitute may be wrong). The `/BaseFont` is a direct name in its own font dictionary and is copied once per distinct font. The fonts listed for an ordinary document are unchanged and a page's own font still hides an inherited one of the same resource name. What is charged: font resource entries (name length plus 64) per page read. Not charged from the allowance but capped: the three indirect-capable fields above (256 bytes each, checked before copying); the direct `/BaseFont` and descriptor reads are once per distinct font dictionary and bounded by the file. Not covered: a page that has a font dictionary of its own still pays, within the allowance, for the shared ones it inherits; the Fonts tab still says `No fonts are referenced` when every page was skipped (the warning is in `doc_info` and the document's warnings, not a separate field); the page-tree walk and per-page resource copies elsewhere are unchanged. So one array shared by every page (for example 1,000,000 entries over 100,000 pages) costs at most four entries per box instead of its length per page and box. Applies to `page_boxes`, `page_set_box` and the Set Page Boxes dialog (`rect_of` in organize), the printer-marks box reader (`pdfcraft_organize_boxes`, edit) and the preflight page-size check (`page_box_out_of_range`, PDF/A 6.1.13). Behaviour: an array of exactly four numbers reads as before; an array of more than four entries whose first four are numbers, which the old readers refused (organize reported it `ignored`, edit defaulted it, preflight skipped its size check), now reads as its first four, and preflight now flags such a box that is too small or large; an array whose first four are not all numbers, but which holds four numbers among more entries, used to read and is now no box, as in the viewer. Not covered: the page-tree walk still clones each page's dictionary and copies a /Pages node's inherited values into each page, so a huge direct inherited /MediaBox, /CropBox or /Resources over many pages is still copied per page; other array readers (annotation /Rect in annot, redaction verify extract) are unchanged. Tested: `a_box_is_its_first_four_entries_all_numbers_as_the_viewer_reads_it` (organize), `printer_marks_read_a_long_box_array_from_its_first_four_entries` (edit) and the new lines of `implementation_limits_are_reported` (preflight). | 2026-11-19 | minor | Gap 18: the Print readiness dialog has three fields, Total ink limit (%), Least bleed (mm) and Least resolution (ppi), for the `ink_limit`, `min_bleed_mm` and `min_ppi` of `print_readiness_with_options`. An empty field is the default (the engine's own, so the report and its wording are exactly what they were). A typed value is applied with Enter or when the field loses the keyboard, not on every keystroke, because applying measures the document again on the UI thread (the PDF/A check and every page and image); the typed text stays in the field and the line under it says `Press Enter to apply` while it waits. Each field has one line under it that is always there, so the dialog does not move: the allowed range and default, in red the reason when the text was refused (a value that is not a number, NaN, infinite or outside the engine's range: ink 100 to 400, bleed 0 to 50 mm, ppi 36 to 1200, from its own `TAC_LIMIT_RANGE`, `MIN_BLEED_RANGE` and `MIN_PPI_RANGE`; a refusal changes nothing and says to use digits 0-9). A comma is accepted as the decimal mark, full-width digits are read as digits, and -0 is 0. Reset to defaults (disabled at the defaults) clears all three. The findings, the Measured lines and Copy report (which gets a line naming each changed threshold) all come from the one measuring that follows the options; the engine's default constants (`TAC_LIMIT_PERCENT`, `MIN_BLEED_MM`, `WARN_PPI`) are now public and give the field hints. The report's scroll area is capped by the window height, so Close and Copy report stay in reach on a short window. The values are remembered in the settings JSON (`print_readiness` with `ink_limit`, `min_bleed_mm` and `min_ppi`; a default one is not written, each key is read alone, and a missing, mistyped or out-of-range saved value keeps the current choice). Headless: `ui.set readiness-ink-limit`, `readiness-min-bleed-mm` and `readiness-min-ppi` (a number or `default`; anything else is refused with `<key> must be a number from X to Y, or default` and changes nothing; an open report is measured again once; setting the value a field already has still puts its text right) and `ui.state.print_readiness` (each a number, or null for the default); the control docs say so. If the engine ever refused a threshold the error is shown, not replaced by a default report. The new labels and messages use `tl!` and are English only: translations are rows in craft-i18n and show once `craft-i18n.rev` moves, and a missing row falls back to English. The tools are unchanged (`print_readiness` already took all three). Tested: `print_readiness::every_threshold_changes_the_findings_the_measured_lines_and_copy_report`, `the_dialog_measures_with_the_default_thresholds_until_one_is_set`, `refused_thresholds_change_nothing_and_say_what_is_allowed`, `a_typed_field_applies_when_valid_and_is_refused_visibly_when_not`, `typing_into_the_dialog_fields_applies_on_enter_or_leaving_and_a_bad_value_is_refused_on_screen`, `on_a_short_window_the_report_scrolls_and_close_stays_in_reach`, `typed_numbers_accept_a_comma_and_full_width_digits_and_read_minus_zero_as_zero`, `control_refusals_name_the_key_and_an_unchanged_set_clears_a_refused_text`, `the_thresholds_are_remembered_each_alone_and_bad_saved_values_keep_the_current_choice` and `control::print_readiness_thresholds_are_set_and_reported_through_the_control_channel`; the report-follows-options, typed-field, Enter/leave typing, short-window, number-forms, control-refusal and remembered tests were shown failing with the report measured without the options, a keystroke applying, the short-window cap, the number normalising, the key error and the restore removed. The existing ink-findings and fonts tests now wait for Close to stop moving (a `settle` helper) instead of a fixed frame count. Known limits: no Preferences page control; the dialog drawn by a GPU was not run (the dialog tests use the kittest harness without a GPU); a threshold change still measures the whole document again (the ink limit is applied inside the engine's measuring, so only the apply moment was changed, not a separate judging step). | 2026-11-19 | minor | Gap 21: `doc_compare` takes `ignore_punctuation` (boolean, default false): words that differ only in sentence punctuation (. , ; : ! ? quotes and apostrophes, brackets, hyphen, underscore, Unicode dashes, quotes, bullets and ellipsis, guillemets, inverted marks, pilcrow, middle dot, CJK stops) are not counted as text changes and keep their own text. Kept, because they change a figure or name: symbols (`$ + = < > ^ | ~`), `# % & @ * / \`, the section sign, per mille and basis point signs, primes, the fraction slash, a `.` or `,` between two digits (10.5 is not 105, 1.000 is not 1,000) and a dash before a digit unless a letter precedes it (-5 is not 5). Words made only of such marks equal one another (a hyphen and an em dash), never a missing word or a word with another character; the section sign stays, so `§5` and `5` differ. It combines with `ignore_case`, affects the text comparison only, and the report, Mark as comments and the dialog do not take it. Headless only; English-only tool text. Tested: `compare::tests::ignoring_punctuation_treats_words_that_differ_only_in_marks_as_equal` and `compare_ignore_punctuation::ignore_punctuation_hides_mark_only_changes_and_keeps_real_ones`.
